Jump to: Quick answers · Vampires · Werewolves and hybrids · Magic and demons · The hunt · Across every kind · Reference

A living page. It changes with the mod and is checked against every release. This edition covers 2.40, the fixes and balance release that follows The Hermetic Order of the Blue Rose, Part 2, on Crusader Kings III 1.20, as of 22 October 2026. What changed in each version.

Start hereQuick answers#

The questions that come up most, grouped by topic. Tap a question to open its answer; each one links to the full section.

Vampires

How do I become a vampire?

Get a vampire's blood into you, then finish the change. A vampire or hybrid can use Offer Blood and then Turn Vampire on you, or you can use Ask to be Turned on a vampire you know. Falling in battle or dying a natural death with the blood in you also turns you. Nobody is born a vampire. Every route into vampirism.

Why did my county lose control after I fed?

A body leaves a mark on the county it was found in, and so does a feed somebody talks about. A drained body costs 5 county control; a feed that gets talked about costs 1. Two incidents inside a year open a trail, Something Stalks Here, and at the third the county's lord is told. Trails and what leaves them.

Werewolves

How do I turn someone into a werewolf, and can it be cured?

A werewolf uses Offer Gift or Force Gift. That gives the Infected Wound, which lasts one to three years and ends in a turning, a reprieve or death. The cure is the Abandon Lycanthropy decision, which needs the Supernatural: Embrace Decisions rule set to On. The rule is off by default. The bite, in full. The werewolf cure.

How do I get one of the six hides?

Be a werewolf or hybrid whose traits suit the hide, then get hold of it. Most start as relics in shrines at holy sites. Learn where one is at a Werewolf Gathering, then hold the county, besiege it, go there on pilgrimage, or use Send for a Hide. A hide that accepts you makes you its Champion. Where the hides are. Who each hide chooses.

Hunters

How does a hunter find me, and what can I do about it?

He follows the trail your kills leave on a county and tracks for three weeks. If his Tracking bar reaches 60 you are warned. At the door you choose an answer: fight, go to ground for two years, look him in the eye, send your progeny, let the wolf out, buy him off, or point him at a worse county. The door, from both sides.

How do I play a hunter?

Take the Hunter trait in the ruler designer, or use Ask to be trained as a Hunter on a hunter you know. You pick monsters or witches first, and training then takes 75 to 115 days. If it fails you become an apprentice and finish on a hunt with your trainer. The work itself is the Supernatural Hunt activity. Hunters. Riding out on a hunt.

Witches, demons and mages

How do I become a witch?

A witch brings you in. The route is the Convert to Witchcraft scheme, which a witch runs on someone who is not one. With the Supernatural: Embrace Decisions rule on, the Embrace Witchcraft decision makes you one at once. Nobody is born a witch: what passes to children is the Source and Yaga's Blood. Becoming a witch.

How do I become a demon?

Take Minor Demon in the ruler designer. Otherwise there are three routes: be born to a demon with Demonic Heritage, which becomes the trait in your twenties; answer a Magic Eruption with I must harness this power at all costs; or finish the Ascendancy special project. Under the Supernatural: Mortals Becoming Demons rule set to Players Only, only players can still take those roads. Becoming a demon.

How do I become a mage?

Mage is the capstone of the Arcane tree in the Magic lifestyle. Arcane is closed to a plain witch: it opens with a magic artifact, the Source blood, a place of power, an academy, or a house that has one. Finish the tree and the Mage trait is yours, unless you already hold Warlock or True Witch: you get one of the three. A hunter cannot take it. The Order of the Blue Rose also teaches its acolytes to be Mages, and with the Supernatural: Embrace Decisions rule on, the Embrace the Mage decision does it at once. The Magic lifestyle and its trees.

How do I join the Order of the Blue Rose?

You are called; you cannot apply. Each year every temple sends a note and a blue rose to a few people in reach: Mages, people with the Source, members' children, learned witches and scholars, and since 2.39 a player Temple Magus can send one himself with Invite into the Order. Answer it, pass two tests, take the oath. To be noticed sooner, remember the name when you hear the rumour, use Seek Out the Order, or, if the Adeptus Major's stranger ever comes for you, answer I would rather be one of you. Who the Order calls.

What are magical artifacts, and how do I get one?

Witchcraft's ten magical artifacts, from the Black Grimoire to the Darkblade, turn up over the years with a ruler or his courtier: one a year while any of nine is missing (the tenth, the Enchanted Ring, comes only from research). The Search for Magical Artifacts tells you who owns one and can give you a claim; then you take it by theft, war or Zecchia. Relics (the 2.39 Beetle and Chains among them) and the book's pages are magic items too. Magical artifacts, in full.

Setup and older saves

What is the load order with A Game of Thrones?

A Game of Thrones, then AGOT submods, then The Long Night & Azor Ahai if you use it, then Supernatural, then the compatibility patch last. Supernatural runs on the base game alone. Do not load it with Witchcraft or V&L. Load order, in full.

Why does House Hellion have hundreds of members in my old save?

Before 2.35 (10 September 2026), every witch a witch hunt invented was born into House Hellion, which is the demons' house. New witches have no dynasty. Nothing moves the old ones out, so a save from before the fix keeps them. House Hellion.

The shape of itWhat the mod is#

Supernatural
DeveloperDlonem
GameCrusader Kings III 1.20
First release2022
Current version2.40, on CK3 1.20
RequiresBase game only
Workshop2856525601
Without SteamManual install
SourceDlonem/CK3SupernaturalMod
Discorddiscord.gg/Efvd2B97xx
LanguagesEnglish and Russian; Spanish in part (detail)
LineageCarries Rycie's Witchcraft forward (credits)

Supernatural puts vampires, werewolves, hybrids, witches, demons and the hunters who go after them into Crusader Kings III as characters rather than as scenery. A vampire is a lord with a court and a title. A werewolf is somebody's son. The hunter who arrives at your gate was trained by a man whose name is in his trait tooltip.

The design rule the mod keeps coming back to is that power is visible. Killing is not free: a body takes something out of the county it was found in, and so does a feed somebody talks about, and what it takes is the thing a hunter can read months later. Compelling people leaves traces too: three botched compulsions in one county inside five years, and the county starts to notice that nobody remembers that week. Killing somebody leaves a body, and the morning after you choose what the body looks like. That chain, from an act to a mark to a lord's letter to a man at your door, is what 2.35 built, and it is the spine of the whole mod now.

Underneath that there are six lifestyles with ten perk trees between them, a trait for each kind of creature, most of them with a power track and rank names of their own, six legendary wolf hides, a secret society of Mages, ten men-at-arms regiments, twenty-seven game rules, and a large body of witchcraft content carried forward from another mod. The pieces are covered in order below; if you only want one part of it, the contents rail on the left will take you there and every heading has a link you can paste into a chat.

Before you playInstall and load order#

On Steam, subscribe and enable it. It runs on the base game and needs no other mod. Which version to use depends on your version of Crusader Kings III.

Without Steam, take the archive from the releases page. If you took the Source code (zip), open the CK3SupernaturalMod-<version>/ folder first and take the two items out of it. You need both supernatural_mod.mod and the supernatural_mod folder, dropped side by side into the CK3 mod folder, so that you end up with mod\supernatural_mod.mod and mod\supernatural_mod\. The full walkthrough with paths for all three operating systems is on the download page.

If the launcher does not show the mod after a manual install, it is almost always one of two things: only the folder was copied, and the launcher reads .mod files rather than folders; or the .mod file is a launcher-generated copy whose path= points at an absolute folder on somebody else's machine. The portable form reads path="mod/supernatural_mod" and has no remote_file_id line. The descriptor.mod inside the mod folder is a different file and is not the one the launcher reads.

Which version for which game#

Versions 2.36 to 2.40 are built for Crusader Kings III 1.20; 2.39 was built against 1.20.1.1, and the 1.20.1.2 patch changed nothing the mod copies. Version 2.35a and earlier were built for 1.19, and 2.35a crashes on 1.20 as a new game loads in.

With A Game of Thrones: as of 17 October 2026 AGOT had not been updated for 1.20 (its last update was build 0.5.2.1, on 4 September), and the compatibility patch follows AGOT's update. Until then, play AGOT on CK3 1.19 with v2.35a, the last version built for 1.19.

Load order with A Game of Thrones#

  1. A Game of Thrones
  2. AGOT submods
  3. AGOT: The Long Night & Azor Ahai (optional)
  4. Supernatural
  5. AGOT: Supernatural & The Long Night Compatibility Patch, always last

The patch is a separate subscription. It exists because Supernatural and The Long Night define the same twenty-two entries: marriages, secrets, disinheritance, the witch ritual, birth events, schemes, abductions and more. The patch merges them so neither mod silently erases the other's work. One of those entries, the marriage-acceptance rule, was behind multiplayer desyncs in testing.

Do not run these alongside it

Witchcraft and V&L are the important two. Supernatural already carries that content, so loading either next to it puts two copies of the same files against each other and whichever loads later wins.

Also known not to get along: AGOT: Battlefield Duels, Better Barbershop, Asia Expansion Project, Cultural Armies Pack.

Compatibility and what it overrides lists which base-game files the mod replaces, which is what decides whether another mod will conflict with it. For multiplayer, see Multiplayer.

The blood

Vampires: how one is made, how it feeds, and what its maker can ask of it.

Vampires

The bloodVampires#

A vampire is immortal, cannot have children, does not age past the day they turned, and is the only creature in the mod with a relationship to the person who made them that the game itself tracks and prices.

VampireKnown VampireTrue VampireVampire lifestyle

The trait gives +3 intrigue, +2 stewardship, +1 learning and +10 prowess before any rank, immunity to non-epidemic disease, and no prowess loss from age. It renames itself by rank as the Vampirism track fills.

Becoming one#

Every route into vampirism runs through one hidden event, which is why they all behave consistently: it refuses anyone who already holds a supernatural or hunter trait, records the age you were when it happened so the visual ageing rules have something to freeze, clears any curable disease you were carrying, and decides whether you end up with a hidden secret or an openly Known Vampire trait.

Ask a vampire for it. The interaction is Ask to be Turned, used on a vampire you know to be one, or, since 2.39, on your spouse, close family, maker or progeny if they are one. Only an adult player uses it; the AI never asks. On acceptance you get their blood first: the Vampire's Blood modifier, worth +0.4 health, +2 max combat roll, 20 per cent faster stress loss and 15 per cent more lifestyle experience, and a flag that the rest of the system reads. The blood wears off after three years, but the change comes before that, at a random point within a year. If you change your mind first, Ask to Not be Turned stops the change. It does not remove the blood.

Be offered it. Two interactions, both used by a vampire or a hybrid. Offer Blood gives the blood to someone who does not have it, and on its own it never turns anyone. Turn Vampire finishes the change at once, and only appears against someone who already carries the blood. An AI vampire uses both if it rules a county or more; an AI hybrid uses neither. The AI weighs a sick recipient as a better candidate than a healthy one, and a friend, child, spouse or soulmate far above a stranger.

Fall in battle with the blood in you. While you carry vampire blood, a knight or commander who would have taken a fatal blow instead comes off the field alive, at roughly two-thirds of one per cent per combat pulse for a prowess-15 knight, scaled by prowess and by how badly the fight is going. Three days later you wake with no pain and a closed wound, and the chain gives you about a month:

BeatWhenWhat happens
You Should Have Died3 days after the fallOne option. You get up. If somebody felled you, the game remembers who.
What the Blood Wants+14 daysThe choice: drain a man on your own side who will not be missed, ask the vampire who gave you the blood if they are standing there, or refuse.
The Last of It+14 daysOnly if you refused. Feed, or refuse a second time and die of it.

Refusing twice kills you, and the death reads woke from death with a vampire's blood in his veins, would not feed, and died again. Asking the donor is the branch where nobody dies, and it needs them alive, vampiric and standing in the same place you are. If there is nobody suitable to drain, the game creates somebody, so the chain works for a landless adventurer with no court.

Die of natural causes with the blood in you. Vampire blood is wired into the game's own second-chance-at-death rule, alongside the base game's Know Thyself perk. You come back as a vampire instead of dying.

Nobody is born a vampire. A vampire cannot have children, and no setting of the Supernatural: Inheritance/Pregnancy rule produces a vampire child. Birth is a werewolf route.

Start as one. The Vampire trait is in the ruler designer, for 150 points.

The blood on its own. Anyone carrying the blood, AI included, can get the event The Blood. Its second part can turn you, and its riskier answer kills six times in ten.

The random embrace events. Six chains, all of them player-only, fired at three per cent per year for each adult player character who is not already a witch, a hunter or a creature when the Supernatural: Events rule allows it. Two are vampire routes, A Night Amongst the Nobles and A Unique Visit. A third, Market Day, can end with you a vampire or a werewolf. They are also the reason a player meets these things at all in a world where the AI mostly does not.

The Embrace Vampirism decision lets you pick it yourself. It needs the Supernatural: Embrace Decisions rule set to On, and that rule is off by default. So does the cure, Abandon Vampirism. The ten decisions.

No children. Since 2.39 nobody under 16 is turned. The five interactions that turn someone (Offer Blood, Turn Vampire, Offer Gift, Force Gift, Make Hybrid) are not shown on a child, a child cannot use them or ask to be turned, the random embrace events need an adult, and the vampire and hybrid initiations refuse a child (a ruler designed as one at game start excepted). A child cannot use the creature decisions either: the satiate decisions, the embrace decisions, the Vade Maecum decisions or the Homunculus Beetle. Born wolves are unchanged.

Ranks and power#

Vampirism is both the rank ladder and the currency. Compel Mortal and Invoke the Bond are paid for out of the same track that names you, so a maker who leans on his progeny can fall back down a tier.

RankVampirismWhat it is
Fledgling Vampireunder 35The starting state for almost everyone.
Vampire35 to 70The Vampires regiment unlocks at 50.
Revered Vampire71 to 99
Elder Vampire100The cap. No compulsion command needs it: the strongest, End your Life, opens at 85. On a hunt, Look them in the eye needs it unless the hunter brought silver.
The numbers: what each rung of the track adds

Track rungs sit at 20, 40, 60, 80 and 100, and they are cumulative, so they do not line up exactly with the rank names above. A character at 70 is called a Vampire and is carrying the rungs up to 60.

RungWhat it adds
20+1 intrigue, +1 diplomacy, +8 prowess, +15% movement speed, +5% vampire lifestyle experience
40+1 intrigue, +2 stewardship, +8 prowess, +10% movement speed, +10% experience
60+2 intrigue, +2 stewardship, +3 learning, +9 prowess, +5% long reign bonus, +5% experience
80+2 intrigue, +2 stewardship, +2 martial, +10 prowess, −5% dread decay, +10% experience
100+4 intrigue, +2 stewardship, +2 martial, +2 learning, +10 prowess, +15% scheme discovery, +1 domain limit, +10% long reign bonus, +5% experience

At a full track, with the base trait, that totals +13 intrigue, +10 stewardship, +6 learning, +4 martial, +1 diplomacy and +55 prowess, plus a quarter again on movement speed and a domain slot.

How the track fills. Every living vampire gets +1 a year and nothing more, which is a hundred years to Elder on the drip alone. The real lever is feeding: a clean feed on the decision pays 15 to 35, a feed that ends in a kill pays the same, the traveller on the road pays 5 to 12 and blood you paid for pays 10 to 20. Surviving a hunter pays the same for every kind: 15 to 30 for beating one on a hunt, 8 to 15 for looking him in the eye, 5 to 12 for going to ground or sending him to another county, and 4 to 10 for running when you are warned. The century events pay perk points rather than track experience.

Feeding: Satiate Thirst and the Feeding activity#

There are two feeding systems. The Satiate Thirst decision is the quick one: a seven-year cooldown for a player, a three-day wait, and a short event chain with three ways to go about it. The Feeding activity is the long one: you travel to a town, you can bring up to five other vampires, and it runs over about six weeks with a visible success bar.

The decision offers a courtier from your own realm, a traveller on the road, or blood you buy for a little gold. Taking the courtier is the highest-paying and the riskiest: seven times in ten it goes cleanly, twice in ten the courtier dies, and once in ten you are seen and the secret starts moving. Higher Vampirism shifts weight out of both failure branches and into the clean one, so power makes you tidier as well as stronger.

The activity is the set piece. It needs a province with a city or tribal holding within reach of your court, and you choose two things before you ride:

ChoiceCostWhat it does
Vampire InstinctsfreeThe default. Nothing.
Scout the City25 gold+10 on the success chance.
Ambush35 gold and up+20 on the success chance.
Respect the DeadfreeThe default spoils choice.
Take What You Need20 pietyUnlocks a loot option at the end worth 20 to 125 gold plus twice the county's prosperity.
Target the Wealthy50 goldUnlocks a richer one, 50 to 250 gold plus four times prosperity.

Three weeks of searching, then the stalk, then the approach, then the kill. The person you are following is bound to the activity the first time you see them, so it is the same face all the way through. At the end the game rolls your success chance, and a clean night gives you six ways to finish it and 25 prestige; a botched one gives you three, all of which are damage control.

The killTrack XPWhat it costs the county
Drain them completely15 to 22A body. −5 county control, and a mark on the trail. Since 2.40 it lifts 65 stress, 15 more for a sadistic or gluttonous vampire; a compassionate one still comes away with some stress, and a temperate one lifts less.
Take just enough10They live, carrying Bloodloss for three years. No mark unless somebody talks, about one time in ten.
Take a small taste2 to 610 to 20 piety. No mark unless somebody talks.
Use the escape route5Nothing at all. Needs the hidden passage found earlier in the run.
Feed and loot4 to 8The gold from the spoils option you paid for. No mark unless somebody talks.

The three botched answers are to silence the witness, which is a second roll and can leave you with two bodies instead of one; to flee, which is a seven-in-ten chance the secret gets out and always a mark, because they are telling people; or to compel them to forget, which is a roll that starts at 45 and is a great deal better with the Compulsion perk. If the compulsion fails, they remember, and that is a mark too.

The numbers: the success chance, term by term

The bar starts at 50 and is clamped between 5 and 95. Everything below is added to it, and the breakdown is visible in the tooltip during the activity.

TermValue
Vampiric power+4 for each band above 10, 25, 40, 60 and 80 Vampirism, to a maximum of +20
Target development−0.2 per point of county development
County control−0.15 per point
Epidemic in the county+12
The county's holder is at war+8
Host is a hybrid+5
Host is a Known Vampire or Known Hybrid−15
Ambush laid / city scouted+20 / +10
IntentSatiate Thirst +5, Strengthen the Blood −5 (a player's default), Embrace the Night −10
Other vampires with you+5, +4, +3, +2, +1 as the party reaches 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6, to a maximum of +15
Host prowess / learning / intrigue×0.15 / ×0.1 / ×0.1
Host is shrewd / comely / reckless+5 / +5 / −5
An Original at the table+15
What you chose during the runthe running total of the night's events

A worked example: a Fledgling at 15 Vampirism with 20 prowess, 8 learning and 10 intrigue, feeding alone in a well-run city of 30 development and 80 control, with no preparation and the player's default intent, Strengthen the Blood: 50 + 4 (power) − 6 (development) − 12 (control) − 5 (intent) + 3 (prowess) + 0.8 (learning) + 1 (intrigue) comes to about 36 per cent. The same vampire with an ambush laid, two friends along and a poor, plague-struck county is over 90.

Who the victim is. The game looks in the local holder's court first, then a neighbouring county's court, then your own court as a last resort, and creates somebody on the spot if none of those work. Your own people are the last choice, not the first.

Afterwards. Everyone who attended carries Recently Fed for three years, worth +1 to four skills and +10 attraction, and cannot be invited to another feeding while it lasts. A victim who survives carries Bloodloss for three years, which a hunter can find and question.

Sire and progeny#

Whoever's blood made you is your Sire, and you are their Progeny. The relation is asymmetric on purpose: the progeny likes the maker by +400, the maker likes the progeny by +150. It is set on every route in, which was not true before 2.33; a vampire made by the older paths had a sire recorded in their tooltip and no actual relation, so none of the things below worked for them.

What the bond is for is Invoke the Bond, an interaction a sire can use on their progeny once every eight years. It has twenty orders and each one is paid for out of the sire's own Vampirism track, which is the whole design: leaning on your bloodline costs you rank.

OrderCostOrderCost
Entertain Me5Support my Influences20
Demand Gold10Be a Fallen Hunter20
Dismiss your Regent10Give me your Prisoners20
Become my Patron10You Owe Me25
Tell me your Secrets10Surrender your Wars25
Release your Prisoners10Marry me30
Join my Wars15Take my Faith35
Become my Lover15Take my Culture35
Join Me15Give me the Titles40
Break your Betrothal15Commit Suicide75

Refusing an order breaks the bond outright: the relation is removed from both sides and the sire picks up an opinion penalty that is also a legal reason to imprison or execute him. A sire needs at least 10 Vampirism to invoke anything at all.

The bond does quieter work as well. A sire and a progeny can always invite each other to a feeding, without either of them having to hold the other's secret, because a maker knows what their progeny is. An AI creature will not sell out its own blood to a hunter. And a hybrid's death on a hunt can be remembered by two different makers, the one who gave the blood and the one who did the clawing, where a vampire's can only be remembered by one.

The Sire Bond#

New in 2.40. When someone you turned also loves you, your blood holds them. The Sire Bond makes them your puppet, a kind of its own in the base game's Puppets list (Intrigue, Puppets), and through them you can act where they stand.

Who can hold it. A player who is a hybrid, or a vampire who has learned Vampire's Kiss. Nobody else sees the Sire Bond slot. The bound one must be your progeny (your blood turned them, Sire and progeny) and your lover or soulmate, an adult, still a vampire or hybrid, and not played by a person. The AI never holds a Sire Bond; an AI sire uses Invoke the Bond.

It forms by itself. When you turn your lover or soulmate, or someone you turned comes to love you, The Sire Bond arrives a few days later, or after about two months when the turning takes longer to finish. Then [she] is mine. makes them your Sire Bond puppet. I will not hold [her] like that. lets the bond go, and it will not form again by itself. You hold one Sire Bond puppet at a time; if you already act through someone, the new bond simply holds until you choose it. On a save from before 2.40, a player whose turned lover already loves him gets the event within his first three months.

It lasts. The bond outlives the romance: if the love fades you are told The Bond Holds, and they stay yours. It ends only when one of you dies, when either of you stops being a vampire or hybrid, or when you release it.

  • Sense the Sire Bond (Friendly, free): takes up a bound or loving progeny as your Sire Bond puppet, or changes from one to another. The one you set aside stays bound and can be sensed again.
  • Seduce Me (on their puppet card, once a year): if you are still lovers, they come to you and your stress eases; if the romance has ended, they set out to seduce you again.
  • Release the Sire Bond (on their puppet card): they are free, and the bond will not form again by itself. While they still love you, Sense the Sire Bond can bring it back.

What you can ask of them. Right-click someone and act as your sire-bound lover. Nothing you ask wears on them: no stress, no lost affection and no yearly cost.

FavourYour cooldownWhat it does
Speak for Me180 daysSomeone in their reach (their court, family, spouse, vassal or liege, or someone in the same place) thinks 20 better of you, fading over five years. Not again on the same person while it lasts
Draw Out Their Secrets1 yearSomeone in reach who trusts them (a good opinion of them, a friend, a spouse or close family) may give up a secret you did not know. A hidden roll on your lover's intrigue and that trust; if it fails, the target trusts your lover less
Cover My Tracks2 yearsOn the AI lord of the county your lover stands in, when it carries a trail. If the trail leads to you, your lover may talk him out of one mark of it; if that fails, he trusts your lover less. If the trail is not yours, you are told so
Give Them Your Blood1 yearVampire blood for someone in your lover's reach who trusts your lover, is not played by a person, has no vampire blood in them already, and whom the blood would take. It heals them now, and if they die with it still in them, or ask to be turned, they rise a vampire with your lover as their maker

They also do everything any puppet can: Seize and Imprison, Strike Them Down, Loom Over Them, Keep Them Company, Seduce, Romance, Order a Murder, Hand It Over and Send Me Your Gold. The base game's own puppet actions suit a lover who wants to please you: gifts, befriending, swaying, learning a language, sharing and exposing secrets, hosting and inviting, alliances and truces, demanding payment or an item, vassal directives and leaving their own spouse, and, when they hold land, imprisoning, releasing, pardoning and paying ransoms. Each keeps the base game's own rules: torture needs a lover who is a torturer or sadistic, and a fabricated hook or blackmail needs a schemer.

A careless favour. Each of the bond's own favours, and each puppet deed done through it except handing over an item, sending gold, or a seduction or romance aimed at you, has a small hidden chance to leave a hole in the memory of the county where your lover stands, the same trace a botched compulsion leaves (Covering your own kills). For a careful sire it is about 3%. It rises with low Vampirism, with leaning on the bond again within three years, with a lover who is stressed or has come to hate you, with a ruler as the target, and when you are already known for what you are; a deceitful sire is a little safer. It never passes 40%.

Compulsion (Compel Mortal)#

Compulsion is the vampire's reach into other people's heads. The interaction is Compel Mortal; Compulsion is the name of the perk that strengthens it. It is available to vampires and hybrids, has a six-month global cooldown and a three-month cooldown against the same person, and it works on mortals only. Since 2.39 werewolves cannot be compelled either, so it is never offered on a vampire, werewolf, hybrid or demon, and since 2.40 never on a golem or a child. It is not offered on a witch who is openly one, or whose secret you hold; a secret witch can be tried, but it fails and each of you learns the other's secret. A mind guarded against domination (the Draught of a Clear Mind, an equipped Seal of Solomon or Dragonscales, and Witchcraft's own protections) greys it out: Their mind is guarded against domination.

You also have to be able to reach them. The target must be in your realm, your liege's realm or a bordering one, or share a court with you, or be standing in the same place you are, or be at the same activity. A feast is a compulsion range extender.

A player's compulsion makes a puppet (2.40). There is no list of commands any more. If it takes, you pay 25 Vampirism, or 15 as a True Vampire (the last perk of the vampire tree, or of the hybrid's vampire tree), and the mortal becomes your Compelled for one command: see The Compelled. You need that much before you try, you hold one Compelled at a time, and the target must not already be someone's puppet. Another player's character can never be compelled into one.

The AI's commands. An AI vampire still compels the old way: it picks one of these commands for its own reasons and pays its price. Since 2.40 it is mostly the callous, sadistic and arrogant who do it, and their victims grow Wary just the same. Release Me is the one row a player still uses, from his own cell.

CommandNeedsCostsDoes
Become my Patron1010Adds them as a contact. Landless adventurers only.
Demand Gold1515They pay you 100 gold.
Join Me2515They become your courtier.
Become my Lover2515Sets the lover relation.
Leave Prison205Releases them from their cell. Not a wolf whose gaoler owns the Warded Chains.
Release Me (2.39)2015From your own cell: click your own portrait, and you compel your gaoler. It is the only order you can give from prison. If it takes, a hidden roll decides whether it is done without a fuss: 20 plus 0.6 for each point on your vampiric track, at most 85. Quiet, he lets you go and believes it was his idea; loud, it counts as an escape. Refused, he learns what you are and you go to the dungeon. Not for a hybrid held in the Warded Chains.
Tell me your Secrets2020Reveals every secret they own to you.
Do What I Say3535Gives you a loyalty hook.
End your Life8585They die. This one leaves a body.

Resistance is built from your own power, which climbs steeply with the track and climbs about sixty per cent faster again with the Compulsion perk, set against the target's will: learning resists, brave and stubborn resist hard, craven and trusting fold, and a hunter resists by −20 before you start and a further −10 for every ten points of Monster-Hunter track he has. A hunter at the top of his track is effectively immune.

Two things happen afterwards. A refused compulsion hands your victim your secret if you have one. And a compulsion used in a county that carries your own trail can bury an incident, or leave a hole in the county's memory if it goes badly. Covering your own kills has the odds.

The Compelled#

New in 2.40. A Compelled puppet carries out one command, and the compulsion ends with it. Use Compel Mortal on their portrait, or pick them from the Compelled slot in the Puppets list, which shows to vampires and hybrids and lists your courtiers, guests, councillors and prisoners. Then act as them: right-click yourself, or someone else, and choose them in the "interact as" row.

CommandWhereWhat it does
Join My WarOn yourselfA landed puppet joins every war you lead, on your side. Not while they fight a war of their own, or have a liege or vassal at war with you
Join My CourtOn yourselfAn unlanded puppet comes to your court, with the family who travels with them
Become My LoverOn yourselfThey become your lover. It is not a romance, and nothing that cares for romance counts it as one. Not on close family
Do What I SayOn yourselfA strong hook on them. 10 more Vampirism, 35 in all (25 as a True Vampire)
Tell Me Your SecretsOn yourselfEvery secret they keep, and every secret they know about others
Become My PatronOn yourself, as a landless adventurerA landed puppet becomes one of your contacts
Send Me Your GoldOn yourself100, 500 or 1,000 gold from their own purse, or all of it
Release ThemOn one of their prisonersThey open the cell. Not for a wolf held in the Warded Chains
End Your LifeTheir puppet cardThey die and leave a body, and the next morning you decide what the county makes of it (What They Will Find). 60 more Vampirism, 85 in all (75 as a True Vampire). Not in another player's family while the multiplayer rule against it is on
Let Them GoTheir puppet cardEnds the compulsion with no command. The Vampirism is not returned

On someone else, a Compelled can also do the deeds any puppet can: Seize and Imprison, Strike Them Down, Loom Over Them, Keep Them Company, Seduce, Romance, Order a Murder or Hand It Over. Whichever you choose is the command.

Afterwards. Unused, the compulsion fades after a month (The Compulsion Fades). Five to twelve days after it ends, or 25 to 35 when the command sent them out on an errand, someone close to them (a spouse, a friend, family or the court physician) tells them they have not been themselves, or, with no one to tell them, they notice it alone (Not Yourself). They become Wary for three years: they cannot be compelled while it lasts, and plots against them go a little worse. They learn it was you only if they already knew what you are, and then they also think 30 less of you (Compelled Me). Nobody can be compelled again within 60 days of the last time.

How a vampire looks#

Turning lightens a vampire's skin by one step. That is the Supernatural: Paleness rule, which ships as "Default (Vampires)" and can be extended to hybrids or switched off. How the portrait ages over the centuries is the Supernatural: Ageing rule (Game rules).

There are no fangs. The mod has no fangs portrait, although its event text mentions them. The game files still carry text for a "Supernatural: Visual Fangs" rule, but the rule itself was removed, so it never appears on the game rules screen.

Rank does not show. Nothing in the portrait code reads the Vampirism track, so a Fledgling and an Elder are drawn the same; the rank shows in the trait name and the modifiers.

The Originals#

Eight characters are placed in history as the Mikaelson family: Mikael and Esther, and their children Freja, Finn, Elijah, Niklaus, Rebecca and Kol. Esther and Freja are witches. Six of them turn on the same day in 866, and five days later Mikael founds a landless adventurer camp and takes the others into it, so the Originals start a campaign as a travelling company rather than a realm. Niklaus is the mod's one placed hybrid, and every hybrid that has ever existed in any save descends from somebody he or a player made.

Mechanically, being one of them does three things: an Original at a feeding is worth +15 on the success chance, the male Originals wear no beard and no headgear, and any vampire attending a feeding with one has a four-in-five chance of a short event chain about meeting them, once per character ever.

The moon

Werewolves, their packs and the six hides, and the hybrids made from them.

WerewolvesPacksThe Six HidesHybrids

The moonWerewolves#

A werewolf is mortal. It ages and scars like anyone else. What it has against an ordinary death is fifty years of added life expectancy at a full track, twenty-five more if a hide chooses it, and thirty at full Hide Bond.

WerewolfKnown WerewolfTrue WerewolfWerewolf lifestyle

The trait gives +2 martial, +2 diplomacy, +1 learning and +15 prowess before any rank, plus immunity to non-epidemic disease and +10 life expectancy. It renames itself by rank as the Lycanthropy track fills, and it is the only creature whose power reads as fighting strength first.

Becoming one: the bite, the wound and birth#

Being clawed does not make you a werewolf. It gives you the Infected Wound, a health trait with its own Infection track that sits on you for one to three years and then resolves. The exact date is rolled the moment the wound lands, somewhere between 365 and 1,095 days out, and nothing you do moves it.

While you carry it you are visibly sick. The base wound costs 0.75 health, 2 martial, 1 intrigue, 3 prowess and a slice of fertility, and it disables combat leadership outright for the whole incubation, not just the bad end of it. Each tier of the Infection track adds more on top, and the tiers rename the trait as you climb: Infected Wound, Festering Wound, Virulent Wound, Ravaging Wound, Consuming Wound. At the top rung you are carrying −2.5 health, −10 martial, −15 prowess and a fifth more stress gain.

The track moves three ways. It creeps up on its own, three points a month, which over a long incubation is most of a full track. Roughly every three to four months an event fires from a pool of nine, and each one is a choice between pushing the infection down and paying for it in stress, or letting it climb and taking the comfort. And one event in that pool, The Wolf Stirs, asks you the real question: do you want this. Answering yes lowers the infection. Answering no raises it. The flag you set matters far more than the number, and this is the single most counter-intuitive thing in the chain.

Some of the other events in the pool matter more than they look. A Healer's Hands is the biggest single drop available, at up to 20 points, at a 15 per cent risk that the physician works out what you are. A Hunter Grows Suspicious is where naming the werewolf who clawed you hands a hunter a named target, and is one of the ways hunts start. The Wound Worsens can take a limb. The One Who Clawed You opens a short relationship chain with the wolf who made you that can end in friendship, a lover or a soulmate.

How it ends. On the appointed day The Fever Breaks fires, you pick one of up to seven stances, and the game rolls one of six outcomes against your infection level, your traits and the flags you set along the way:

OutcomeWhat happens
Something MoreYou turn, and start at 30 Lycanthropy. Grants Strong if you lack it.
The Change Takes HoldYou turn, and start at 10.
A Hard TurningYou turn at zero, plus 30 stress.
The Curse Loosens Its GripYou stay human and gain Strong.
A Bitter ReprieveYou stay human, wounded, plus 20 stress.
The Change Consumes YouYou die.

Low infection is what buys the clean turnings; high infection feeds both the scarred reprieve and the death. Yielding forecloses the good non-turn entirely. Resisting makes both non-turns much likelier and also feeds the death bucket, because resisting is what drove your infection up in the first place.

Hides and packs in the fever. Three things from 2.37 reach into this chain. When a hide is near, the pool gains a ninth event, It Lies Beside the Bed, and The Fever Breaks offers Reach for the hide, which always ends in the best turning (how to get a hide). A wolf carried through a Werewolf Gathering gets likelier clean turnings and the stance Let the pack carry me. And a hide that carried you through the fever counts 20 toward its fit with you.

The Confer Death game rule does not protect you here. It is read only by the two hybrid turnings, and by nothing in the werewolf wound chain. A player who sets it to Off will still lose characters to The Change Consumes You.

The three ways to hand it out. None of them costs gold or piety.

  • Offer Gift. You ask; they decide. The wound starts at 5 to 15 infection and is flagged voluntary, which helps at the climax. The AI starts at −25 against and is moved most by fear of its own death, by being your child, spouse or soulmate, and by dread. A hook auto-accepts it, and the hook is consumed.
  • Force Gift. Hostile, no reply, straight to a wound at 40 infection, tier three before the first event fires, flagged forced, which pushes the roll toward the rough turning and toward death. You need to be physically near them: same location and same army, activity or landless status, or they are your vassal, prisoner, courtier or guest. It costs you nothing but gives you +5 dread and puts the victim on a potential-rival footing. A player can do it once every five years; an AI once every ten.
  • Ask for the Gift. The mirror, and player-only: the AI never asks. You need to know the target is a werewolf, or, since 2.39, he is your spouse, close family, the wolf who clawed you or one you clawed. None of the three is shown on a child or usable by one, and a bite in an event can wound a child but never infects him. The wound starts low and voluntary, same as being offered it.

There is no equivalent of the vampire's Ask to Not be Turned. Once a wound is in you, it ends in a turning, one of the two reprieves, or death.

Birth. Children of werewolves are born werewolves, never hybrids. Which parent counts depends on the Supernatural: Inheritance/Pregnancy rule, which ships on the Default (Werewolf Trait Only) setting. A newborn does not get the trait directly; it gets a flag, and a hidden event decides how much of a head start the bloodline gives it, weighing every parent and grandparent. Two supernatural grandparents is a meaningfully stronger child than none.

Ranks#

Five names, on the Lycanthropy track:

LycanthropyName
Under 20Fledgling Werewolf
20 to 49Werewolf
50 to 79Seasoned Werewolf
80 to 99Dread Werewolf
100Elder Werewolf

The 50 boundary is the one that matters: it is where the Werewolves regiment unlocks. Rank does not change your portrait. Before 2.38 the third and fifth ranks were Feral and Primal.

The numbers: what each rung of the track adds

Rungs sit at 20, 40, 60, 80 and 100 and are cumulative, so they do not line up with the rank names. A character at 70 is called a Seasoned Werewolf and is carrying the rungs up to 60.

RungWhat it adds
20+8 life expectancy, +1 martial, +1 diplomacy, +7 prowess, +5% werewolf lifestyle experience, +10 dynasty opinion
40+8 life expectancy, +1 martial, +7 prowess, +5% experience, +1 to the size cap of Wolves and Bats, −15% inbreeding chance
60+8 life expectancy, +1 martial, +1 diplomacy, +7 prowess, +5% experience, no prowess loss from age
80+8 life expectancy, +1 martial, +7 prowess, +5% experience
100+8 life expectancy, +1 martial, +1 diplomacy, +7 prowess, +5% experience, +1 to the size cap of Werewolves and Vampires, +1 personal scheme, +15 powerful vassal opinion

2.38 cut the Martial the trait gives: +2 at the start and +1 a rung, where it was +3 and +2. At a full track, with the base trait, that totals +50 life expectancy, +7 martial, +5 diplomacy, +1 learning and +50 prowess, a quarter again on lifestyle experience, and an extra scheme slot.

How the track fills. Every living werewolf gets +1 a year, which is a hundred years to Elder on the drip alone. The main source is Satiate Hunger, on a five-year cooldown for a player. A successful hunt pays 15 to 35, a kill 10 to 25, running the border 12 to 25, and crossing another wolf's ground 15 to 30. Turning cleanly pays 10 or 30 once. Surviving a hunter pays the same for every kind: 15 to 30 for beating one on a hunt, 8 to 15 for looking him in the eye, 5 to 12 for going to ground or sending him to another county, and 4 to 10 for running when you are warned. Twenty to twenty-five years of consistent hunting is a realistic run to the cap.

Satiate Hunger#

Satiate Hunger is the decision, and it is free: no gold, no piety, no prestige. Three days later you pick a route. Hunting your own people is the high-return one and the only one with teeth in it: 70 per cent a clean hunt, 20 per cent you kill a vassal, 9 per cent you are seen and become a Known Werewolf, and 2 per cent a hunter puts a silver arrow in you. Rank shifts that in your favour: at Lycanthropy 15, 35 and 55, weight moves out of the two bad results and into the clean hunt, 30 points in all by 55. Running the border and crossing another wolf's ground are the safe options: lower experience, no death roll, a suspicious neighbour, and in the border case a prowl mark on the county.

Shifting#

Werewolves and hybrids get a self-targeted Shapeshift interaction with three forms. It is free, instant, has no cooldown and no duration, so you stay in the form you chose until you choose another. The AI can never open it; the block that would tell an AI when to shift is present in the file but unreachable.

FormWhat it gives
HumanNothing. This is the absence of the other two.
Half-Wolf+3 prowess, +2 martial, +2 learning, −2 diplomacy, −1 stewardship
Werewolf+6 prowess, +4 martial, −4 diplomacy, −2 stewardship, and you lose your hair

The full wolf form is worth a further +15 to your side of a hunt duel, which is the one place the choice has real weight. Set against that, a shapeshifted body is unmistakable: if you are killed while shifted, the corpse reads as a werewolf no matter what you actually were.

What shifting does not do is change your model. There is no wolf portrait, no wolf head, no new skin. The only visible change is going bald in full wolf form, plus the modifier on your character sheet. The prowess in both forms is the kind that deliberately does not alter your portrait's build.

One other thing can put you in wolf form: during a hunt, when a hunter is at your door, you can answer it as the wolf. That is the only timed shift in the mod, thirty days, and it costs you your secret with every hunter attending.

Maker and get#

The mod tracks who clawed whom with a pair of relations, Clawed and Clawed By. They are identifiers and nothing more: both are worth zero opinion. Compare the vampire's Sire and Progeny, worth +400 and +150. The werewolf's sentiment comes from a separate pair of permanent opinion modifiers instead: +50 grateful if the turning was voluntary, −50 resentful if it was forced. A forced victim can be linked to the wolf who made him and be his rival at the same time.

Maker and get also count as kin when a hunter comes: see Who stands with the creature. Standing groups of wolves are Packs.

Packs and the Werewolf Gathering#

Everything here applies to hybrids too; the differences are under Hybrids. A pack is a standing group with an alpha, a Second and up to 25 wolves, the alpha included, and the wolves have a place among each other that rises and falls.

All of it sits behind the Supernatural: Packs game rule, which is on by default. With it off there are no packs, but the Werewolf Gathering still runs and wolves can still Challenge each other.

Pack Standing#

Every werewolf and hybrid has a second track, Pack Standing, from 0 to 100. It decides who may found a pack, who stands Second, and who may Challenge the alpha. A wolf born to it starts at 25; a wolf made by a bite starts at nothing. A werewolf who becomes a hybrid keeps his standing. On a save from before 2.37, a wolf born to it gets his 25 the first time he founds a pack or finishes a Werewolf Gathering.

What moves itPack Standing
Each year in a pack+5 below 50, +3 from 50, +1 from 75
A Werewolf Gathering completed+10, or +20 with the intent Earn My Place
A Challenge won against a wolf who stood near you+15
A Gathering run on a rival's land+5 to everyone who came
Leaving a pack−15, or −7 with Wolf Politics
A Challenge lost, or turned down−10

Every gain is doubled in a pack whose alpha is Alpha's Champion, except a Challenge won (since 2.40). The yearly gain slows as a wolf rises, so the top of the track takes years in a pack, Gatherings and Challenges to reach. A True Alpha in a pack never sits below 75 for long: the yearly tick lifts him back up.

The numbers: what Pack Standing adds

Rungs at 25, 50, 75 and 100. The first adds +5 attraction opinion, +5 courtly opinion, +1 stewardship, +1 learning and +0.25 prestige a month; each of the other three adds +5 attraction opinion, +10 courtly opinion, +2 stewardship, +1 learning and +0.5 prestige a month. Since 2.40 the rung at 75 also adds +5% werewolf lifestyle experience, and the rung at 100 another +5% and +2 prowess. At 100 that totals +20 attraction opinion, +35 courtly opinion, +7 stewardship, +4 learning, +1.75 prestige a month, +10% werewolf lifestyle experience and +2 prowess.

Founding, joining and leaving#

Found a Pack costs 50 prestige and needs Pack Standing 50, or 40 for a wolf born a wolf or for a hybrid with the Pack Leader perk, or 25 when another wolf at your court could run with you. A Champion can found one at any standing. An AI wolf founds a pack only with another wolf at his court or as a Champion, and invites up to three of the wolves at his court as soon as he has. You are its alpha, and the highest-standing wolf after you is your Second.

Interaction or decisionWho, on whomWhat it costs
Invite to the PackAn alpha, on a wolf in no packNothing. A wolf who refuses cannot be asked again for two years, or one as Tundra's Champion. (Before 2.39 the invitation could vanish and start that wait anyway.)
Ask to Run With Their PackA wolf, on another pack's alphaIf you are leaving a pack to do it: −15 standing (−7 with Wolf Politics), and −15 opinion from your old alpha for five years
Lure AwayAn alpha with Wolf Politics or standing 75, on a wolf in another packHis old alpha thinks −25 of you for ten years. Once every five years per wolf.
Leave the PackAny wolf but the alpha−15 standing (−7 with Wolf Politics), and −15 opinion from the alpha for five years
Disband the PackThe alphaNobody loses standing. The AI never does it.

You can only invite, ask or lure a wolf you know to be one: openly known, family, your spouse or a pack-mate, someone whose secret you hold, someone tied to you by the claw, or, since 2.38, a wolf at the same court. A wolf turned by a bite whose maker runs a pack with room gets An Invitation three days after he turns.

Packs recruit. Each year a packless adult wolf has a 20% chance of being asked by an AI alpha with room who knows what he is, does not dislike him and is not his rival. The alpha must be his kin, or tied to him by the claw, or be within reach and either share his faith or culture or think well of him; the wolf must not think badly of the alpha either. Kin, the claw and a Champion alpha weigh more, and the same alpha asks the same wolf at most once in two years. The question comes as An Invitation.

An AI wolf asks to run with another pack, or leaves his own, only for a reason: he has no pack, he and his alpha have no use for each other, or a friend or lover runs in the other. Asked with Ask to Run With Their Pack, an AI alpha whose pack has only one or two wolves is readier to say yes.

When an alpha dies, the pack goes to his co-alpha if he has one, then to the highest-standing Champion in it, then to the wolf who killed him if that wolf is in the pack, and then to the highest-standing wolf left.

What a pack gives#

  • Runs With a Pack: +1% werewolf and hybrid lifestyle experience and +0.5% to every lifestyle for each wolf in it, so a full pack of 25 is worth +25% and +12.5%. A pack with a True Alpha in it counts double. The Pack Mentality perk adds half as much again.
  • Every year: Pack Standing (+5, or +3 from 50 and +1 from 75) and +2 Lycanthropy, or +3 under an alpha who is a Champion, with one more for two Champions in the pack and two more for three or more. A Champion in a pack gains +5 Hide Bond a year rather than +2.
  • Two Champions in one pack give every member Two Spirits Run Together: +2 prowess and +10% werewolf and hybrid lifestyle experience. Three or more give The Old Ones Gather: +4 prowess, +0.2 health and +20%.
  • On a hunt, a pack-mate in the county comes through the door as kin, worth 12 off the hunter's side (see Who stands with the creature).

The Pack Comes Home (2.39) is the pack's own night by its fire. It is the yearly moment whenever a player wolf of the pack is due one, and for a pack with no other pack in range. A player needs at least two other grown, free wolves in his pack, and gets it at most every other year, about every three on average. The alpha and each wolf think 10 more of each other for three years (Ran Together), two AI wolves may become friends or fall out, and the player chooses how to spend it: go home to his spouse, stay by the fire with a pack-mate (who may become his lover), sit up with one (a friend), as the alpha Speak at the fire (+2 Pack Standing for every wolf of the pack, doubled for Alpha's Champion), or keep to himself.

The pack shows in the Situations window. The alpha owns its panel, and since 2.38 every other player in the pack sees a copy listing the alpha, the Second, a co-alpha, the Champions and the wolves, since 2.40 as The Rest of the Pack, so nobody is listed twice. Since 2.40 both panels also list Other Packs: the alphas of the packs within reach whom you may know to be wolves, because it is open, because you hold the secret, because a Blood Pact binds you, or because a pack moment named him. A button on the left of the screen, Your Pack, opens the Situations window on your pack alone; Show All Situations brings back the rest.

Once a year each pack has a 20% chance of a moment, 50% when a player runs with it. With a pack nearby it can be a scent on the border that ends in a rivalry or a truce, a wolf from the other pack asking to run with yours, two wolves of the two packs clashing, two wolves from different packs finding each other (lovers only if both are 18 or older), or word of where a hide is. A player alpha is asked when the moment is his to decide, and a moment never moves or wounds a player's character, or makes him a lover, without asking.

Pack Members Clash (2.40, was Young Wolves Fight). One of your wolves squares up to a wolf of another pack, and the event names that pack by its alpha's house and land, and names its alpha. Nothing is rolled until the alpha answers:

  • Pull them apart. Your wolf is disappointed in you. Between packs at odds, both alphas keep a truce.
  • Let them settle it. They fight it out, and the loser is hurt. They may come away friends, or with a grudge.
  • I stand behind [name]. Your name goes into the fight: if your wolf wins you gain prestige and he gains Pack Standing, and if he loses you lose prestige. Taking a side also sours relations between your house and the other alpha's a little, and more if a wolf dies, so fight after fight two houses can come to a feud. Two packs of the same house are not affected.

Between packs at odds (a rivalry, a feud over a hide, or a poor opinion either way, but never two packs bound by a Blood Pact) the fight is real: one time in ten the loser dies, and only ever an AI wolf, and backing your wolf may make the other alpha your rival. Any clash can be noticed and leave a trail on your wolf's county, one time in ten, or one in five between packs at odds. An AI alpha answers by his temper.

Challenge#

One wolf can Challenge another he knows to be a wolf if they are rivals, if he has the On The Prowl perk (werewolf tree) or the Challenge perk (hybrid tree), or if they share a pack and his standing is at least 25. To challenge your own alpha you must be the Second, or the Second must have lost to the alpha in the last two years, and you need standing 50. Neither of you can be badly wounded. You can challenge once a year, and the same wolf once every two. A Challenge can be sent to a wolf who will refuse it; refusing costs him 10 standing. An AI wolf refuses a Champion when he has no hide himself, and a fight he would likely lose, unless he is bold or it is his rival.

A blood debt. Since 2.40, when a wolf kills a member of the Order and the Temple Magus answers The Wolves Have Teeth with I will remember, every living member of that temple holds a blood debt against the killer, and the two houses think less of each other. A wolf who holds such a debt may Challenge the killer whatever their standing. Winning clears the debt and pays a little prestige. The killer himself is never told about his own kill.

The Hide Speaks (2.40). Before a Challenge, each player Champion in it hears his hide, the challenger first. Let it fight. (+2 Hide Bond), Hold it back. (he fights as a wolf, but his Hide Strength stays out of this fight), or, for Silverback's Champion facing a weaker wolf of his own pack, Stand down.: there is no fight, the one who stands down loses 10 Pack Standing, and his hide thinks better of him (+5 Hide Bond). Each hide speaks in its own voice.

The fight is decided the way every fight between creatures is, both sides in wolf form. Nobody dies of a Challenge. The loser is wounded, loses 10 standing and thinks −10 of the winner for five years. The winner gains 15 standing, as long as the loser stood no more than 10 below him. Beat your alpha and the pack is yours. If the alpha beats his Second, any wolf in the pack at standing 50 may challenge him for the next two years, or until the pack has a new alpha. A co-alpha stays co-alpha only beside the spouse the Unification joined them to.

Why an AI wolf challenges. Never for nothing. He challenges his own alpha only when the alpha is too weak to lead or is cruel to the better wolves of the pack. Too weak: he would likely beat him; the alpha is infirm, incapable or blind; the pack holds the alpha 15 standing below him; the alpha is craven; or the hunters know the alpha's name while the challenger still hides. Cruel: the alpha is sadistic, callous, arbitrary, deceitful or a kinslayer, the challenger is none of these, and he has the virtue that stands against it (compassionate, just or honest), or he has come to dislike the alpha, or two of the pack have. Another pack's alpha he takes on only over a feud: a rival, a grudge, or real hatred. Any other wolf he challenges over a feud, or to climb his own pack against a wolf who stands as high and whom he would beat. He picks fights he can win, takes worse odds against a cruel alpha, and never challenges his spouse, lover or friend. When an AI wolf challenges you, he tells you why.

The Werewolf Gathering#

An activity for werewolves and hybrids. Any wolf who is a ruler or a landless adventurer can host one, every five years, for the same gold as a camp party. It is held at a county seat in reach, a forest or taiga by preference, with up to 12 guests: werewolves you are close to, your own pack, other alphas you know of, and anyone you know to be in the bite's fever. Hybrids are an optional invitation.

PhaseLengthWhat happens
The Calling2 weeksThe wolves arrive.
The Run4 weeksEach week the host has an 80% chance of an event, about three in all, from fourteen: a farmer's flock, a hunters' camp, an old wolf, the old stones and more. The run costs the county 1 development and leaves a trail with the host's name on it.
The Circle1 weekThe fire: the Choosing, Word at the Fire, challenges, pacts and the Unification.

Intents. The host can Lead the Pack, Run a Rival's Land (held abroad, everyone who came gains prestige and +5 standing), Seek the Old Ways (a 60% chance to learn of a lost hide that would take him, or where the hide that fits him best is kept; since 2.40 that includes a hide nobody wears in somebody's keeping, and the old wolf names its keeper) or Hold the Unification. A guest can Earn My Place, Challenge the Alpha (it only comes to a fight for the host's own Second, or any wolf of his pack once the Second has lost to him; since 2.40 an alpha is not offered it) or Seek Another Pack.

At the fire. A hide that someone has brought and nobody wears has a 60% chance (70% with a player at the fire) to choose the best-fitting wolf there; its holder can let it go or fight for it, though a holder who wins is its Champion only if it would have him. An AI holder never fights a player the hide chose. A guest who came to Seek Another Pack asks to run with the host's pack, and a player host is asked. In Word at the Fire every wolf learns one thing he did not know: where a hide is, that one is lost, or who the True Alpha is. Since 2.40 it never names a hide he holds or one kept in his own county, it tells him first of hides he has never heard of, and if word of his own hide has got out, that comes as a line of its own. A wolf at standing 50 who learns that someone holds a hide nobody wears gets a claim on it. If Silverback's and Midnight's Champions are both there, they leave as rivals, unless they are married or lovers.

At the end every wolf gains Pack Standing, +5 Lycanthropy and, for a Champion, +5 Hide Bond. Wolves of different packs or faiths who shared the fire think +10 of each other for five years. Anyone who came in the bite's fever has been carried: at The Fever Breaks the clean turnings are likelier, death is less likely, and there is a new choice, Let the pack carry me. If he turns, he joins the host's pack, if the host runs one with room.

Call the Packs is Alpha's action. For a year its Champion can invite every alpha in reach to a Gathering, known to him or not, and each one who comes owes him a favour if the Gathering ends within that year. It can only be taken when a Gathering could follow: not within five years of his last.

The True Alpha, the Unification and the Blood Pact#

The True Alpha rises at most once in a hundred years, anywhere in the world. He has to be at Pack Standing 100, not the alpha of his pack, never have killed a wolf, and carry at least two of Just, Honest, Compassionate, Brave and Diligent. Each pack rolls 5% a year for its best candidate. When he rises he either takes his pack, and the old alpha thinks well of him for it, or leaves to found his own and takes some of it with him. Since 2.40 a player alpha decides it in Red Eyes in Your Pack: [He] leads now. (the True Alpha becomes alpha and you keep your standing), Then [he] must take it. (you fight him for the pack, wolf against wolf; if he wins he leads, if you win he leaves to found his own and some of yours may follow), or Go, and take who will follow. An AI alpha answers by his temper. A True Alpha's pack counts double for its lifestyle bonus, he has Hide Strength even without a hide, and Alpha wants him more than anyone.

The Unification joins two packs whose alphas are married to each other. It is held at a Werewolf Gathering under the intent Hold the Unification, needs a third wolf there to stand as elder and the two packs to come to no more than 25 together, and only works if there are no secrets between husband and wife. Since 2.40 it also runs for a spouse who already runs in your pack: there is nothing to merge, but the spouse becomes co-alpha and every wolf is Evolved as below. Its requirement says what it needs (a spouse who leads another pack or runs in yours), and a pack can be unified only once. The packs become one, the spouse becomes co-alpha, and every wolf in it is Evolved: he fights as if in wolf form even when he is not, worth +15 in a fight between creatures and on a hunt. If either alpha is a hybrid, every wolf also gains +10 life expectancy. An AI host never joins a player's pack to his own, and a player whose pack is joined is told.

The Blood Pact is sworn at a Gathering when another pack's alpha comes to your fire, or since 2.40 through Offer Pack Terms. Between two rulers it is a real alliance; either way it is +20 opinion both ways for twenty years. A pact-bound pack's Champions are spared by your hide's hunt for the book's pages (Finding the pages).

Offer Pack Terms, and feuds over a hide#

New in 2.40. Offer Pack Terms is an interaction in the Supernatural menu for an alpha, on another alpha he knows to be a wolf, once every two years for the same alpha. He offers one of three terms:

TermWhat happens if it is acceptedNeeds
Run under my leadTheir pack joins yours, and their alpha runs in it at his standingNeither pack joined by the Unification, and the two together no more than 25
I will run under yoursYour pack joins theirs, and you run in it at your standingThe same
Swear a Blood PactBoth packs stay as they are, bound by a Blood PactNo pact already sworn between them

Who sees it. Since 2.40 Offer Pack Terms shows for any adult wolf, on the alpha of another pack whom he knows to be a wolf by the usual rules for knowing one, whose pack is bound to his by a Blood Pact, or whom one of the pack's yearly moments has named (that lasts twenty years, and each such moment renews it). Only an alpha can send it: a pack member sees it greyed with Only your pack's alpha can offer terms for it, and a wolf with no pack with You must lead a pack to offer terms. It is a letter, so distance does not matter. On a running save, the knowledge begins with the next moment between the two packs.

An AI alpha weighs the size of the two packs, what he thinks of you, a pact already between you, whether you have beaten him in a Challenge, and any feud over a hide. An ambitious alpha means to lead rather than follow, a gregarious one likes company, and a paranoid one does not trust you. The terms are checked again when he answers, so an alpha who has fallen or a pack that has filled in the meantime cannot be joined.

A Feud Over a Hide. Since 2.40 a feud follows only a hide that changed hands unkindly between two packs: taken from its Champion's body, won at a Gathering's fire, or taken from a living holder against his will (by a demand, a theft or a war). A hide that passed willingly starts no feud. The event says how it was taken, and the alpha who lost it chooses: They will not forget, and neither will I. (the feud, as below), [the taker] will answer for it. (a Challenge, where one is allowed; winning settles who is stronger, but does not bring the hide back), or Let it go. The hide chose. (both alphas let the grudge go, and some will call it weakness).

The Six Hides#

Six old wolf skins, each with a spirit and a will of its own: Silverback, Timber, Tundra, Graybeard, Midnight and Alpha. A hide chooses a werewolf or hybrid who fits it, and that wolf becomes its Champion. Everything here applies to hybrids too; the differences are under Hybrids.

A hide is an artifact in the trinket slot. Only an adult werewolf or hybrid can put one on, and only the hide's own Champion gets its power: anyone else who wears it gets 0.3 prestige a month and nothing more. One body carries one hide. A Champion who comes into a second one has to choose between them in Two Wolves: the hide he keeps gives him +10 Hide Bond, and the one he puts aside will not have him for five years.

The six, and who they choose#

A hide reads your traits. It takes you if you have at least one trait it likes and more that it likes than it dislikes. Failing that, it adds up a fit score, +15 for every trait it likes and −20 for every one it dislikes, plus half your Lycanthropy, and takes you anyway at 40 or more. Being carried through the bite's fever by a hide adds 20 to that score, and so does standing in a Werewolf Gathering's Circle (40 for a player). Since 2.40 a hide also leans toward the family of the Champions who wore it before: +15 if one was your parent, +10 a sibling, +8 a grandparent, +5 more if that Champion reached Hide Bond 75 or left his mark, and +5 if he died wearing it, at most +30 in all. This counts everywhere a hide chooses, and when it chooses you it says It has been in your family before, if you knew. Alpha is the exception: it takes only a leader, meaning the alpha of a pack or a ruler of duke rank or higher. With the Packs rule off, a True Werewolf or a wolf at full Lycanthropy also counts.

HideLooks forTurns fromIts Champion
SilverbackBrave, Just, Honest, Compassionate, StrongCraven, Deceitful, Sadistic+10 prowess, and the strongest in a fight. Its action is Stand Over Them: his guard on someone in his family or court for five years (detail).
TimberShy, Paranoid, Vengeful, WrathfulGregarious, TrustingAlone in the Woods while in no pack: +5 prowess, +10% werewolf and hybrid lifestyle experience. Harder to track and harder to unmask. Its action is Cover Your Trail (it was Vanish): the trail that points to him in the county he stands in is wiped out, Something Stalks Here and all, so hunters have nothing there to follow. Only that county, and not while a hunt is running there. Ten-year cooldown.
TundraCalm, Deceitful, Ambitious, PatientHonest, Impatient+2 diplomacy, +2 intrigue, and talks its way out of what others fight. Its action is Read Them: one secret of someone in reach, every five years.
GraybeardBrave, Stubborn, Arrogant, StrongCraven, ParanoidThe trait: +10 advantage, −30% retreat losses, −20% stress gain. Its action is Hold the Line: +10 advantage and −50% retreat losses for a year, every five years.
MidnightBrave, Just, Loyal, WrathfulCraven, Deceitful, Sadistic, DisloyalSince 2.40 a noble fury rather than a cruel one: +1 prestige a month, and cuts down more knights in battle. Its action is Let It Take Me: at war, with the wolf let run, twice the kills in his next battle and major stress after it. Three-year cooldown.
AlphaAmbitious, Diligent, Just, ArrogantLazy, Content, CravenLeaders only. As strong in a fight as Silverback, and every wolf in a pack its Champion leads gains Pack Standing twice as fast. Its action is Call the Packs.

Since 2.40 every hide trait and every hide's entry in the game's concepts ends with a plain paragraph, What it does, that says the same as this table.

A mortal can be chosen too. A man with no creature or hunter trait, and no witch, who is holding a hide nobody wears, and who fits it at 90 or more, has a one in four chance each year of It Knows You. Heed the call and he is turned and made its Champion; refuse and it leaves him alone for ten years.

A hide that turns you down says why. It Will Not Have You names the reason: no trait it likes, as many or more that it dislikes as it likes, or, for Alpha, that you lead nothing. That hide will not try you again for five years. Raising your Lycanthropy helps, since half of it counts toward the fit.

Silverback carries a page. Unless the Vade Maecum rule is Off, one of the four pages of the Vade Maecum Infernal starts inside Silverback's hide, and a Silverback remade after being lost gets it back. At Hide Bond 60, The Others tells its Champion there is a weight in the hide that is not his. A Mage can draw the page out of the hide, ask its Champion for it, or tear it away. The pages.

What a Champion gets#

The worn power is the same for all six: +20 prowess and a further +25% prowess, +25 life expectancy, +0.5 health, +5% knight effectiveness, and +1 to the size cap of the mod's monster regiments. The differences between the hides are in the Champion traits and in what each one's Hide Bond track adds.

  • He hears magic. A Champion counts as having Witchcraft's Magical Senses, so a spell cast near him rings. Once a year he can follow the ringing and go after the caster: a fight between the two that never kills, where winning wounds the caster and gives him +5 Hide Bond, and losing wounds him instead. Since 2.40 following it pays +2 Hide Bond either way. If the caster holds part of the Vade Maecum, the ringing carries the smell of the book, and following it tells him who holds it and gives him a claim on it. Once he has followed a caster, he can answer the next ringing from that caster with Let it ring for [name] no more; the decision Listen Again undoes it. Since 2.40 the Ringing's Champion options give his own Champion trait as the reason they are offered, not the Mage trait; Let it ring for [name] no more is not offered again for a caster he has already silenced; and Listen Again is shown only to a Champion. Witchcraft's own option to suppress the senses is not offered to a Champion who is not a Mage.
  • A Champion who practises magic, as a Mage, a True Witch or one born with the Source, regains a great deal of power every year in the Bond, and a Mage among them gains a point of Combat Magic as well. Other wolves get nothing extra.
  • He can raise the Werewolves regiment without the Lycanthropy 50 it normally needs, and can found a pack at any Pack Standing.
  • A known Champion is feared: +15 dread while he carries the Known Werewolf or Known Hybrid trait, +25 for Timber's.
  • Old age does not break him. Once a year the hide clears Infirm, Clouded Eyes, Faltering Heart, Withering Mind, Fragile Bones, Incapable and Blind, the same cleanup that keeps vampires young, and it clears them the day he is chosen.
  • An immortal Champion, which in practice means a hybrid, also gets Ageless Wolf: +20% werewolf and hybrid lifestyle experience.
  • The strength passed on (2.40). A new Champion inherits something from those who wore the hide before him: 5 Lycanthropy for each earlier Champion, at most 25, and a free perk point in his wolf tree when there were three or more.
  • The hide speaks his tongue. At Hide Bond 25 it learns his culture's language, and every later Champion of that hide speaks every language it has learned.
  • Nobody can take it from him while he lives. A worn hide carries no claims, and any transfer away from a living Champion is undone.

Hide Bond and the voice#

Hide Bond is the Champion's track, from 0 to 100. It is how deep the hide's spirit has gone into him, and it drives his Hide Strength.

What moves itHide Bond
Each year the hide is worn+2, or +5 while in a pack
Answering the hide's voice (the first choice of the first five)+15 each
Beating a hunter on a hunt+10, once per hunt
Each enemy knight he kills in battle+2, at most +6 a battle and +20 a year
A Werewolf Gathering completed+5
Two Wolves, whichever hide he keeps+10
Following the ringing / and winning+2 / +5 more
A page of the Vade Maecum in his hands: I will guard it / A Mage may have it+5 / −5, or −15 for Silverback's Champion
The Hide Finds a Page: Find it / Not now+5 / −5
The Hide Speaks: Let it fight. / Stand down.+2 / +5
Letting a beaten Champion crawl back after the hide duel+2
Taking the hide off, then putting it back on−5, or −10 if he needed to breathe

Without a war, a full track takes about ten to fifteen years. Along the way the hide speaks to him six times, no closer together than two years. The +15 Hide Bond needs the first answer; the action and the perk points come whichever he chooses.

Hide BondEventWhat it brings
Two weeks after he is chosenIt Speaks+15 Hide Bond
20The Last One+15 Hide Bond
40What It Wants+15 Hide Bond, and the hide's own action unlocks
60The Others+15 Hide Bond and a free perk point
80Leave My Mark+15 Hide Bond, and the choice below
100One WolfA free perk point

Leave My Mark. At 80 the hide asks for more than his tongue. If he leaves his mark, whoever wears the hide after him starts at half the Hide Bond he had when it left him. A Champion who wore the same hide before also keeps three quarters of his own old bond, and starts from whichever is higher.

The numbers: each Champion trait at Hide Bond 100

Every track has rungs at 25, 50, 75 and 100. The totals below include the trait's base.

Champion ofAt Hide Bond 100
Silverback+15 prowess, +4 martial, +4 stewardship, +10 courtier and guest opinion
Timber+5 prowess, +4 intrigue, +4 martial, −20 to the highest success chance a hostile scheme against him can reach
Tundra+6 diplomacy, +6 intrigue, +5 prowess, +10 to the highest success chance his own personal schemes can reach
Graybeard+5 prowess, +4 martial, +4 learning, +10 advantage, +10% army toughness, −30% retreat losses, −20% stress gain
Midnight+9 prowess, +4 martial, +1 prestige a month, +20% knight effectiveness (before 2.40, +15 dread and +20% dread gain)
Alpha+5 prowess, +4 diplomacy, +4 martial, +10 vassal opinion

Every one of them also adds +0.3 health, +5 life expectancy and +15% werewolf and hybrid lifestyle experience by 100. With the worn power, a Champion at full bond carries +30 life expectancy.

Hide Strength#

In a fight between creatures a Champion counts for more. His side is multiplied by his Hide Strength: ×1.5 at Hide Bond 0, rising to ×2.5 at 100. Silverback and Alpha add a further 0.25, and so does being the True Alpha, so the ceiling is ×3. A True Alpha who wears no hide is ×1.25.

It applies to a hunt's final fight when the hunter has come for a Champion, to a Challenge, to the fight over a hide at a Gathering, to following the ringing, and to two Champions meeting on a battlefield. It does not apply to cutting down ordinary knights in battle, which works on its own budget.

The numbers: how a fight between creatures is decided

Each side's weight is 50, plus the prowess difference between them (at most 25 either way), plus 0.6 × their creature track, plus 15 in wolf form, plus 10 for the alpha of a pack. That is multiplied by Hide Strength, and by 1.6 for a hybrid. The chance to win is A² ÷ (A² + B²), never below 2% or above 98%. The game never shows the number. The tooltip says The fight leans toward and a name when one side has 65% or better, and The two are evenly matched. between 35% and 65%.

Where the hides are, and how to get one#

The Supernatural: The Six Hides game rule decides how the game starts:

SettingAt the start
OffNo hide is ever created.
LostAll six are lost and wake in their own time.
Scattered (default)Alpha is lost. One of the other five, chosen at random, goes to an AI werewolf at Lycanthropy 50 or more who fits it, if there is one. The rest are kept in shrines.
AwakenedEach of the five goes to a fitting AI werewolf where one exists, and to a shrine where none does. Alpha is lost.

A shrine is a random holy site of any faith with room for another relic, held by a mortal ruler of a faith that reveres it. The county carries A Relic Kept Here (+1 county opinion), and under a Christian faith the hide is kept as the cloak of Saint Christopher. A hide nobody can place goes to the treasury of the highest-ranked mortal ruler instead. On a save that started before 2.37 the hides are placed at the first new year after the update.

Finding out where one is. When a hide goes into a shrine, every player wolf hears of it (Word Travels). Wolves also learn where hides are at a Werewolf Gathering's fire, from a passing word between packs, or by seeking the old ways. Since 2.39 a player wolf on the road may hear an innkeeper's story (A Tale on the Road): each time his journey moves into or beside a county whose shrine keeps a hide he does not know about, there is a 20% chance, and after it fires it does not come again for three years. What a wolf learns he knows for ten years. The hides placed at the start of a game are not announced: those have to be found.

Four ways to take a hide out of a shrine:
