  • Hold the county, or be its holder's top liege. The game lets either take a relic down from a holy site, and a hide is no different. AI werewolf lords have a decision for it.
  • Siege it. When a siege of the county completes and you are a werewolf or hybrid, or a wolf commands your army there, A Hide in the Shrine lets the wolf take it. The game's own siege looting can get to it first.
  • Make a pilgrimage there. The Relic at [county] offers to walk out with it: an intrigue duel against 25. Fail and you are caught: the county's holder thinks −30 of you for ten years, and you cannot try again anywhere for five.
  • Send for a Hide. A decision for a wolf who knows where a hide is kept. It costs a medium sum of gold, about six months of your income, and since 2.40 it sends a named man: your spymaster, or else your courtier with the best intrigue. Word comes back by distance: 45 to 90 days close by, 90 to 180 farther off, and up to a year from across the world. Word from [county] then offers the roads open to you. The chance of each is hidden; the option says in words whether it looks likely, could go either way, or is a long chance. Tell him to steal the hide. weighs his intrigue and half of yours; if he is caught he is held at the keeper's court and they know who sent him. Throw the Powder in the shrine., if you hold an Obfuscation Powder: nobody there remembers your man, whatever happens, and the Powder is spent. Go yourself, by night., for a werewolf with On The Prowl or a hybrid with Master of the Hunt: if it goes wrong you are hurt and the county talks of a wolf. Send a wolf of the pack instead., for an alpha: the wolf pays for a failure in blood. Make the keeper bring it to you., for a hybrid at 50 Vampirism or more, against an AI keeper who is not a creature and whose mind is not guarded: if it fails, the county talks of something that came in the night. Or Call him back with nothing. If the hide is gone before he gets there, the letter names the hide he went for.

A lost hide goes looking. It wakes after 5 to 20 years (Alpha, 15 to 40) and picks a wolf who fits it. If that wolf is a player who rules a county, a great white wolf is seen in his capital (Something in Your Woods); hunt it down with a great success and the hide is his. Anyone else simply finds it, in It Came Out of the Trees.

A hide does not lie idle. A hide that spends ten years with an AI who is not its Champion (a mortal, a wolf it refused, or a Champion keeping it as a spare) slips away one winter and is lost, to wake again for a wolf it fits. Since 2.40 that includes a hide an AI wolf keeps wearing after it refused him: it no longer goes on and off him, and an AI it refuses is never asked again. A hunter's trophy, a shrine's relic and a hide a player holds are never taken. A hide is never seeded to, and never asks, a golem, a demon-blooded character or Baba Yaga.

There are three more ways into one. At a Werewolf Gathering, a hide nobody wears that someone has brought can choose a wolf at the fire (The Choosing). In the bite's fever, Reach for the hide is offered when a hide is close, and always ends in the best turning. And a hunter who kills a Champion is left holding the hide in What the Beast Left, where he can keep it, give it to his liege, give it to his church, or try to burn it.

In war and on the hunt#

A new Champion's wolf is held back. When a war starts that he is part of, The Wolf and the War asks once whether to let it run, hold it back, or stop asking. The decisions Let the Wolf Run and Hold the Wolf Back change it at any time.

With the wolf running, he cuts through the enemy's knights on a fixed budget each battle:

ChampionPicks in a battle
Hide Bond under 503
Hide Bond 50 to 994
Hide Bond 1005
Midnight's Champion, or a battle in forest or taiga+1 each
Under Let It Take MeDoubled

Each pick kills an enemy knight whose prowess is no higher than his, with a 50% chance of a second. A hunter riding as a knight on the other side can find him first: one time in four he is wounded and the pick is spent. Every kill reads torn apart in battle, pays the usual prestige for a knight and +2 Hide Bond, and at ten kills he becomes the Wolf if he has no other nickname. Once a battle there is a 25% chance (Timber's, 10%) that the enemy commander works out what he is.

Two Champions on opposite sides who are both letting the wolf run may find each other on the field. Since 2.40 that is the hide duel. Battle lines that name you, as the Champion or as his victim, read properly since 2.40.

On the hunt. Any wolf at the door can answer Let it out, but for a Champion it is a card the others do not have: the hunter dies without a fight, the hunters who came with him learn what he is, and the card takes ten years to come back (seven at Hide Bond 100). Hunters find a Champion's trail harder to read (−15 on the tracking bar, −10 more for Timber's Champion without a pack), and a hunter who carries a hide himself reads any wolf's better (+10). AI hunters leave a known Champion with his card ready alone unless they mean it: a Monster-Hunter track of 75 and a reason, such as a relative or a trainer the Champion killed, a rivalry or a zealous streak. A hunter who does kill one gets double the prestige and Monster-Hunter experience.

Silverback's Stand Over Them puts its Champion's guard on someone in his family or court for five years. If a hunter comes for them, they can call on him, and he fights the hunter in their place.

The hide duel#

New in 2.40. When two Champions on opposite sides of a battle both have the wolf running, they may find each other, or they may not. A hidden roll decides it. It is likelier when either of them commands an army, is under Let It Take Me or has Hide Bond 75 or more, and in a small battle; it is less likely with Timber's Champion, or when a side brings ten knights or more. A pair that misses each other is not paired again for sixty days, and both go back to the enemy's knights. Hold the Wolf Back keeps a Champion out of it entirely: its tooltip says another Champion on the field will not find you, and Let the Wolf Run says one may.

Between two AI Champions it is settled at once, and one may break off before it starts: likelier when the fight leans against him, when he is craven, and for Timber's Champion. When a player is one of the two, it is played out during the battle in the duel window, both fighters on screen:

EventWhoThe answers
Across the FieldThe Champion who sees the other firstGo for the throat. / Wear it down. / Slip away into the fighting.
It Comes for YouThe one the other went forMeet it head on. / Hold your ground. / Break away. It tells you if the other is going for your throat.
It Is RunningThe one the other slipped away from, if he saw it goRun it down. / Let it go. Midnight does not lose a scent, Timber knows how to disappear, and a runner who is already hurt is easier to catch.

No number is shown. Each event says in words whether you are the stronger, the two are evenly matched, or the other is, and whether the other hide is one your family should hold. The fight itself is a fight between creatures: Hide Strength, Hide Bond and the wolf's track decide it, and going for the throat leans it a little your way.

  • Wear it down on both sides: the loser is wounded, and dies only if he was already badly wounded or maimed.
  • The throat on either side: this fight can kill. A loser who carries any wound dies of it. The winner is wounded too, unless he was much the stronger.
  • Getting away: a wolf who slips or breaks away and is not caught is out of it, and a wolf caught turning fights at a disadvantage.
  • Death comes last, and the killer takes the hide, as before. The result, Two Wolves on One Field, shows the winner standing and the loser in pain or dead. A winner who wore the other down can Let [him] crawl back to [his] own for +2 Hide Bond.

Your Wolf Has Seen Another. When an AI Champion who serves a player at war, as a landless knight, finds another Champion on the field, the player is asked: Let [him] go. or Call [him] back. A toast reports how it went.

In prison: Break the Chains#

New in 2.39. A Champion in prison can try to Break the Chains once in ten years: Try to break out of prison as the wolf. It cannot be used while his gaoler owns the Warded Chains. The roll is hidden and weighs two numbers against each other:

  • Breaks: 20, plus 40 × his Hide Strength, plus how far his prowess is above or below average (diplomacy for Tundra), and +15 for Alpha in a pack.
  • Holds: 50, +25 in a dungeon (not for Graybeard), +25 if the gaoler is a hunter, +10 for each hunter at his court, up to three.

A new Champion in house arrest with ordinary prowess breaks out about 62% of the time; a Silverback at Hide Bond 100 in a hunter's dungeon with two hunters at court, about 52%. A separate roll decides whether anyone sees it: 10, plus 0.3 for each point of Hide Bond and of his wolf's track, +15 for Timber, −15 for Midnight, −15 for a hunter gaoler, −10 in a dungeon, between 5 and 75.

The Door Gives Way: he is out, +5 Hide Bond. Seen, one of the gaoler's knights is wounded (two for Midnight, none for Silverback or Tundra), his secret is exposed (Timber's only to the gaoler), and the county is marked as for a kill; unseen, nobody is hurt and only a light mark is left. Caught at the Bars: he is wounded and moved to the dungeon, and if anyone saw it the gaoler learns what he is. Nobody dies either way. A player gaoler is told The Empty Cell, and the seen versions end: "Warded chains would have held him."

Losing a hide#

  • Death. The hide goes to whoever killed him. Failing that it goes to his heir, and failing that it is lost. His house is left with a claim on it, and so is his pack's alpha.
  • Ask to Unbind the Hide. A Mage or True Witch who knows what you are can cut the bond, if they agree to, for 150 gold. It leaves a wound, never a fatal one. You keep the hide, but it will not have you for ten years.
  • Abandon Lycanthropy or Abandon Being a Hybrid ends it, and takes you out of your pack too. Both sit behind the Embrace Decisions rule, which ships off.
  • Two Wolves, when he takes the other hide.
  • Tear the Hide Away. A Mage's spell to rip the Vade Maecum's page out of a worn hide. If it works on a Champion who was already maimed or badly wounded, he dies of it, and by the rule above the hide goes to the Mage.

When a hide passes unkindly from a wolf in one pack to a wolf in another, the two alphas can fall into a Hide Feud: −30 opinion both ways for ten years, and worse relations between their houses. Since 2.40 it needs a hide taken by force, at the fire or against its holder's will, and the alpha who lost it may also answer with a Challenge or let it go (feuds over a hide).

The cure#

Abandon Lycanthropy is a decision with no cost and no cooldown, and it takes everything: the trait, Known Werewolf, True Werewolf, all eight lifestyle perks and your secret. Since 2.37 it also ends a Champion's bond with his hide, which stays in his hands, and takes him out of his pack. It is behind the Embrace Decisions game rule, which ships off. With that rule at its default there is no cure in the game at all.

The cure leaves two things behind. If you were in wolf form when you were cured you stay in wolf form, and bald, permanently. The only thing that un-shifts a werewolf is the Shapeshift interaction, and that interaction will not show for someone who is no longer a werewolf. And nothing at all removes an Infected Wound before it resolves: no decision, no interaction, no physician. Once wounded you carry the health penalty and the wait, and the wait includes a death bucket.

BothHybrids#

A hybrid is the mod's most powerful creature and its rarest. One is placed in history. None can be born. Every other hybrid has to be made, one character at a time, and at the default settings the turning can kill.

HybridKnown HybridHybrid lifestyle

The trait carries +3 intrigue, +2 martial, +2 learning and +18 prowess before any rank, the highest base prowess of anything in the mod, plus immortality, disease immunity, no prowess loss from age, and a fertility penalty. It has three tracks, Vampirism, Lycanthropy and Pack Standing, and unlike the vampire and the werewolf it never renames itself: a hybrid at a full hundred on both tracks still reads "Hybrid" in the interface.

The numbers: the two creature tracks, and what a finished hybrid is worth

Both tracks have rungs at 20, 40, 60, 80 and 100, and each rung adds 5 per cent to hybrid lifestyle experience.

TrackAt a full 100, cumulative
Vampirism+10 intrigue, +2 stewardship, +40 prowess
Lycanthropy+5 martial, +3 diplomacy, +20 prowess, +0.10 fertility

With the base trait that is 78 prowess, +13 intrigue, +7 martial, +3 diplomacy, +2 learning, +2 stewardship, and half again on lifestyle experience. There is no life expectancy anywhere on the trait, because it does not need one.

Making one#

Two interactions make a hybrid, and both start from a werewolf. A vampire can never become one. Make Hybrid is used by a vampire or a hybrid on a werewolf who already has vampire blood in them, so the sequence is: be a werewolf, be given the blood with Offer Blood, then be turned. The hybrid Ask to be Turned runs the other way: a werewolf asks a vampire or a hybrid, and needs no blood first.

A human with no creature trait has two other routes: the ruler designer, and the Become a Hybrid decision, which needs the Supernatural: Embrace Decisions rule set to On.

The two turnings are the only place the Supernatural: Confer Death rule is read, and at its default they are dangerous. Make Hybrid starts at 40 live, 60 die, and each point of health above a pivot moves 10 from die to live. The pivot is 4 at the default, 5.5 on More Death and 2.5 on Less Death, so living is the likelier result above health 5, 6.5 and 3.5. Ask to be Turned starts at 50 live, 50 die, with the same pivots. With the rule set to Off, the turning always succeeds.

What a hybrid can do#

A hybrid can do nearly everything both parents can. It shapeshifts. It compels, paying from its own Vampirism track at the same thresholds a vampire pays. It feeds, on its own decision with a seven-year cooldown rather than the werewolf's five, which splits fifty-fifty between the vampire chain and the wolf chain. Since 2.40 the decision stays open until both tracks are full, and once one track is full it always runs the other one's chain. It claws, though no AI hybrid ever will, because the AI side of both claw interactions requires the plain werewolf trait. It regenerates: the cleanups that strip disfigurement and the traits of old age run on vampires and hybrids. Among werewolves only a hide's Champion gets them.

A hybrid cannot be compelled, and since 2.39 neither can a werewolf (Compulsion).

In a hunt a hybrid carries +10 on its own account, and the game reads whichever of its two tracks is higher when it prices the fight, the lord's investigation and the prestige for the kill. If it dies shifted, the body reads as a werewolf; otherwise it reads as a vampire. The mod never gives a hybrid a death of its own: in the old confrontation chain it dies as one or the other on a coin flip.

Hides and packs#

Packs and the Six Hides work for a hybrid as they do for a werewolf. These are the differences:

  • A hybrid Champion does not age, so he also gets Ageless Wolf: +20% werewolf and hybrid lifestyle experience (What a Champion gets).
  • A hybrid counts ×1.6 in a fight between creatures (Hide Strength).
  • The Challenge and Pack Leader perks are in the hybrid's Lycanthropy tree. Challenge lets him Challenge any wolf he knows; Pack Leader lets him found a pack at Pack Standing 40.
  • A werewolf who becomes a hybrid keeps his Pack Standing.
  • A hybrid alpha in a Unification gives every wolf of the joined pack +10 life expectancy (The Unification).
  • Hybrids are an optional invitation to a Werewolf Gathering.

The hybrid lifestyle#

The hybrid lifestyle is tied with the vampire's for the most expensive in the mod, and it buys eighteen perks across two trees, Vampirism and Lycanthropy, where the vampire has ten in one and the werewolf eight. Both capstones together grant the two traits the vampire and werewolf capstones grant separately, and the master focus needs both of them. Finishing it takes roughly 27,000 lifestyle experience and two full tracks. Lycanthropy is the slower of the two tracks to fill.

The cure#

Abandon Being a Hybrid works like the werewolf cure and sits behind the same game rule. It takes the trait, the known trait and the secret, ends a Champion's bond with his hide and takes him out of his pack. It leaves all eighteen perks and both capstone traits in place.

The craft

Magic and the people who work it: witches, Mages, warlocks, necromancers, covens and the Order, the items they fight over, and the demons that magic can call up.

MagicMagical artifactsThe MageMagical ResearchThe Order of the Blue RoseThe Gnostic CouncilThe Vade MaecumDemons and House Hellion

The craftMagic#

The magic in Supernatural is Rycie's Witchcraft mod, shipped inside Supernatural in full, with Supernatural's own layer added on top of it: a hunter who can walk that world, a witch hunt that can find a coven, and a set of fixes where the two meet. That is why the witch layer is so much larger than everything else here, and why so much of it behaves differently from the creatures.

Because Witchcraft ships inside Supernatural, running both at once is the one incompatibility that matters. See what not to run alongside it.

WitchTrue WitchMageLegendary MageWarlockSourceYagaGrand Master

What a witch is#

Unlike a vampire or a werewolf, a witch has no single trait with a track that counts up. Magic is spread across a family of traits, and you climb through the lifestyle rather than through the trait:

TraitWhat it is
WitchThe base trait. −1 diplomacy, +1 intrigue, +1 learning, +10% magic lifestyle experience, +20 opinion from other witches. It is the thing every faith doctrine, every scheme and every gate keys off.
True WitchThe Witchery tree's capstone trait. +3 intrigue, +10 baseline dread, half again on diplomatic range, and it unlocks the Search for Magical Artifacts. It can never sit on a hunter.
MageThe Arcane capstone, renamed Legendary Mage at a full Secret Knowledge track. It has five tracks of its own, and almost everything a mage can do runs through them. The Mage and its five tracks.
WarlockThe Black Magic capstone. +3 intrigue, +10 baseline dread, +1.5 health, −5% army upkeep, −5 opinion from everyone.
SourceGenetic magical potential, and the only inheritable one that matters, and one child in a hundred is born with it. It has a Magic Potential track that pays life expectancy and fertility, and from 20 restores some stored power once a year, and an Instability track that charges for it in stress and health.
YagaBaba Yaga's own trait: immortal, +10 health, +40% magic lifestyle experience, +50 baseline dread. It can only be taken while no Yaga is alive and you hold her hut: by a True Witch who has visited it, with Claim the Yaga title, or by a mage who breaks in on a Research Expedition.

There is also a female-line version of Source called Yaga's Blood, which passes from mother to daughter at a 90 per cent chance, and a Grand Master of the Coven trait with an Authority track.

Becoming one#

The route is a scheme, Convert to Witchcraft, run by a witch on a target who does not already have the trait. It is secret, it takes a while, and its success turns on the target's opinion of you, their zeal and your learning. A zealous target resists, unless their rite both reveres witches and accepts witchcraft, which turns zeal in your favour. Attraction helps, up to +30. Being a coven house is worth +20. Mystics, revelers, herbalists, seducers and the excommunicated are all easier marks.

Embrace Witchcraft is the other road, new in 2.39, and it needs the Supernatural: Embrace Decisions rule set to On. It is free, has no cooldown, and makes an adult a witch at once: the Witch trait, or the witch secret if witches are shunned or criminal in your rite or your liege's. A vampire, werewolf or hybrid may take it; a hunter or a witch hunter may not. There is no decision to stop being a witch, and the AI never takes it.

Witches do not inherit the trait. What inherits is Source, Yaga's Blood and possession. The mod's history makes only two witches, both Mikaelsons: Esther and Freja. The magic history file holds one character, a demon who dies in 809, before the earliest bookmark. Every other witch you meet in a campaign was made at runtime.

Magical artifacts#

Magic in this mod is something you can hold, lose and have stolen. This subsection covers every magic item: what counts as one, where they come from, the named ones, relics and the reliquary, lending, and who will come after yours. The six hides are not magic items; they have their own section.

What counts as a magic item#

Two lists in the files matter. Witchcraft's magical artifacts are ten items, and they are what most of Witchcraft's own rules count: the eight-item limit on the Search, the AI thieves and the mage duel. The mod's wider magic items are those ten plus the eleven relics, the four pages of the Vade Maecum and the bound book: 26 kinds. Pledging, Seize Their Relics, Zecchia and the 2.39 AI read the wider list.

Magical artifactHow it enters the world
Ancient Singularity (illustrious)The yearly pulse. Magical Research can wake it, or improve it into a Mk.II
Ring Of EverlastingThe yearly pulse, to a ruler; Merlin joins the finder's court
Black GrimoireThe yearly pulse
DarkbladeThe yearly pulse
Crown of ShadowsThe yearly pulse; its coven appears the first time it is made
Orb of TruthThe yearly pulse
Magic CatalystThe yearly pulse
DragonscalesThe yearly pulse
Hell's ScepterThe yearly pulse
Enchanted RingOnly from Magical Research, the Magic Ring project

DarkbladeCrown of ShadowsBlack GrimoireDragonscalesRingAncient SingularityMagic CatalystOrb of TruthHell's ScepterAmulet of ProtectionBaba Yaga's broomMerlin's wandWitch hunter's crossbow

The yearly pulse. Every year, while any of the first nine is missing from the world, one of the missing ones appears, chosen at random. The Witchcraft: Magical Artifacts rule decides where: anywhere (Random Distribution, the default), within a player's reach (Player's Area), or the first one to a player (Guaranteed for the player, which blocks achievements). The finder is a ruler of count rank or higher, or a landless adventurer, and one time in four a grown courtier of his instead. A dynasty finds one only once.

Other ways in. The Search for Magical Artifacts tells you who owns one and can give you a claim. Imprisoning a holder can win you his artifact with the Arcana legacy's first perk. After that it is taking: the yearly thief, theft and artifact wars, Zecchia, Seize Their Relics, and the endgame wars, which take every magical artifact, relic and page the loser holds (but not a bound book). Potion experiments make potions, not magic items.

Artifacts that are not magic items. The eleven potions, the Amulet of Protection, Yaga's Broom, Merlin's Staff, the witch hunter's Advanced Crossbow and the Ancient Scroll are artifacts, but no rule counts them as magic items. Three sit in between. The Ring Of Death and both versions of the Ancient Singularity Mk.II open the Arcane focus, and the Ring Of Death guards against curses as the Ring Of Everlasting does. The Mk.II you get by improving your own Singularity also counts toward the Search's eight and stands in for the Singularity, so a new one does not appear; Abdul Al-Hazred's does neither. None of the three counts for pledging, Seize Their Relics, Zecchia or the 2.39 AI. All ten magical artifacts are unique and cannot be reforged. Only the Darkblade, the Crown of Shadows and Merlin's Staff change their look by culture.

Search for Magical Artifacts#

The True Witch's activity, for a ruler of count rank or higher. Six months at your capital spent finding out where the world's magical artifacts are, and making a case for one of them. It is held at your capital, costs the game's activity price plus 100 gold, and runs every twelve years. You must hold fewer than eight magical artifacts. Increased Funding costs another 300 gold for a faster start.

At the start the game draws up to three magical artifacts you do not own, from anywhere in the world. A few weeks in, Large-scale Search asks how you look: your spies, your books, your contacts or your coven. A little later, Target Selection asks which of the three trails to follow; the first is the easiest. Every month an event moves your progress up or down: paying an informant, putting your spymaster on it, giving up your familiar, a stranger who knows too much, a rival's interference.

Progress at the endResult
Under 50Fruitless Search. Stress.
50 to 74Successful Search. You learn who owns your chosen artifact, and you remember it.
75 to 99Very Successful Search. As above, and a personal claim on it, but only if a hidden 40% roll during the Large-scale Search went your way.
100 or moreFlawless Search. A personal claim on it, every time.

The Search never hands you the artifact. The best it gives is a claim; taking the thing is up to you. Since 2.38 it can hand you a lesser relic: each month on site there is a 10% chance of A Lesser Thing, once a search. Relics do not count toward the eight, and since 2.39 the relic can be the Warded Chains. The artifacts it can find are the Ancient Singularity, the Ring Of Everlasting, the Black Grimoire, the Darkblade, the Crown of Shadows, the Orb of Truth, the Magic Catalyst, the Dragonscales, Hell's Scepter and the Enchanted Ring. A Singularity Mk.II made by improving your own can be found; the one Abdul Al-Hazred carries cannot.

Relics#

The relics are lesser magical items: seven and the Seal of Solomon from 2.38, since 2.39 the Homunculus Beetle and the Warded Chains, and since 2.40 the Fors Factorum, eleven in all. Each sits in the trinket slot, does one thing and never decays. None is one of Witchcraft's magical artifacts, so a relic does not open the Arcane focus or meet Witchcraft's other needs for a magic item, and relics do not count toward the eight a Search for Magical Artifacts allows. The 2.38 relics need to be worn, and your own: a borrowed Glove, Necrophone or Mirror does not work, and the Powder cannot be borrowed. A borrowed Blade, Bell or Pendant works for whoever wears it; the Seal cannot be pledged, so it is never lent. The Warded Chains work for whoever owns them, worn or not, borrowed or pledged; the Beetle is used from your inventory, but not while it is pledged, unless a Magistratus or higher has borrowed it. The Fors Factorum opens its spell to whoever holds it, and to every member of a temple whose Reliquary keeps it.

RelicWornUse
The Glove (famed)+2 intrigueQuestion Under the Glove, on your own prisoner, every two years. He chooses: I will tell you the truth (you learn one of his secrets, or one he knows about someone else), I will lie (he is burned, wounded but never killed, and you gain a hook on him; he hates you for it), or, for a werewolf, Endure it. One time in four word gets out: his liege thinks less of you, and your county gets a witch mark
The Necrophone (famed)+2 learningCall the Dead, once a year. Ask where a page of the Vade Maecum is, where the lost hide is, or where the Seal of Solomon is kept. The third call breaks it
The Enchanted Blade (masterwork)+4 prowessOn a hunt against a werewolf or hybrid it counts as silver (+15). It is the one thing that lets a hunter win against a man Bound to the Book
The Echo Pendant (masterwork)Immunity to ordinary cursesNone
Obfuscation Powder (common)NothingThrow the Powder: the trail on the county you stand in goes cold, and the powder is used up. Since 2.40 a Powder you carry also throws itself when you leave a mark on the county you stand in (The Powder Did Its Work), and it can pay for Respondeo or a road in Send for a Hide. Only the Order makes it, with Prepare the Powder
The Mirror Shard (famed)NothingBind Our Fates, with someone who agrees (an AI only for a lover, a best friend, or a vassal who adores you), every ten years. For ten years, if either of you is killed, the other dies the next day. A natural death only ends the bond. Break the Mirror ends it and destroys the shard
The Bell of the Dead Hour (masterwork)+10% dread gainWhen a hunter comes for you in the county you are in, +40 to the chance you are warned
The Seal of Solomon (illustrious)+2 learning, +10% piety, cannot be magically dominatedSee below
The Homunculus Beetle (famed, 2.39)+5 dreadStarts a plague. See the Beetle
The Warded Chains (masterwork, 2.39)NothingA wolf held by their owner cannot take his other shape or use Break the Chains. See the Chains
The Fors Factorum (famed, 2.40)+1 learningThe founders' letter: it opens Cast the Fors Factorum to its holder and to his temple. Never lent, and Zecchia never takes it

Where they come from. With the Order on, the relics wait for the temples. Once six temples stand, or ten years into the game, each temple is looked at on its next 1 January: if its Reliquary is empty, it gets a relic for its head and one to three for its members, all pledged, and a player Temple Magus is told Your Temple's Reliquary Has Its First Relics. A temple founded later is looked at the same way. Before 2.40 the relics were handed out when the Order first seated its temples, often before any stood, and never again, so most Reliquaries stayed empty; a running game fills its empty ones at the first new year under 2.40. At the same time four more go to AI characters of learning who are not hunters: two to rulers of learning 12 or more (landless adventurers included) and two to AI courtiers of learning 14 or more. Each of these is drawn from seven at equal odds: the Glove, the Necrophone, the Blade, the Pendant, the Mirror Shard, the Bell and, since 2.39, the Warded Chains. A Search for Magical Artifacts has a 10% chance each month of turning one up (A Lesser Thing), once a search. The rest move by war, theft, Zecchia and hunters, and the Chains can also be made (below). Powder is never seeded, the Seal is never handed out at random, and the Beetle and the Fors Factorum are each placed once, on their own.

The Homunculus Beetle#

New in 2.39. A beetle the length of a thumb, sealed in a stoppered jar, that starts a plague when it gets inside someone. It is a relic, so it can be pledged, stolen, taken in war or by Zecchia. Since 2.40 it can be lent, but only to a Magistratus or higher, who may use it; the AI never borrows it, and a demon's Compact never reaches it. Gifting it just hands over the sealed jar: nothing wakes.

Where it starts. One beetle is placed once in a game. With the Order on, it goes to the Gnostic Council's Keeper of the Reliquary (or, if he has no temple, the most learned Temple Magus), pledged to his temple, once a temple stands or ten years into the game. With the Order off, or no temple, it goes to the most learned AI ruler who could hold a page. An older save gets it in its first year under 2.39.

InteractionMenuOn whomHow it goes
Swallow the BeetleSupernatural (yourself)YouYou carry it. No roll
Slip the BeetleHostileSomeone at your court, a guest, your spouse, or anyone in the same place, but not your prisonerA hidden roll two to five days later: 40, plus 3 for every point your intrigue beats theirs, between 10 and 85. If it fails they know you tried to poison them, nobody dies, and you keep the beetle
Feed It to the PrisonerHostileYour own prisonerNo roll

All three need an adult who holds the beetle, unpledged and not on loan, or a Magistratus or higher who borrowed it, while no beetle plague is already running. When a borrowed beetle is loosed, the loan ends, the lender is told (The Lent Beetle Is Loose) and so is the lending temple's Temple Magus. A Temple Magus can Take the Beetle into Custody from a member who keeps it pledged to his temple and has not lent it: it passes to him, still pledged. A member of the Order who looses it breaks the Order's rules and loses 20 standing (for Slip the Beetle, only if it works). A non-member gets Loosed the Beetle (−30, fading over ten years) from the Grand Magus and from each Temple Magus he already knows to be one, so it never gives a member away. An AI Order member never uses it; another AI uses it only if sadistic or callous, and only on a rival at its own court or in its own cell.

The plague. Three to ten days later, The Beetle Wakes: a major outbreak of the Blue Plague starts where the carrier is, and he is its first victim. It never starts on its own, reaches at most 30 to 45 provinces, and gives Blue Fever (−1 diplomacy, stewardship, intrigue and learning, half prowess, −3 health, −10 attraction), which can kill; a survivor recovers after 45 to 120 days (The Blue Fever Passes). With epidemics turned off in the game rules there is no outbreak: the carrier and up to three courtiers with him catch the fever instead.

The Order's answer. Every player Temple Magus, and a player master of the Order, gets The Blue Plague: Contain it. (10 power; the plague spreads a quarter as fast for up to five years, and the outbreak's county is quarantined for a year), Find out who carries it., or It is not the Order's affair. If no player contains it within 90 days, an AI Temple Magus does. The Elemental Transference cures the fever, and on the carrier it draws the beetle out.

It comes back. When the plague ends, the carrier dies (It Crawled Out of the Body) or the Transference draws it out, the beetle comes back in its jar, one beetle however many of these happen at once: to the member who contained or drew it out, pledged to his temple; otherwise to the carrier, the lord whose court he is at, or his heir. If it is not pledged, every other Temple Magus gains a claim on it.

The Warded Chains#

New in 2.39: iron chains worked over by a Mage until the links carry a warding. They hold a wolf, a werewolf or hybrid; to a vampire they are only chains. They work while the prisoner's gaoler owns them, worn or not, so a pledged or borrowed pair counts. While they hold, the wolf cannot shapeshift into either wolf form (going back to human is still allowed), cannot Break the Chains, cannot be freed by a compulsion (the AI's Leave Prison, or Release Them through a Compelled gaoler), and a hybrid cannot use Release Me. A wolf already in a wolf form when he is jailed by someone who owns them is made to put it down. Vanilla's escape and ransom still work, so a Champion is hard to keep, not impossible.

How to get themWhoCooldownCost
Ward the Chains (decision)A Mage, a True Witch, or a Magistratus or higher of the Order, who can spend 10 power5 yearsSome gold and 10 power. Anyone nearby who can hear magic hears it
Commission Warded Chains (interaction, Supernatural menu)Anyone, on a character with the Mage or True Witch trait5 years150 gold to the Mage, who spends 10 of his own power. He agrees by his opinion of you (base 10, +20 if greedy, −50 if you burned out a hall of Mages in the last two years)
Found or takenAnyoneThey are one of the seven random relics, so they can be seeded at the start, turned up by A Lesser Thing, or move by gift, loan, war and theft

A True Witch who is not a Mage can only ward chains while she is Power-Saturated, and the same goes for a Magistratus who is not a Mage, so in practice they come from Mages. The AI wards or buys a pair when it holds a prisoner it knows to be a wolf, when a wolf was seen breaking out of its cells, or when it is a hunter.

The reliquary#

A temple's or coven's reliquary is everything its members have pledged to it. Pledge to the Reliquary one of your own relics or magical artifacts (not a hide, a page, the book or the Seal); a member of both a temple and a coven chooses which. Since 2.40 the pick lists of Pledge, Take It Back, Ask to Borrow and Borrow from the Reliquary show only the items you can choose, and when there is none the button says why. The item stays yours, and while it is pledged it cannot be taken in a siege or a raid. War, Zecchia and a hunter's evidence can still take it. Take It Back unpledges it (below). The Homunculus Beetle and the Warded Chains can be pledged; each counts 2 points.

Every member gets The Reliquary (a temple) or The Coven's Reliquary: +1% monthly magic lifestyle experience for each point, at 2 points for a relic and 3 for a magical artifact. A temple's bonus stops at +30%, a coven's at +15%.

Lending, and Take It Back#

Ask to Borrow lets a Medicum or higher borrow an item pledged to his own temple (a Magistratus before 2.40; an Acolyte sees it greyed, with the reason), once in five years; one of the Adepti can ask any member he knows. Before 2.40 the window opened on the first item the lender owned, often a hide or the Beetle that could not be picked, so the button stayed blocked: borrowing never worked once members held more than one item. An AI lender weighs his opinion and the borrower's rank, and always obeys his own Temple Magus. A loan lasts three years, the lender keeps a claim, and it comes home early if the borrower dies, is cast out, leaves the Order or breaks a demon's Compact. An item can be lent once in three years. The Obfuscation Powder and the Fors Factorum are never lent, and the Homunculus Beetle only to a Magistratus or higher (the Beetle). A demon with a Compact can also Borrow from the Reliquary, never the Beetle, and a Magistratus can borrow from another temple at a Conclave, by the same rules.

A loaned item that is taken by war, theft or Zecchia ends both the loan and the pledge. If the lender dies, it goes to his heir, and since 2.40 it stays pledged when that heir is of the same temple; since 2.39, if the lender is gone from the world altogether, the loan ends and the item is free.

Pledges that last (2.40). A member's pledged items stay pledged when they pass to an heir of the same temple or coven, and a Temple Magus who founds or moves to a new temple takes his pledges with him. Before, any change of owner unpledged an item, which slowly drained the Reliquaries. Every Reliquary list is rebuilt once when a running game loads 2.40.

Take It Back takes back one of your own pledged items that is not out on loan. Since 2.39 it also finds an item pledged to a temple that no longer exists, which before could stay locked for good. A pledged item that leaves you by war or theft breaks the pledge (Your Pledge Is Broken). Throwing a Powder, or spending one at a sister's trial or for Respondeo, updates the Reliquary at once.

The Seal of Solomon#

A brass ring cut with the seal of the king who bound demons. It is a holy relic to every Abrahamic faith, and starts enshrined in Jerusalem if an Abrahamic ruler holds it and the shrine has room, else at another Abrahamic holy site that fits, else in the treasury of the greatest Abrahamic AI ruler, else it is lost for 10, 20 or 30 years. Its county carries The Seal of Solomon Is Kept Here (+2 county opinion). A Seal that is destroyed is never made again.

Worn, it stops magical domination, and since 2.39 that includes Compel Mortal: a vampire cannot compel its wearer, not even a gaoler asked to unlock a cell. Zecchia cannot be sent after its wearer or rob him, and a wearer who knows her name can summon her without being a Mage or spending power. The Order's master, wearing it, can make a demon kneel. It cannot be pledged, and Zecchia never takes it.

To take it from its shrine: hold the county; or, as a Mage or someone who knows where it is, finish a siege of that county (The Ring Beneath the Altar); or go there on pilgrimage knowing it is there (The Ring at the Shrine: an intrigue roll, 3 for each point plus 10, between 10 and 80; caught, the county's lord thinks 30 less of you). The Necrophone, Zecchia and the book can each tell you where it is.

Seize Their Relics#

A casus belli from 2.38. A landed Mage, Order member or coven member (not a landless adventurer) can declare it on anyone holding at least three magical items that can move: magical artifacts, relics, pages, or the book while no Contract runs. Since 2.40 a single page of the Vade Maecum, or the book, is reason enough. Hides never count. A player may declare at any distance; the AI only against neighbours, and only when it is a greedy or ambitious Mage. Since 2.39 the Order's enemies can declare it too: anyone on the road to breaking the Order (a hunter, a witch hunter, the head of a faith that condemns witchcraft and the rest), but only against a member whose Blue Rose secret he knows.

Victory takes every relic and magical item the defender owns, pledged or not, and leaves him a claim on each. A white peace costs the attacker a little prestige. Defeat costs him gold and prestige, but nobody loses a claim over it. Witchcraft's Demonic Invasion and War Against the Witch now take relics and pages too.

Who comes after your items, and why#

Before 2.39, Witchcraft's AI went after magic items on power or chance alone, sometimes from the other side of the world. Now every AI road to your items adds up one score of reasons and acts only at 30 or more, and only against someone in its diplomatic range.

ReasonScore
You are its nemesis, or else its rival+60, or +40
At war with it+30
It thinks badly of you, and very badly+20, and +20 more
It has a claim on one of your magic items+30
Greedy, ambitious, arrogant, deceitful+25, +15, +10, +10
Vengeful, against a rival or nemesis+10
Just, content, honest, compassionate, generous−25, −25, −15, −10, −10
A Warlock or Necromancer+25
A landless adventurer+15
It already holds three or more magic items: greedy or ambitious, or otherwise+15, or −10
It holds none+10
You are its liege or its vassal−20
You are both of the Order, with no rivalry, nemesis or war between you× ¼
It is a witch who is not a Mage× 1½
You are its friend, lover, spouse or family, or it likes you (40 or more)Never

So a rival alone (40) is enough, greed with nothing in hand (25 + 10) is enough, and greed with a just conscience (25 − 25 + 10) is not. The roads that read it:

  • The yearly thief (Path To True Power): an AI witch or Mage with intrigue over 12 picks the holder it wants most and steals, or goes to war. Since 2.39 a just, content or compassionate thief is far less likely to choose war.
  • Striving for power and the artifact seeker, the AI True Witch's and Mage's own hunts: claims, thieves, abduction, purchase, a duel, war, or Domination. They dominate a player only if he is their rival, their nemesis or at war with them. The seeker's Domination now hands the item over two weeks later; before 2.39 the owner was dominated and kept it.
  • The mage duel needs a reason as well (the AI's reasons).
  • Zecchia. An AI Mage can hire her for an item he has a claim on, for a page of the Vade Maecum whose holder he knows, or against a rival with something to take, and only if he is vengeful or greedy or wants that item. She takes the wanted item first. The victim is told only "It was taken in the night. Nobody saw who took it." and gets no claim on it, because the claim message would have named the hirer.

Seize Their Relics keeps its own rule: only a greedy or ambitious AI Mage, and only against a neighbour.

The lifestyle and its three trees#

The Magic lifestyle is tied with the Demonic for the cheapest in the mod, and it holds three trees, Witchery, Arcane and Black Magic, where the vampire, werewolf and demon lifestyles hold one each and the hunter and hybrid two. Twenty-one perks across the three, three capstones, three traits.

They are not equally open, and each has its own focus:

TreeWho can climb itCapstoneWhat the path gives you
Witchery, 8 perksThe first three perks need only the witch trait or the secret. The rest, short of the capstone, need the Witchcraft focus or the True Witch trait, and Mystical Insight needs the focus itself.True WitchAn amulet, a familiar (dog, cat, rat, horse or bird), Use Scrying, an extra nature trait, +20 opinion from admirers, passive magic detection
Arcane, 7 perksThe Arcane focus, or already being a Mage. A plain witch cannot take that focus: she also needs a magic artifact (the Enchanted Ring and an awakened Singularity count), the Source, the Magical Experiments perk, a place of power held while landed, a Gnostic Council grant, Research Cooperation from a summit, a house with the academy, an event that opens it, or, as a True Witch, an academy standing anywhere in the world.MageCounter-spells, Create Potion, Power Strike and Aura Enhancement, Perform Magic Ritual, then the Mage trait
Black Magic, 6 perksThe Black Magic focus, which any witch can take, as can a True Witch, anyone holding a magic artifact or the Source, and anyone with Magical Experiments.WarlockCast a Curse, then Cast Powerful Curse, Domination, a life-extending ritual, and Summon Demon

The Arcane tree's root perk, Magical Experiments, is a gamble. Four times in five it is +1 learning. The other fifth it leaves you disfigured, wounded, one-eyed or one-legged, and an injury you already carry drops out of the roll, so the odds tilt further toward learning. Do not take it if an event has already made you a Mage: for anyone but the mod's special characters it removes the Mage trait.

A hunter cannot take any of the three capstones. That block is explicit and it shows you why in the tooltip. Before it existed, a hunter could buy the perk, the trait would silently fail to attach, and the perk point was gone.

One capstone. Since 2.38 a character holds one of the three. The Mage, Warlock and True Witch perks each refuse anyone who already has another of the capstone traits, with the tooltip You have already finished another path. A practitioner can finish only one of the three. Since 2.39a the perk before each capstone (The Ritual before the Mage, Summoner before the Warlock, Magical Senses before the True Witch) warns that the next perk closes the other two paths, and once you hold one path, the first perk of each other tree tells you its last perk is closed to you (a False Mage is the exception; see Known issues). The other perks of all three trees stay open. Characters who held two or three before 2.38 keep them. Witchcraft's own ways of handing out a capstone (an AI taught sorcery, the Magic Catalyst, Enchanted Blood, a mythical legend, and the events that make Mages and True Witches) pass over anyone already on another path, and Travel To Another World teaches such a character teleportation instead. Each capstone clears your focus when you take it. Every perk in all three trees, with its numbers, is in the perk reference.

The Magic lifestyle is open to anyone with the Mage or True Witch trait as well as to witches. The AI's nature now weighs on its focus, on top of Witchcraft's own leanings: Arcane suits the just, compassionate, honest, patient, diligent and ambitious; Black Magic the arbitrary, vengeful, wrathful, deceitful and callous; Witchcraft the content, gregarious and trusting. One fitting trait doubles the weight and two or more make it four times; an opposed trait halves it. A player chooses freely.

The Mage and its five tracks#

The Mage trait comes from the Arcane tree's last perk, or since 2.39 from Embrace the Mage, a decision behind the Supernatural: Embrace Decisions rule. It is free and has no cooldown, and it is closed to a Warlock or True Witch (You have already finished another path. A practitioner can finish only one of the three.). It makes you a witch if you were not one, gives the whole Arcane tree plus Mind Control, Fate Weaver and Summoner from Black Magic, and clears your focus. The first experiments can go wrong: one time in five they leave a lasting injury (disfigured, wounded, one eye or one leg). The AI never takes it. On its own the trait is modest: +1 learning, +1 intrigue, half a prestige a month, and the Teach Sorcery and Mage Duel interactions. What makes it the strongest thing in the craft is its five tracks, which fill as you use magic and unlock most of what a mage can do. At 100 Secret Knowledge the trait renames itself Legendary Mage.

Most mage content checks for a true mage: someone with the trait who is not secretly a False Mage.

TrackHow it fillsWhat each rung gives
Secret Knowledge+1 for casting any of fifteen spells, and from research, summits, triangulation and duels.25: +3 learning, and with the Counter-spells perk you can spend power to counter a spell. 50: +3 learning, +5 power from every standard gain, and Magic Triangulation shows the artifact you choose. 75: +3 learning, +10 hostile scheme success, Cast the Ultimate Spell. 100: +3 learning, your spells cannot be detected, a legend seed of your own, and the Legendary Mage name. Each rung also adds +2 starting progress to Magical Research.
Combat Magic+1 per Power Strike, Fire Storm or Tornado, and from duels, Esoteric Training and research.35: +5 advantage, +25% fatal casualties for the enemy, and one free Magic Shield in every duel. 65: +5 advantage, −25% siege phase time, the Shadow element. 100: +5 advantage, +20 travel safety, the Fire element.
Demonology4 per gain, 6 while you hold Houska Castle. Summoning, events, research.35: summoning no longer costs stress, and a binding goes wrong 3 times in 100 instead of 30. 65: +2 diplomacy, +2 learning. 100: the Banish Demon spell, and you can summon demons by type or by name.
Alchemy4 per gain. Potions, events, research.30: +1 gold a month, +0.5 health-penalty negation, Create Potion at half price. 70: +3 gold a month, +5 life expectancy, Create Potion every three years instead of four. 100: +5 life expectancy, the Magic Transmutation Spell, and you choose what Create Potion brews.
Power SaturationThe mage's store of power. The only track that falls as well as rises.10: +0.2 health, +1 Power, Fire and Kinetic in duels. 50: +1 Power and Fire, +5 travel safety. 100: +1 Power, +5 prowess, +5 advantage.

Power does not refill on its own. There is no monthly trickle. It comes from acts: Perform Magic Ritual (the Arcane tree's The Ritual perk, every five months), Mana Siphon, Consume, Feed the Darkblade, a Magic potion, and dozens of events. A standard gain is 10, plus 5 at Secret Knowledge 50, plus 5 for each kind of place of power you hold (15 at most), plus 5 or 10 at high Magic Potential, and more for a Greedy for Power mage, an equipped catalyst and a few others. It stops at 100. The Source is the one exception: from Magic Potential 20 it restores some power once a year. Spells cost 5, 10 or 20, and you need at least 10 to cast a standard spell and 20 for a large one. Someone who is not a mage stores power as a modifier instead of a track.

The False Mage#

Some characters carry the Mage trait without the power: a secret, False Mage, says they are pretending. A False Mage shows the trait but fails the true-mage check that most mage content uses: no Magical Research, no Teach Sorcery, no research spells, no mage contracts. A few things look only at the trait, among them Mage Duel and a Gnostic Council appointment. A player can choose to become one when an event offers it; one True Witch in ten is offered it six months to three years after the capstone; and two in ten court mages the game generates are frauds. Exposure strips the Mage trait, costs a prestige level and five years of Liar Liar, and a liege who employed them as court mage holds it against them for ten years. Taking the Magical Experiments perk ends the pretence the other way: the secret lapses, and the perk takes the Mage trait away with it.

Spells#

There are 54 magic interactions, most of them spells, and almost none costs gold, piety or prestige: Recall from the Gnostic Council can cost 5,000 prestige, and a refused Mana Siphon or cyrograph costs prestige. The price of a spell is its cooldown, its stress, its power, and the risk of what the target does with it.

Since 2.38, 27 of them, the friendly and diplomatic ones, sit in the Supernatural menu beside the mod's own interactions. Curses and attacks stay under Hostile, and the witch trial under Prison. Witchcraft's marker on anything that counts as black magic, which read Use Black Magic (Black Grimoire) as if the Grimoire were needed, reads This is black magic since 2.40.

Three things can happen to a spell you cast. It can be countered, by a mage who took Counter-spells, or by a court mage protecting his liege's household. It can be stopped outright by an antimagic seal. Or, if the target is a Mage, a demon or Yaga, it can be deflected into a mage duel, which is a different event entirely and not one you chose to start. You also cannot cast while you are already duelling.

The spells themselves run from a curse and a domination at the low end through healing, power strikes, aura enhancement, mind scanning and shadow steps, up to plague, firestorm, tornado, earthquake and teleportation at the far end of the mage tracks. Cooldowns vary widely and are the main limit: Power Strike is a day, Domination is a week, Hypercognition is eight years, and an Earthquake cannot be aimed at the same person twice inside twenty.

A mage learns spells four ways. The Learn new spell decision needs a true mage holding a county with the Academy of Magic, runs every fifteen years, gives +2 Secret Knowledge, and draws one spell you do not know: Bless the Land, Healing, Shadow Step, Plague, Hypercognition, Aura Reading and Thorn Walls are equally likely, Teleportation a little less, Fire Storm rarely. A mage can Teach Spells to another mage every ten years. Magical Research can end in a new spell. And the Arcane tree's Caster perk gives Power Strike and Aura Enhancement outright, and a character who is not yet a Mage also gets the choice of a third spell or another benefit.

Potions#

There is no brewing screen. Potions are artifacts in your inventory with a hidden marker, and there are eleven of them.

You brew with the Create Potion decision, which needs either the witch trait plus Herbalist, or the Advanced Alchemy perk, and, as of 2.34, the hunter tree's Combat Spells perk, which is Supernatural's own addition reaching into the Witchcraft layer. It costs gold by your highest title: 10 for a baron or someone landless, 20 for a count, 40 for a duke, 100 for a king and 150 for an emperor, all halved at Alchemy 30. The cooldown is four years, three at Alchemy 70.

Create Potion is a menu, and most of it is not potions. It offers a Poison, a Love Potion or a Health Elixir, each a three-year modifier on you rather than something in your inventory, and since 2.39 the Draught of a Clear Mind (below). Only a Mage or a True Witch gets I will experiment, which makes one of the potion artifacts seven times in ten; a brewer who is both a herbalist and has Advanced Alchemy never fails it. So a witch-herbalist or a hunter can brew, but never one of the eleven potions. The experiment rolls Regeneration, Magic, Mutagen, Unknown, Youth, Charisma, Might, Learning or Fertility; Mind-Reading only for a Mage or Yaga; and Liquid Aether, a crafting reagent rather than a drink, only for a true mage. At Alchemy 100 a Mage chooses the potion outright.

The Draught of a Clear Mind is open to anyone who can use Create Potion, including the Alchemy-100 Mage's menu, at no cost beyond the brew's. For three years nobody can compel or dominate the drinker or make him forget: Compel Mortal greys out on him, Witchcraft's Domination and mind-control fail, the Order's Pulveris Memoria takes nothing, and what the Order's forgetting would have erased stays known to him.

The player drinks through a self-targeted interaction with a one-day cooldown. The ten separate drink decisions in the files are all AI-only and you will never see them.

Two worth knowing before you drink them. Regeneration clears thirty-eight different afflictions in one go, from leprosy and the pox through blindness, maiming and disfigurement, and it also takes away New Eyes, if you have it. Mutagen is a real gamble: one time in ten you come out Herculean and clean, most of the time you trade fertility for muscle, and one time in twenty it leaves you weak, infertile and disfigured.

The magic activities#

Five activities belong to the craft. Witchcraft's four each gate on a different rung of it, and all four need a title above baron. The fifth, the Order's Conclave, is called by the Order.

ActivityWho can hostCostCooldownGuests
Magical ResearchA true mageThe game's activity price + 100 gold10 years, 5 as the Gnostic Council's Keeper of the Reliquary1, and an assistant
Mage SummitA Mage who is legendary, very prestigious, or whose house holds Magic Traditions1,000 gold, halved up to three times10 yearsUp to 30
Search for Magical ArtifactsA True Witch holding fewer than eight magical artifactsThe game's activity price + 100 gold12 years, the longest in the mod1
Grand RiteA witch whose house is a coven, or a landless adventurer whose camp can host ritesA small sum of gold5 years, halved with a Temple of the Horned GodUp to 30
ConclaveThe Grand Magus, or the Temple Magus the Order asks; at a county seat he holdsThe game's usual price for a gathering5 yearsEvery member of the Order in reach, up to 40

"The game's activity price" is the base game's feast price: 30 gold for a count, 60 for a duke, 140 for a king and 170 for an emperor. A tribe halves the first part before a king's or emperor's extra 50, and a culture that makes feasts dearer doubles it. For most rulers Magical Research and the Search each cost 130, 160, 240 or 270 gold before any options.

Magical Research#

Magical Research is how a mage gets stronger on purpose. You pick a project, work at it for five months, and if you collect enough breakthroughs you are rewarded with what the project promised: a new spell, a permanent power, a regiment, an artifact, or years of life. It is open to a true mage who holds at least a county and is an adult. A Mage on the Gnostic Council's Keeper of the Reliquary seat can run it every five years instead of ten, and a reminder appears in your alerts when it is ready.

There are two kinds. Magical Research happens where you are standing. A Research Expedition travels to one of the world's three places of power, and plays out as a story at that place instead of a project; see Research Expeditions. The budget and the three specialist intents are for the first kind only.

Research BudgetExtra costStarting progressNeeds
Economy VariantNoneNone—
Increased Funding100 gold+5—
Exhaustive Funding200 gold+10Ambitious, or any learning education

Your intent decides which projects you are offered, which events can find you, and what kind of experience the good outcomes pay:

IntentProjects aboutExperience it pays
General ResearchSpells, the mind, other worlds, necromancy, nature, the Ultimate SpellSecret Knowledge
Life ExtensionLongevity, the body, the soulAlchemy and Demonology
Combat MagicWar spells and cursesCombat Magic
Creation and ArtifactsGolems, transmutation, rings, the Ancient Singularity, fortificationsAlchemy, or Secret Knowledge once Alchemy is full

An expedition can only be General Research. You can take one guest, but nothing in the activity rewards the guest except an evening with the books.

How a research runs#

  1. Extra Support. On arrival you choose an assistant, or work alone for a little prestige. The candidates are witches you trust: a friend or soulmate, your heir, a witch you hold a hook on, a mage courtier, another player. The assistant does not travel or attend; they simply help. They learn that you are a witch, take Assisted in Magical Research for twenty years (+1 learning, +10% magic experience), think better of you, and gain Secret Knowledge if they are a mage below 100, or a permanent +1 learning if not. How much they add to your work depends on how good they are: +5 from a mage past 75 Secret Knowledge, +3 past 25, +2 from any other mage, +1 from a True Witch, a demon or an Erudite, nothing from anyone else.
  2. Research Focus. Two days later you pick the project. You may change your mind once, which costs eleven days.
  3. Five months of work. Each month one event from the research pool finds you, plus one more scheduled at the start. On top of that, any night there is a chance that someone spends it on the books: a little stress, and +1 progress.
  4. The verdict. At five months the game counts your progress against what the project needed.
  5. Calibration. Four projects play out a follow-up story here: Time Travel, Design Combat Spell, Artificial Human and Magic Ring. If you finished far ahead, Excess Breakthroughs comes next.

Projects and breakthroughs#

Every project needs one to four breakthroughs, and a breakthrough is 25 progress, so a four-breakthrough project needs 100. You are offered up to three projects drawn at random from your intent's pool, plus that intent's basic project, which is always there. The third random project is only offered if you have the Erudite perk from the base game's learning tree. A basic project can be repeated; an advanced one can be completed once per character; a unique one once in the whole world.

Combat Magic (each needs one breakthrough fewer for the Gnostic Council's Adeptus Major)

Since 2.38 an AI chooses necromancy by its nature. Unless it is sadistic, callous, vengeful, lunatic or possessed, the Necromancy and Lich projects, the Undead sounds good. answer and Cheat Death weigh a tenth of what they did. The cut does not apply to someone already on that road: a Necromancer, and for the project and the answer a Warlock too (and for the answer, the Corrupted).

Where progress comes from#

You start with some progress before the first day of work, and the right setup can be worth a whole breakthrough:

Starting progress fromWorth
Research Budget+5 or +10
Secret Knowledge past 25 / 50 / 75 / 100+2 / +4 / +6 / +8
A mage tower: Mage's Study / Laboratory / Spire, or an estate tower of level 2 / 3 / 4+3 / +4 / +5 (a level 1 estate tower +2)
Accursed Tomes in your estate, level 1 / 2+1 / +2
The Magic Theorists Arcana legacy+5
Learning from Mistakes (a failed research), or having visited the Academy of Magic+5 each
An equipped Ancient Scroll+5
Holding the Academy of Magic; the Cult Archives in your camp; the Black Grimoire; the Magic Catalyst; Seasoned Scholar; Hypercognition+3 each
Ancient Tomes, Research Cooperation, Rector of the Magic Academy+1 each
Distracted−5

During the five months, progress comes from your assistant (up to +5), the nights on the books (+1 each), a Hypercognition cast while you research (+3), and above all the monthly events. Most events offer a safe option worth a little and a better one that costs stress, gold, a duel, your secret or your conscience. The big ones are worth +10 to +20 each.
