Success, failure and Excess Breakthroughs#

Success gives the project's reward and Secret Knowledge by difficulty: +2 for a one-breakthrough project, +3 for two, +5 for three, +10 for four. Failure costs nothing but the time: you get Learning from Mistakes for sixteen years (+1 learning, and +5 starting progress on your next research) and +1 Secret Knowledge. There is no wound, no death and no lost gold for failing.

Beat the target by more than one whole breakthrough, and Excess Breakthroughs asks what to do with the surplus:

  • Use it: more track experience, more again if you are a Legendary Mage.
  • Record it in the chronicles: unlocks Write a Magical Elaborate, a decision that costs gold and runs every five years.
  • Spread the word: a great deal of prestige, and a one-in-two chance that witch hunters take an interest in you for ten years.

What can go wrong

Research is safe unless you choose risk. The one way it can kill you is Risky Experiment's plain option, 2 in 100. Your witch secret gets out if you take Total Commitment's quickest option, or answer the Arcane Research nickname event as the Enchanter. A tortured prisoner and your assistant both learn it, and spreading word of your breakthroughs draws hunters.

Research Expeditions#

An expedition travels to a place of power: the Academy of Magic, the Temple of the Horned God or the Hut On The Chicken's Leg. You can only plan one to a place you know about: one you hold, one inside your own independent realm, one an event has told you of (Magic Triangulation, Rumors About Magic, a search's Places of power event, the Grand Magus's answer), or all three if your dynasty has the Knowledge Sharing legacy. Each is meant to be visited once in a lifetime. There is no project and no verdict: the place itself is the story, and it pays out as you go.

  • The Academy of Magic is the safe one. A guide shows you round; the grand library and the deeper halls pay skills and track experience, sometimes Liquid Aether, and one of the rooms you may find is worth +5 learning outright. You leave with Visited the Academy of Magic for good: +2 learning, +5 starting progress on every research after.
  • The Temple of the Horned God is three trials, and the last can kill you. Fail the first and you limp home wounded, with +1 learning to show for it. Pass all three and the rewards are the biggest an expedition gives: track experience across the board, Source potential, and claws or physical and mental gifts. The Horned God's own favour goes only to a Yaga who asks for more, or to a Shrewd answer at the chalice.
  • The Hut On The Chicken's Leg is Baba Yaga's. If she lives and the hut is not yours, you may be taken in by Sefia and learn from her (once per dynasty), and Baba Yaga will think much less of you when she hears. If no Yaga lives and you hold the hut's county, you can win a learning duel to break in and become the new Yaga. Either way you leave with Visited the Hut On The Chicken's Leg (+1 intrigue, +5 life expectancy).
Spoilers: the Temple's trials

The first trial is a riddle. The answer is "Fire? You are fire." Any other answer wounds you, and the expedition ends.

The second is a demon guardian. Fighting it always wins, and a true mage can open with Power Strike for Combat Magic experience. Running away ends the expedition.

The third is a chalice and two basins, green and red. Drinking either one alone kills you, every time. Drink both and the Horned God speaks to you; kneel or refuse him, and either way you are rewarded. A Shrewd character has a third answer: fill the chalice with water from his own waterskin. It earns the Horned God's favour. Walking away is always allowed.

Once anyone has won the Horned God's favour, later visitors find the temple open and get a shorter, gentler visit.

Mage Summit#

A Mage Summit gathers the world's magic users for a banquet and then a Council of Mages, where a Speaker puts a motion to a vote. With the Order of the Blue Rose off, it is the only way to found the Gnostic Council, and the way its Grand Magus is elected once it exists. With the Order on, which is the default, it does neither: the Order fills the chair.

To host you need the Mage trait, a title above baron, a landed or landless administrative government, and one of three things: a full Secret Knowledge track, a prestige level of 4 or more, or a house with the Magic Traditions modifier, which comes from completing a mythical magic legend. It costs 1,000 gold, halved for each of: Magic Traditions, the 50% discount from the Recognition event, and the Knowledge Sharing Arcana legacy. At its cheapest a player pays 125. Then food: the poor option is free, the default Normal food adds the scaled feast price plus 100 gold, and Good food more again. The cheapest summit, on poor food, is 125 gold. The Extraordinary security option adds 300 gold and nothing in the mod reads it. The cooldown is ten years.

The summit is held at your capital, your domicile, a place of power, or a Mage's Laboratory or Spire. Up to 30 guests: by default every Mage, Yaga, arcane-focus character or Magical Experiments holder in the world, and every True Witch, wherever they are and without any range limit; you can add court mages and learned witches in range. Anyone excluded from the community as an Outcast is left off. A mage who was missed can invite himself with Join the Mage Summit on anyone already going, once a year, and not through the same person twice inside nine years.

There are three intents: Sharing knowledge (the default), Diplomatic affairs (for rulers) and Show superiority (for mages, demons and Yaga). They decide which events find you and which motions you can bring. A member of the Order also has Seek Kindred Minds, which pays standing when another member he knows is there, and a Medicum or higher Speak for My Circle.

The banquet and the Council of Mages#

The Banquet lasts four weeks, with a weekly event for every attendee: knowledge, perk points and sorcery lessons for the sharers, hooks and supporters for the diplomats, demonstrations and duels for the show-offs, and potions and scrolls for sale. If you have the guards check invitations at the opening, one time in three they catch a witch hunter at the door, which costs the host 100 prestige on the spot.

Then the Council of Mages. Every Mage and True Witch scores for the Speaker's chair (legendary, a Mage, learning 20+, prestige level 3+, the host, a Gnostic Council member, owning a magic artifact or the Source), bids for it on a diplomacy duel, and the highest score speaks. The Speaker brings one motion; Mages, True Witches and arcane-focus characters vote, and a hook on a voter buys his vote. It passes on a simple majority.

MotionSpeaker needsIf it passes
A joint program of magical researchSharing knowledgeEveryone: Research Cooperation for 10 years (+3 learning, +10% lifestyle experience) and Secret Knowledge
A mutual defence pactDiplomatic affairsEvery landed attendee but the Speaker joins a pact: when an outsider attacks one, the rest are asked to join the war, pay 100 gold, or leave the pact at the cost of a prestige level
"We need a Grand Magus - I nominate myself"Show superiority, a Mage, and no Gnostic Council yet. Not offered with the Order onThe Speaker becomes Grand Magus and the Gnostic Council is founded
The Gnostic Council needs fundsA council memberEvery attendee pays a fifth of their gold into the council's funds
Ban black magic—Curses, powerful curses and domination are forbidden to every attendee
Ordinary rulers will become our puppetsSchemerEveryone: Joint spy network for 10 years (+3 intrigue, +10% hostile scheme success)
[Someone] is a threat and must be eliminatedA target: the Speaker's demon or mage rival, an inquisitor, or a ruler or head of faith who outlaws witchcraftThe target gets Death Sentence for 10 years, and every attendee is offered a curse, a murder plot or a duel against them
[A rival mage] should be excludedA Mage rivalThe target becomes an Outcast for 50 years: no more summits, −10 opinion, a prestige level lost
Extraordinary privileges for the Grand MagusThe Grand MagusThe Grand Magus may Demand Submission from witches and mages
Seal the Ancient SingularityAnother attendee holds an awakened SingularityIt is sealed

With the Order off, if the Gnostic Council exists but has no Grand Magus, the summit is an election instead: no Speaker and no motions. The host, any Mages who declare and the two AI attendees best placed are ranked, and the top four stand, so a declared Mage can be cut; everyone votes once, and the winner becomes Grand Magus with a great deal of prestige.

The Grand Rite#

The coven's gathering, and the one activity that the base game also has: Supernatural's version replaces it. Three weeks, up to 30 guests, a ceremony on the seventh day. You host it as a witch whose house is a coven, or as a landless adventurer whose camp has the Portable Chapel (see Secret Seekers). It costs a small sum of gold, takes three months to gather, and runs every five years, or every two and a half if you hold a Temple of the Horned God.

It has the strictest guest rule in the mod: you and each guest must know the other is a witch, and the guest must be within your diplomatic range. And once the ceremony is over, every attendee learns every other attendee's witch secret. A Grand Rite is a room full of people who can now ruin each other.

IntentWhoWhat it does
Worship the witch godEveryone, the defaultThe base game's rite rewards; a heresiarch also draws one of eight dark gifts
Seek True PowerPlayersOne of nine outcomes: ancient texts, an amulet, power, a debate that pays a perk point, a rumour of someone's magic artifact, a demon's offer, a sacrifice
Nominate Yourself as Grand MasterThe host, if their house is a covenGather support for the Grand Master election
Shape the Coven's PolicyThe host, as Grand Master, with the Coven Authority traditionChoose the coven's focus
Bring the Coven to the OrderThe host, if he is a Magistratus of the Order and a member of the covenEvery Mage of the coven who could join is sent the Order's note

The ceremony asks how you lead it: read from an old scroll, use some tricks, or improvise, each a skill duel; pay an offering for a sure result; or, as a True Witch, tell them about true magic, which always goes well. A good ceremony earns the respect of every participant; a poor one their disappointment.

Choosing a Grand Master#

Eleven days after the ceremony, if anyone stood, the coven chooses. The score is title rank, plus a point each for being a True Witch or Mage, a heresiarch, the house head, owning a magic artifact and having power to spend, plus three for each round of support gathered at the rite. If the house has no Grand Master the best score wins. If the sitting Grand Master stayed away, he is deposed in his absence. If he came, the winner may challenge him, and he chooses: fight in single combat, which can be fatal, step down for 200 prestige, or, as a player against an AI challenger, refuse at the cost of a prestige level and everyone's regard. The new Grand Master learns the witch secret of every member of the house, and every witch in the house learns his.

The five coven policies
PolicyThe coven gets
Spreading Witchcraft+1 personal scheme, +10% Convert to Witchcraft, but hostile schemes against you +5%
Accumulating Wealth+2 stewardship, +20% tax, −5 general opinion
Expanding influence+20% merit, +20% influence, +10 maximum political scheme success, −5% tax
Security PrioritizationHostile schemes against you −5 and −5 maximum, +10 travel safety, −5% prestige
Seeking of Knowledge+2 learning, +10% lifestyle experience, +5% stress gain

Changing a policy you have already set costs 500 prestige. Keeping the current one earns Authority.

Two things at a Grand Rite can cost you your life or your soul

A demon may appear and offer Gift From Hell (+3 intrigue, +1 stewardship, health) for your soul, which it comes to collect eighteen to twenty years later: die, bargain, or fight a greater demon to the death. You can sell another attendee's soul instead. And a Sacrifice kills one attendee for a great deal of power; the coven may find out who did it.

Covens#

A coven is three things at once: a house modifier, a Grand Master at its head, and a culture tradition that gives the coven authority in the realm. The house modifier alone is worth health, fertility and magic experience to every member; the tradition unlocks a cultists regiment, free disinheritance of non-witches, extra benefits on the witch traits, and the Grand Master's own policy intent at the Rite.

You found one with the base game's decision, Found Witch Coven, which Supernatural replaces with an easier one in 2.38: you must be a witch and your house's head, and you need two other adult witches of your house whom you know to be witches, whatever the house's size. The base game wanted four, or three in five adults of a larger house. The AI founds covens too since 2.38, rarely: mostly an ambitious witch, one past fifty, or the head of a house with four or more known witches, never a content one, checked every five years, and never while the world already holds one coven for every 150 counties. The world also makes covens of its own: the Baba Yaga family is one, and a second appears the first time somebody creates the Crown of Shadows. Otherwise you join one by birth or marriage, and rise to lead it at a Grand Rite.

The coven's panel. Every coven shows in the Situations window as Coven, owned by its Grand Master, or by the house head if it has none. It lists the members (every adult of the house the owner knows to be a witch), the coven's reliquary and the decisions below, and is redone each year. Other player members see a copy, and a button on the left of the screen, Your Coven, opens the Situations window on your coven alone, with Show All Situations for the rest. Items pledged to the coven give every member The Coven's Reliquary, up to +15% magic lifestyle experience.

  • Leave the Coven: you are no longer counted among its witches, and what you pledged comes back to you.
  • Disband the Coven: the owner ends it. The house is no longer a coven, and every pledge goes back to its owner.
  • A coven also ends when its house loses a War Against the Witch, and its panel ends when fewer than two adult witches are left in the house.

Covens and the Order. A witch can belong to a coven and to the Order of the Blue Rose at once. A Grand Rite host who is a Magistratus of the Order can choose Bring the Coven to the Order, and every Mage of the coven who could join is sent a note and a rose. A coven may also ask one of its own what he is (The Sisters Ask What You Are): tell them about the Order and lose some standing, or lie and hope they believe you.

Until 2.35 there was no way for an outsider to find out that a house was a coven: nothing let anyone outside the house detect it. Supernatural's contribution is the sweep: when a witch hunt's trail ends at a coven house, a seasoned hunter (Witch-Hunting 60, or an established name as a hunter) can go through the house, up to five rounds in one night, each round another member of that house who is standing in that county. From the second round on he takes a wound before the door even opens, and losing a round ends the sweep the honest way, with him on the floor and four of them still upstairs. If he stops while the culprit is still free, the county records a withdrawal and it costs him 25 prestige. When the culprit belongs to the Order, he can sweep her temple the same way.

The sweep is one night in one house, not a campaign against a dynasty scattered across three kingdoms. House members who are not in the hunted county are never drawn into it.

The Hermetic Order of the Blue Rose#

A secret society of Mages, new in 2.38. It is organised in temples, each under a Temple Magus, and it keeps three words: Silence, Subservience, Observance. Every year each temple chooses a few people with the gift or the mind for it, and the ones who pass its tests are taught to be Mages. The Gnostic Council is its council.

2.39 adds: the temples can vote out the Grand Magus with Invocare, the council can grant him Imperium and cast the Great Respondeo, a temple can meet beneath a Collegium, outsiders can break the Order or ask to be let in, a Temple Magus can invite people himself, and the chapter has a life of its own (romance and the chapter's moments). Hover the Order's Latin in its events and decisions to read what it means: eleven of its words, from Invocare and Imperium to Medicum, Magistratus and Ex tenebris lux, show a translation the way the base game's own Latin does.

2.40 paces and tidies it. Promotions need three years in a rank, and each temple keeps only a few Magistrati. The masks can run a temple for a player who is busy or would rather not, and a Temple Magus can hand his temple on. Respondeo is cheaper and can be sent. The Grand Magus draws on the council's treasury and decides whether it reveals itself. Teaching is paced, and a Mage tutor can teach through Witchcraft's Teach Sorcery. The Fors Factorum and the Elemental Transference are reworked, and the Reliquaries are filled and keep what is pledged to them.

All of it sits behind the Supernatural: The Blue Rose game rule, which is on by default. A save from before 2.38 counts as on. With it off no temple is ever founded, and the Gnostic Council works as Witchcraft ships it. Relics, covens, Zecchia and the Seal of Solomon exist either way.

Membership is not a trait, so nothing on your portrait gives you away. It is a secret, Member of the Hermetic Order, which your Temple Magus learns the day you join. Your temple shows in your Situations as Chapter of the Blue Rose, and a button on the left of the screen, Your Chapter of the Order, opens the Situations window on your chapter alone, with Show All Situations for the rest. The Temple Magus owns the temple's panel and every other player member sees a copy of it. Nobody outside the Order sees either.

If the secret is exposed, the county he is in gets a witch trail for hunters to follow, his temple loses that year's Silence reward, and a member who exposed a brother has broken the Order's rules. Since 2.40 the temple can answer it: see One of Us Is Known.

Ranks and Standing#

RankHow you get thereWhat it opens
Neophyte (not yet sworn)Answer the noteThe tests. A Neophyte is in a temple but is not yet a member
Acolyte (first rank)Take the oathA tutor, and the Order's teaching
Medicum (second rank)Standing 25, three years as an Acolyte, and taught: the Mage trait, or a Warlock or True Witch who finished the Order's teachingYou tutor acolytes, and may Ask to Borrow from your temple's Reliquary
Magistratus (third rank)Standing 50 and three years as a Medicum; for an AI candidate, a free place among the temple's MagistratiRespondeo, Prepare the Powder and borrowing the Homunculus Beetle; with the Mage trait, the Elemental Transference and Make a Golem
Temple MagusHead of a temple: you founded it, inherited it or were granted a charterRaises and casts out members, keeps the temple's way, and, as a player, decides who the temple calls, or leaves it to the masks
A council seat, and Grand MagusChosen at a Conclave, or by the age of a templeThe Gnostic Council. The Grand Magus leads the whole Order

Since 2.40 the temple's panel heads each rank with its number and its Latin, as in Third Rank, Magistratus: the senior members (hover the Latin for what it means), and your own rank line ends with the standing you have.

Standing runs from 0 to 100 and starts at 5. Since 2.40 a member needs three years in his rank before he can be raised (a member who joined before 2.40 counts as having served them). An AI Temple Magus raises up to two Acolytes and two Medici a year, highest standing first, and passes over anyone he dislikes. Each temple keeps one Magistratus for every five members, at least two and at most six, not counting its Temple Magus; an AI Medicum is raised only while there is room, while a player can always be raised, and nobody is stepped down when a temple shrinks. A player Temple Magus gets The Rite of the Chapter once a year with one name for each rank: Raise [name] to Medicum, Raise [name] to Magistratus, Raise them both, Not this year (those passed over are put forward again at next year's Rite) or Leave the temple to the masks from now on. A player raised to Medicum chooses how he will teach: Hard, as I was taught (his acolytes gain 2 more standing a year and think 10 less of him) or Patiently (they think 15 more of him), and since 2.40 he is asked once.

The masks run the temple (2.40). A player Temple Magus can hand the roses and the Rite to the masks with the decision Leave the Temple to the Masks, or with the last option of A Name for the Rose or the Rite. The masks then send the notes and raise one of each rank a year, and a toast tells him each name (Your Temple Sent a Rose, Raised by Your Temple). Run Your Temple Yourself takes it back. A player who is at war, in an activity or in prison is never stopped by the Order's business either: the masks act in his place and tell him (The Masks Held the Rite, The Masks Sent the Roses), and the Order's prompts come at least thirty days apart.

Hand On Your Temple (2.40) lets a Temple Magus step down while another Magistratus of his temple is free to lead it, at most once in ten years. In The Mask Passes he chooses Stay on as a Magistratus of the temple or Work outside the temple, with the Adepti; either way he keeps his standing, and his named successor, else the temple's senior member, takes the chair. An AI Temple Magus does it in old age or by a content or humble nature, never if ambitious or arrogant, and never as Grand Magus.

The main sourcesStanding

Every member carries Esoteric Studies: +0.1% monthly magic lifestyle experience for each point of standing, so +10% at 100. Dues are 5 gold a year (6 for a Medicum, 7 for a Magistratus); the Temple Magus pays none. They go into the Gnostic Council's funds once the council exists, and to the Temple Magus before that.

The Order teaches its Mages. Every year each member with the Mage trait gains one point on one of four tracks, drawn at random: Secret Knowledge, Combat Magic, Demonology or Alchemy. A full track is skipped. The temple's way makes its own track twice as likely, and the Prerogative Treat with the Pit does the same for Demonology.

Temples#

The Order wants six temples, far apart. At game start a player who is a Mage is offered one in A Temple Without a Master: I will be its Temple Magus founds it at your capital. The rest are seated around the best-placed AI Mages, scored on learning and title rank, with a lean toward Western Europe on the base game's map.

Witchcraft makes no Mages at the start of a game, so on a fresh start there is often nobody to seat. The Order then gathers itself: every year, while fewer than six temples stand, the best-placed AI Mage outside it founds one. That is at most one temple a year, so expect the six to appear over the first years of a campaign rather than on the first day.

  • Size. A temple holds 30 members, 5 more for every 25 years it has stood, up to 60, and 10 more while it meets beneath a Collegium, up to 70. AI candidates are only called while a temple is under 80% full; the last fifth is kept for players.
  • How many. One temple for every 170 counties in the world. Charters and daughter temples stop there.
  • Petition for a Charter. A landed member who is not a Temple Magus, at standing 75 (60 under More Hands), while someone else sits in the chair, once every ten years. The Grand Magus decides. Granted, you found a temple at your capital and keep your standing.
  • Found a Daughter Chapter. A landed Magistratus who is not the Temple Magus, at standing 75, from a full temple that has not sent out a daughter in fifty years. Up to five AI members who think well enough of you (20 or more) follow you, keeping their ranks.
  • Found the Order Anew. Shown only when no temple stands anywhere. A landed Mage who knows two other Mages who could join (three, for twenty years after the Order is broken; the AI waits those twenty years out) (at his court or visiting, his vassals, his liege, or anyone whose witch secret he holds) founds a temple at his capital, and each of them is asked in The Order Again.
  • Succession. A player Temple Magus can Name as Successor in the Temple a Magistratus of his own temple. Otherwise the highest rank, then the highest standing, takes the temple. A player who becomes Temple Magus by any road chooses its way in The Mask of the Temple.
  • A temple with no members left is gone. What its members pledged stays with them.

The temple's way#

Each temple keeps one of the three words above the others. A player chooses when he becomes Temple Magus, and there is no decision to change it afterwards. An AI head picks by his own temperament.

WayEvery member getsTrack taught twice as often
SilenceSilent Habits: −5 to the success chance of hostile schemes against youAlchemy
Subservience+2 standing a yearCombat Magic
ObservanceKept Rites: +1 learning, and +10 to the temple's Elemental TransferenceSecret Knowledge

Changing your temple#

New in 2.40. A sworn member can move to another temple and keep what he has earned: his rank, his standing, his years in rank, a place among the Adepti or on the council, and the relics he pledged, which go with him to the new Reliquary. Anything he has borrowed goes back to its keeper, and an Acolyte is given a new tutor at the new temple's year. His rank does not change on the move, so a Magistratus who joins a temple already full of Magistrati stays one.

  • Change Your Temple (decision) lists the four nearest temples with room for one more, each with its Temple Magus. Write to one opens Ask to Join Their Temple on him; I will stay in my own temple. closes it. Use it when your own temple is too far away to take part in, or when you would rather serve under another Temple Magus.
  • Ask to Join Their Temple (Supernatural menu) is shown to a sworn member on the Temple Magus of any other temple, wherever he is, because every member knows who leads the temples; an outsider never sees it. Not while you lead a temple yourself (hand it on first, with Hand On Your Temple), within five years of your last move, or from prison, and the temple must have room. Once every three months, and once in five years to the same Temple Magus.

A player Temple Magus decides for himself. An AI one weighs what he thinks of you, your rank (a Magistratus most, then a Medicum) and whether you are a Mage, whether you live far from your own temple, standing of 50 or more, and his own nature: gregarious and ambitious heads are glad of you, paranoid and arrogant ones are not, he is slow to take a member from a head who is his friend, and he will hardly take a rival at all. Taken in, you move at once: the new Temple Magus thinks 10 better of you (Joined My Temple) and the old one 15 worse (Left My Temple), both for five years, and a player who loses a member is told.

The Collegium#

New in 2.39: a temple can meet in secret beneath a house of learning. The Collegium is an ordinary one-level building that any ruler can raise in his own capital, in a castle, city or temple holding, and nothing in its text names the Order, so a Collegium on the map gives nothing away. It gives whoever holds it +5% monthly learning lifestyle experience, +10% development growth in the county and a little income. It is hidden when the Order's rule is off.

It is rare. Each temple's own hall is always allowed, and besides those there are at most seven ordinary Collegia in the world; the AI begins at most one every two years. That limit holds the AI only: a player can always build one in his capital.

How a temple gets its hall. A Collegium becomes a temple's hall when its Temple Magus builds it, or a council member or the Grand Magus of that temple with 50 standing. Otherwise, each temple's year it looks for a free Collegium where its head's lord rules (the head himself if he holds land, else the court he serves): the lord's capital first, then anywhere he holds. A courtier Temple Magus's AI lord is keener to build one. If none can be found and everyone involved is AI, the Gnostic Council may pay 350 gold to raise one (one chance in five each temple's year, if its funds hold that much). Once in a game, AI temples that already stand are given their halls; on an older save that happens in the first year under 2.39.

A temple beneath its Collegium gets
Room+10 members, up to 70
The Elemental Transference and Make a Golem+10 to their chance
Respondeo cast in that countyNo stress (Respondeo never wounds since 2.40)
The ConclavePrefers to meet there
A Name You Have Heard Before40% a year, not 20%, for a player who holds that county
A member's momentBeneath the Collegium, at most once in ten years: walk with the neophytes (+5 standing), or as a Mage renew the ward over the arch (5 power, the hall warded for ten years, +3 standing)

The hall gives nothing for ten years after a hunter walks The Corridor beneath it. When the county changes hands, a player Temple Magus gets A New Lord Above the Hall: bring him in, carry on as before, or Let the hall go (that Collegium cannot be a temple's hall again for twenty years). A player lord above a hall may get The Cellar That Isn't There, once in fifty years: let the scholars be, have the cellars searched (a hidden roll on intrigue; found, he learns the temple and its master's secret; failed, the Order is told), or ask the master what is taught at night. If the building is gone, the members are told The Collegium Is Gone and the temple meets as before.

Who the Order calls#

Once a year each temple sends a note and a rose to up to three people (four under More Hands) inside its Temple Magus's diplomatic range, if it has room. Anyone in one of these groups can be called:

WhoWhat they need
Mages, and anyone with the SourceNothing more
Members' childrenAge 12 to 30 and learning 8, with a parent in the Order. A member's death marks each of his children for forty years
WitchesAdult, learning 12, a moderate fit, and neither callous nor deceitful
ScholarsLearning 16, age 16 to 40, a learning education of the second tier or better, and a high fit
Unlanded scholarsLearning 12, age 16 to 50, a learning education of the third tier or better, no land, and a fair fit

Never called: hunters, demons, golems, necromancers, anyone Bound to the Book, anyone who turned the note down in the last ten years or failed the tests in the last thirty, and anyone who ever left the Order keeping what he knew.

Among those, the temple prefers learning and fit, a member's child (+50), the Source (+30) and a Mage (+20). It favours a young heir to a great title: +25 for an unlanded candidate of 30 or under who is heir to a duchy or more. It passes over AI lords: −30 for a landed AI, and −30 more at kingdom rank, so the Order grows through courtiers rather than kings. A player is never marked down for his lands.

The numbers: the Order's fit

Fit is how well a character would keep the three words. It decides whether a witch or a scholar is in the Order's reach at all, player or AI, and half of it is added to every candidate's place in the Selection. For the AI it also steers whether it answers, which answer it gives at each test and how it behaves as a member. A player chooses his answers freely.

WordCounts forCounts against
SilenceShy, Patient, Calm +10; Paranoid +5Drunkard, Rowdy −10
SubservienceHumble, Loyal +10; Content, Zealous +5Disloyal −15; Arbitrary, Stubborn −10
ObservanceDiligent, Curious, Just +10Impatient −15; Lazy, Fickle −10
The Order's tasteAmbitious +10, Compassionate +5Sadistic −20; Callous, Deceitful −15

Arrogant is −30, or only −5 beside two of Diligent, Patient and Loyal. Gregarious counts for nothing either way.

A player Temple Magus decides the names himself in A Name for the Rose. Since 2.40 it comes once a year, three or four months after the Selection, with up to three names and their portraits: Send the rose (or Send a rose to each of them), Only to [the first], Not this one or None this year (those passed over are not put forward again for a year), or Send them, and leave the temple to the masks from now on. The decision Run Your Temple Yourself takes the choice back. A Temple Magus who is busy or has left the temple to the masks is told by a toast instead.

Invite into the Order (2.39) lets a player Temple Magus name someone himself, from the Supernatural menu: anyone of 12 or older in his diplomatic range, free, whom he does not already know to be a member, while his temple has room. The Order's note finds them the way it finds everyone the temples choose. Since 2.40, if the person is already of the Order, the note comes back with a blue rose pressed in it (Already One of Us): each of you now knows the other is a member. If the person could never join, nothing happens, which looks exactly like a refused note, so the interaction cannot be used to test anyone. It can be used every six months, and on the same person once in ten years either way.

A player's way in#

  • A Name You Have Heard Before. Two scholars arguing over wine, a book merchant, or your confessor. An adult player with learning 12 and a temple in reach has a 20% chance a year, once in a life, and 40% if he holds a county where a temple meets beneath a Collegium. Remember the name puts you within the Order's reach whatever your traits and makes the tests easier for ten years. Ask around about it is an intrigue roll; if it works, someone speaks for you.
  • A Rose Among the Things. When a member dies, a player child of his aged 6 or more finds a box with a pressed rose, a rose coin and a page nobody can read. Keep them and the Order can find you for forty years.
  • Seek Out the Order. A decision for a player who is a Mage or has the Source, once every five years, while a temple stands: you leave a blue rose on the library steps. Since 2.40 nothing tells you outright that it worked. If you never heard the rumour, the rumour itself is your answer: a boy hands your steward a sprig of rosemary tied with blue thread, and a name (A Name You Have Heard Before). If you had heard it, the toast is only The Rose Is Gone. Either way, if a temple in reach has room, you are near the top of its next Selection. If not, a toast with a line of scene says why. If no note has come after about a year, The Rose Withered tells you so, and you may leave another at once.
  • Ask About the Blue Rose. A prison interaction on a member whose secret you know. He gives up at most two others of his temple, and has broken the Order's rules. Then you will name me to them lets him go and puts your name forward.
  • Since 2.39 a Temple Magus can Invite into the Order you himself, which sends the note at once. An outsider the Adeptus Major's stranger visits can answer I would rather be one of you (Hunters and the Order); a Temple Magus may put forward a new lord above his hall (Bring him in); a member may ask the temple to consider his spouse (Ask the temple to consider her); and a lord can ask the master of a Collegium what is taught there at night. Each of these four puts you near the top of the next Selection if you could join.

The pledge#

  1. A Note and a Blue Rose. On your pillow, pressed into your hand in the market, or inside the book you were reading. Since 2.40 the note names the old chapel outside the temple's town. I will come makes you a Neophyte. Not for me keeps you off the list for ten years.
  2. The Old Chapel, a few weeks later, or, where the temple meets beneath a Collegium, a servant at the chapel door who leads you back into town and down a hidden stair. The masks explain the three words and what failing costs; nobody shows his face yet. I will take the tests or I will walk out now.
  3. The Coin, a month or two later. Bring back a coin with a rose on it from the tomb beneath the chapel, its bell tower, the mill wheel on the river below the town or a cage in the chapel's cellar. Each answer is a roll on one skill: 10 plus 3 for each point of the skill, between 10 and 90, and 15 better if you remembered the rumour. Give my coin to another neophyte always fails: the coin was the test. In the cage, Reach in and grab it costs you a hand if the beast wakes; a werewolf or hybrid can Let it catch my scent instead (+30 to the roll, and the beast never takes his hand).
  4. The Observance. A mask gives you the key to an empty house in the town, and one night someone finds you there and asks what you are doing. Say nothing passes. Lie well is an intrigue roll. Tell him fails. A vampire or hybrid with 15 on his track can Make him forget he saw me: it passes, but the Temple Magus learns what you are.
  5. Will You Heed the Call? Blindfolded, you are taken down to the temple beneath the town, and the masks come off. I heed the call makes you an Acolyte with a tutor, whose name you learn at your first working. A Neophyte at a Conclave takes the oath there, in front of every temple.

Fail at any step and it is Pulveris Memoria: you wake in your own bed, remember nothing of the Order, and cannot be called again for thirty years. Nobody dies in the pledge. A Neophyte still waiting after two years is let go. Since 2.39 a Neophyte who is in prison when a step arrives is asked again thirty days later instead of being dropped, and a mind guarded against domination (the Draught of a Clear Mind, the Seal of Solomon and the like) remembers everything Pulveris Memoria was meant to take.

Tutors and the first spell#

Every Acolyte is given a tutor, the Medicum or higher of his temple with the fewest acolytes. Since 2.40 the Temple Magus teaches only when no Medicum or Magistratus can, an Adeptus after everyone else, and a tutor who is resting or has said I Will Not Take Acolytes is passed over. While he has one he carries Under a Tutor, +10% monthly magic lifestyle experience, and each year there is a 10% chance something comes of it: friends (weight 35), grateful and nothing more (45), or rivals (20), each moved by both natures and by how he is taught. A humble, compassionate or diligent student, a patient tutor, and liking each other make friends likelier; an ambitious, arrogant or vengeful student and a strict tutor make rivals likelier. A student who thinks well of his tutor (40 or more) never becomes his rival. Since 2.39 rivals come less often than they did.

The teaching takes six years, a year less each for the Source, learning 15 and a Magistratus tutor, two less since 2.40 for anyone who has finished Witchcraft's Arcane Training, and never under three. A player hears The Words in the first year and The Candle That Would Not Go Out in the third. It ends in The First Real Spell: I am a Mage now gives the Mage trait, Witchcraft's own way. A Warlock or True Witch cannot take a second capstone, so she is taught the Order's craft instead (I have learned what the Order can teach me), which counts as taught for the rank of Medicum. Since 2.39 anyone else can turn the Mage down the same way (Not yet. I follow another path): taught, but not a Mage.

Pacing the teaching (2.40). A tutor rests five years after each acolyte he finishes, and gets no acolyte to teach to the end while he rests; an acolyte who is ready waits for him. A player tutor is told ([name] Has Learned the Words), and every tutor gains 5 standing when his acolyte finishes. An AI acolyte finishes only while his temple has made fewer than two Mages from AI acolytes in the last ten years, three while it meets beneath a Collegium and one more in an Observance temple. Players are not held by that limit.

The other road (2.40). A tutor with the Mage trait may instead teach a witch acolyte through Witchcraft's own Teach Sorcery. A player tutor is offered it in The Other Road: Teach [her] sorcery opens the Teach Sorcery window, and if the acolyte accepts, Arcane Training begins; finishing it shortens the years in the temple. An AI tutor offers it to a player acolyte directly. Witchcraft's own long wait between pupils applies, and the Order's rest after it.

When a player is tutor or acolyte, the pair has a moment now and then: The First Lesson (which since 2.40 shows the acolyte), The Errand, Two Heads Over One Problem (shared Magical Research gains a breakthrough's worth of progress at once), The Lesson Returned, since 2.40 The Wrong Word, A Question You Cannot Answer (a learning duel) and Where Were You Last Night? (an intrigue duel against the acolyte's spouse or close kin), and once, Late Lessons, which can make them lovers if both are 18 or older, not close or extended family, attracted to each other and fond of each other. A temple also has moments of its own; see the chapter's moments.

A child born to two members who are both Mages has a 10% chance of the Source (A Spark in the Cradle).

Romance and the chapter's moments#

The chapter's moments. Since 2.39 each player member of a temple has a 40% chance at the temple's year of one moment, drawn from fourteen, each at most once in ten years: An Outsider Asks Questions (often a real witch hunter), The Relic That Would Not Sleep, A Neophyte's Test Gone Wrong, The Tutor's Quarrel (which shows your acolyte, and since 2.40 has four more answers: Then show me for a Mage, Try it your way, then for the patient, Not in front of the chapter with diplomacy 12, and Remind [him] who teaches whom for the wrathful or arrogant), and new in 2.39:

  • The Masks Are Watching (Neophyte or Acolyte): keep your eyes down (+3), learn whose face is behind a mask (an intrigue duel), stare back, or as a vampire or hybrid listen to their hearts.
  • Beneath Your Station (a landed Acolyte handed a bucket): scrub the floor (+5), send for a servant, or refuse (−5, and the senior resents it).
  • The Same Night: the rite, or your family who needs you (+5 or −5 standing), or claim to be ill.
  • One of the Seniors (an Acolyte mocked by a senior): endure it, answer him in public, take it to the Temple Magus, find out what he is hiding, or as a wolf let him see it in your eyes.
  • A Brother Asks for Help (a player Temple Magus whose member broke the rules): bury it (one time in five it comes out on you), let the masks judge him, or stay out of it.
  • A Feud in the Chapter (or on the Council, for a Grand Magus): take a side, make them work together, or let them settle it (a wound, never a death).
  • Where Do You Go at Night? (any member whose spouse is not of the Order): lie, tell the truth (which breaks the Order's rules), ask the temple to consider your spouse, or let them think it is someone else.
  • After the Others Have Gone and A Door Left Open, below, and Beneath the Collegium.

The creatures act as what they are. Besides the options above, a vampire or hybrid can look an outsider in the eye in An Outsider Asks Questions (10 track experience, 20 against a hunter, who needs a full track; not on a guarded mind), a hide's Champion or anyone with Magical Senses can listen to what The Relic That Would Not Sleep wants, a wolf can let the wolf out when They Came for What You Know, and a wolf can follow the scent at a Conclave. Most Order and pack moments come at least 60 days apart; follow-ups and reports never wait, but they count.

The Relic That Would Not Sleep (2.40) names the item that hums and who keeps it, and each answer says what it does. I will quiet it (a Mage) and Listen to what it wants (a Champion, or anyone with Magical Senses) each earn 5 standing. Listening also brings What the Relic Wanted a few days later: learning experience and, if a living member of your temple keeps the relic, a way to ask him to lend it to you. Leave it be costs nothing, and weeks later The Relic Is Quiet Again tells you whether it fell silent by itself, or whether a Medicum, a Magistratus or the Temple Magus of your temple quieted it and earned the standing instead.

Romance. When two members become lovers and one is the other's patron (his own Temple Magus, or the Grand Magus) while the other is a Neophyte, Acolyte or Medicum, both 18 or older, the junior may ask him to speak for him at the rite (What Is It Worth?, Your Lover Asks a Favour). The favour is +10 standing from a Temple Magus or +15 from the Grand Magus. It never gives a rank: Medicum still needs the teaching and 25 standing, Magistratus 50. People talk: a peer of the same rank may take Favouritism (−25 for ten years) toward the junior, and both lovers carry the charge until the next Conclave, which costs each 10 standing. The new After the Others Have Gone (a player senior and a willing junior) and A Door Left Open (a player junior who speaks up, or asks a price for it) start such affairs. A lover who was compelled never counts.

The Prerogative#

What the Order works toward for ten years, set by its master: the Grand Magus if he is a member, otherwise the Temple Magus of the oldest temple. A player master uses Set the Order's Prerogative, which lists the five before you choose; a Conclave proclaims it when it is due. Deeds that serve it earn standing, and an Adeptus earns 5 more for each.

PrerogativeWhat earns standing, and what else it does
Recover What Was LostPledging an item for the first time +10, drawing a page of the Vade Maecum +15, winning a relic war +20, holding the Seal of Solomon for the first time +30
SilenceCasting Respondeo +15, throwing Obfuscation Powder +5, a year with nobody in your temple exposed +3; an exposed member loses 10 more
The Book Must Be FoundHearing where a page is +5 (once in five years), drawing a page +15, opening the Black Stone +20
More HandsTutors earn +4 a year for each acolyte, every temple calls four people a year, and a charter needs standing 60
Treat with the PitRule One is lifted: no standing lost for Zecchia or a demon's Compact. A demon's favour pays +15 standing, and Demonology is taught twice as often

What members can do#

The Adepti. Once a year the Adeptus Major calls the highest-standing Magistratus at 75 who does not lead a temple. If he accepts, he works outside the temples: 5 more standing for every deed that serves the Prerogative, and he may borrow from any member he knows. A Temple Magus who steps down can join them, and since 2.40 an Adeptus tutors only when nobody else in the temple can.

Respondeo, and One of Us Is Known#

Respondeo is the Order's spell of forgetting, a decision for a Magistratus or Temple Magus, every five years (ten before 2.40). The county you stand in forgets what it saw: any hunter's trail there goes cold, the witch secrets of your temple's members in it are known again only to the temple and their families, and since 2.40 a member of any temple whose Blue Rose secret was exposed there is forgotten too, known again only to his own temple. It never wounds now. It costs medium stress (none in the county of your temple's own Collegium) and a year of Spent on Respondeo. When you cast it you choose how it is paid:

  • Use the Obfuscation Powder: your own Powder, or else one your temple's Reliquary keeps, whose keeper is told.
  • The temple will pay it with me: two members of your temple who are free and willing pay 10 power each, wherever they live. AI members pay first, if they do not dislike you; a player member is asked, and gains 5 standing if he pays. Before 2.40 only lower-ranked members who were your own courtiers or vassals could pay, which almost never happened.
  • I will pay it alone: 10 power (it was 20).
  • Not now: nothing is cast, but the five-year wait has begun (Known issues).

One of Us Is Known. When a member's secret is exposed, the exposure is remembered by his temple for two years, and a player Temple Magus who is free gets One of Us Is Known, which since 2.40 is a real choice: Send [name] to answer it (the nearest Magistratus of the temple who can pay for it himself; a Grand Magus may also send another temple's Temple Magus), I will go there myself, or Let it lie. The one sent travels for one to three months and pays by the same ladder; you pay nothing, and a toast tells you [county] Has Forgotten, No One Could Pay or An Order Refused. A player who is sent gets Word from Your Temple Magus (or from the Grand Magus): pay by the ladder, I cannot pay for it, or I will not go, which costs 10 standing and leaves the one who sent him thinking 20 less of him for five years. An AI member refuses only if he thinks very little of the one who sent him, or has broken the Order's rules. An AI temple, or a player Temple Magus who is at war, in an activity or in prison, sends someone itself, at most once in three years, and the busy player is told (The Masks Sent Someone).

The Fors Factorum#

The Order's most secret spell, kept in a letter in the founders' own hand. Since 2.40 the letter is a relic, The Fors Factorum (famed, +1 learning). It is placed once, with the Order on, once a temple stands or ten years into the game: to the Gnostic Council's Keeper of the Reliquary if he is a member, else to the most learned Temple Magus, pledged to his temple. If it is ever lost from the world, it surfaces again. It is never lent, and Zecchia never takes it.

Cast the Fors Factorum (it was Learn the Fors Factorum) is open to the Grand Magus, the Order's master, a Temple Magus, or a Magistratus who has mastered metamagic, who holds the letter or whose temple's Reliquary keeps it, who does not already have cancer, and who has not cast it in the last five years. A Magistratus whose own Metamagic research finishes may be offered it then. Its tooltip: For a few years your spells will cost you no power. Then the spell takes its price from your body.

  • For two to five years your spells cost no power, and Magic Without Price gives +10% monthly magic lifestyle experience.
  • Then the cancer comes, once (before 2.40, a cancer every three to ten years for life). While it stays in your body your spells stay free.
  • Any cure ends the spell: a physician, the Elemental Transference or anything else that takes the cancer away. A player is told The Spell Is Broken. If a Transference unravels later, the cancer comes back but the spell does not.
  • It can be cast again five years after the last casting, but never while you carry cancer.

A Fors cast before 2.40 pays its next price under these rules, once.

The Elemental Transference#

Heal by Elemental Transference is an interaction for a Magistratus or Temple Magus with the Mage trait, on someone in his diplomatic range, every five years. Five people take the illness, a fifth each, for five years; if one of them is killed in that time, it comes back. The ritual costs 10 power and succeeds at 20 plus 3 for each point of learning (+10 as Temple Magus, +10 in an Observance temple, +10 beneath a Collegium), between 25 and 95. A failure can open a rift that lets a demon out.

What it cures. Before 2.40: cancer, consumption, leprosy, the great pox and the Blue Fever. Since 2.40 also infirmity, fragile bones, typhus, the plague, smallpox, pneumonia, the bloody flux, measles and ordinary illness. An AI member still asks for it mostly for the first five.

Who carries it. Members of the temple are asked in The Temple Asks for Your Blood, and since 2.40 only once: Take my blood (this time only), Take my blood, and ask me whenever the temple needs it (asked again in future), or Not mine (never asked again). A member who is busy is not asked. Willing AI members are taken first; the places left are filled by townsfolk from the caster's capital, and each townsperson's family is paid a small sum, by the patient, or by the caster if the patient cannot. Townsfolk whose family cannot be paid do not sign. Their children under 16 also get Studying At University, and the family thinks better of whoever paid. The interaction needs five who could carry it: members, or townsfolk you can pay for. Members who carry it gain 15 standing, and the caster 10; townsfolk gain nothing in the Order.

Asking. A member with an illness it can cure uses Ask the Temple to Heal You, every five years; his Temple Magus hears it in A Brother Is Dying (A Sister Is Dying for a woman). A refusal means he cannot ask again for five years. A player Temple Magus who is busy is not asked: the masks begin the working and tell him. Since 2.40 the patient, and a player who worked it, see it in the Situations window: Worked By, They Carry It, and the year the risk ends.

The Conclave#

The masked gathering of every temple, and a new activity. The Order calls one in four cases: when the Grand Magus's chair is empty and three or more temples stand; every ten years, so a Grand Magus who is a member can proclaim the Prerogative, while three or more temples stand; when a player Grand Magus takes Call a Conclave, at most once in five years; and since 2.39 when someone calls Invocare. The one asked hosts at a county seat he holds, or since 2.40 at his camp or estate if he holds no land; only when he has none of these does the Temple Magus of the oldest temple that can host do it for him, and since 2.40 his note says why he was asked. A Temple Magus asked in the Grand Magus's place can Let another temple call it, which passes the call on and ends his alert; a Grand Magus can put his own call off with Not now. A player who is at war, in an activity or in prison is passed over and told. It costs the game's usual price for a gathering, and only one Conclave runs at a time. It prefers to meet where a temple has its Collegium.

Every member in the host's reach is invited, acolytes included. A Temple Magus (+40), a council member (+30) and a Neophyte waiting for his oath (+30) are the likeliest to come.

PhaseLengthWhat happens
The Masks2 weeksMeetings: A Familiar Mask, The Old Story, A Mask Knows Too Much, and, for a member who knows the Grand Magus staged a rift, What the Grand Magus Did
The Reckoning1 weekEvery member present gains 3 standing. Rule-breakers who came are judged: a player host hears one in The Accused (Forgiven, A penance for −20 standing, or Cast out); the rest are cast out if the host thinks less than −20 of them, otherwise they lose 20 standing. Since 2.39 favouritism is raised (−10 standing each), a Grand Magus facing Invocare is not judged in the room (the vote is his judgment), and the motion is heard. The Prerogative is proclaimed if it is due
The Anointing1 weekNeophytes who passed take the oath in front of every temple, and members with proof make their entrance
The Vote2 weeksInvocare's slips are counted first. If the chair is empty, the four best-placed Temple Magi present stand for it (each listed once, a 2.39 fix), and Temple Magi and council members vote. Most votes wins; a tie goes to the stronger candidate. The Grand Magus then fills empty seats (a player gets Fill the Seats)
IntentWhoWhat it pays
PresideThe host, by default+10 standing and prestige
Watch and LearnGuests, by default+5 standing
Speak for My TempleMedicum or higher+10 standing
Seek the ChairA Temple Magus, while the chair is emptyA better chance in the vote, and +5 standing
Seek a SeatA Magistratus not yet on the councilA better chance of a seat, and +5 standing
Find a TeacherAn Acolyte with no tutorA tutor
Borrow from Another TempleA MagistratusA pledged item from a member of another temple, on loan for three years
Ask for the TransferenceA member with a deadly illnessThe Elemental Transference, with the volunteers already in the room
Make an EntranceA member with proof: a golem or demon he killed in the last twenty years, or a relic, a page, the book, or a famed or illustrious magical artifact not shown before+20 standing. An Acolyte is raised to Medicum on the spot (An Entrance, which since 2.40 shows a witness opposite the entrant; a host who brings proof rises from the chair)

If a Conclave called for an empty chair has not filled it three years later, the Temple Magus of the oldest temple takes the chair. Nothing hides the Conclave from the map, so expect it to show there like any other activity.

Invocare: voting out the Grand Magus#

New in 2.39. Invocare is a decision for a Temple Magus or a member of the Gnostic Council with at least 50 standing. It needs another Temple Magus to second it (one who thinks −10 or less of the Grand Magus, or your friend), at least three temples standing, a Grand Magus who has held the chair for three years and does not hold Imperium, no Invocare heard in the last ten years, no Conclave running, and a Temple Magus free to host (with land, or since 2.40 a camp or estate). Nothing is paid when you click it: it calls a Conclave.

The Grand Magus hears first (The Chair Is Questioned) and picks one defence. It is paid when the motion is heard; an AI picks by his nature.

DefenceWhat it does
Then they will remember what they owe meCalls in his hooks: every voter present he holds one on must vote to keep him
Open the council's coffers100 gold from the council's funds buys the three AI voters least sure of their vote, each 35 less likely to vote against him. Players cannot be bought
Let me remind them what the Order has done under meA learning duel. If it works, every AI voter is 15 less likely to vote against him
A show of power20 power: every AI voter with the Mage trait is 10 less likely
Let them see who they are crossingNeeds 40 dread. Voters who fear him weigh it double; voters who do not are 10 more likely to vote against him
I will not wait to be pushedHe steps down before the vote: +10 standing, and he cannot stand in the election that follows
Let the masks decideNothing

At the Reckoning the motion is heard: the caller loses 20 standing and the ten-year wait begins. Every Temple Magus and council member present votes; the caller counts against the Grand Magus and the Grand Magus for himself. A player votes in Invocare: Aye, Nay, or a blank slip. An AI voter starts at 50 and moves with what he thinks of the Grand Magus, rivalry and friendship with either man, whether the Grand Magus broke the Order's rules (+30, more if the voter is just) or showed favouritism (+10), a Grand Magus with 75 standing who set the Prerogative in the last ten years (−15), and his own nature (loyal −15, content −10, an ambitious man who wants the chair +20), between 10 and 90.

The slips are counted as the Vote begins (The Slips Are Counted). More Ayes than Nays removes him: −25 standing, out of the chair, his Imperium ended, and the same Conclave elects his successor; he cannot stand in that election, but he can at a later one. A tie keeps him. If it fails, he gains 10 standing and thinks 30 less of the caller for twenty years (Questioned My Authority), and the caller loses 20 more standing and his council seat if he has one. If the motion lapses, or no Conclave can meet, nobody pays anything.

The rules, leaving, and the Old Law#

Breaking the Order's rules marks a member for ten years and costs him 40 opinion with his Temple Magus (Broke Faith). It covers exposing a brother's secret, talking under Ask About the Blue Rose, sending Zecchia after a member you know is one, being caught paying her from the council's funds, a cruel member binding the Vade Maecum, bending the Old Law, and since 2.39 loosing the Homunculus Beetle, telling your spouse the truth, a Temple Magus's cover-up coming out, and a staged rift being exposed. Favouritism is a lesser charge, raised at the next Conclave. An AI Temple Magus casts out a marked member at the temple's year. No rule of the Order kills.

Leave the Order is a decision any member can take, and being cast out ends the same way. The Order Does Not Let You Walk Away gives two answers:

  • Let them take it: Pulveris Memoria. You forget the Order and lose your magic: the Mage and True Witch traits, the 21 magic perks, your spells, your stored power, the Fors Factorum and any council seat. The Source and the witch trait or secret stay, because you were born with one and other people know the other. You cannot be called for thirty years.
  • I will keep what I know: you keep everything and can never come back. Four times in ten, two to five years later, They Came for What You Know: a learning roll, and if you lose it, Pulveris Memoria anyway.

A wolf may remember. Since 2.39, when Pulveris Memoria takes the Order from a werewolf or hybrid, the names he knew are kept. Witchcraft's The Ringing can bring them back (10 plus 8 for every ring counted, at most one ring in 180 days, +10 at Hide Bond 50, at most 60: 18% on the first), and so can a slow pull, checked every three to six years while the thirty years last (8% plus 0.12 for each point of his track, at most 20%). Either way he gets It Comes Back and knows those members again. His magic does not come back. A mind guarded against domination is never wiped in the first place.

The Old Law. The Order takes no necromancers. A member who becomes one gets The Forbidden Art each year until it is settled: Renounce it (the Necromancer trait, the Master Necromancer modifier and the right to raise Skeleton Warriors go, with major stress and −20 standing), Let them judge me, or Leave before they cast me out.

Rule One is "with demons, don't". A demon may still Seek a Compact with the Order; the master hears it in A Demon at the Temple Door, and wearing the Seal of Solomon he can make it kneel instead. A demon with a Compact can Borrow from the Reliquary, and owes a favour for every loan.

Hunters and the Order#

  • A hunter who beats a member in a hunt's fight learns his secret and his temple, and takes one of his pledged items as evidence (Tell Me About the Rose).
  • When a witch hunt's culprit is a member, a hunter with Witch-Hunting 60 or a name can choose They answer to a temple and sweep her temple the way he would sweep a coven: up to five rounds, each a member who is in that county. Taking the Temple Magus alive gives him every member's secret.
  • In a sweep in the county where the temple has its Collegium, the second door is The Corridor: walk through (it opens at once if he holds a Magistratus of that temple in his dungeon; otherwise a hidden 50, +25 brave, −25 craven, +15 if he knows the temple, −20 if the hall is warded), or turn back at no cost. Through, the temple is profaned for ten years and its hall gives nothing; failed, he takes major stress and the sweep ends. A swept temple counts toward breaking the Order.
  • The Adeptus Major Moves: a hooded, unnamed stranger. A player hunter who learned or swept a temple gets him a year later, at most once in five years, and since 2.39 the Order sends him on purpose, to AI outsiders too: to anyone it is watching on the road to breaking it, to the accuser of a sister who died on trial, when the council wages war on hunters, and on the night before a Break. Take the gold and forget the house (100 gold), I would rather be one of you (new: if you could join, you are near the top of the next Selection and keep everything you know), or Make me, a battle of wills on learning that ends in Pulveris Memoria if you lose, unless your mind is guarded. Never a death.
  • When Witchcraft's Witch Trial begins on a member, her Temple Magus gets A Sister on Trial (or A Brother on Trial) a few days later. Dead: have her answered for (the Adeptus Major visits the accuser) or say her name at the next rite. In a cell: buy her freedom from an AI gaoler, throw the Powder at her gaolers so the accuser forgets her, or let her be. Free: bring her back, or watch her in case she talked.
  • Respondeo, Obfuscation Powder and the council's Great Respondeo are the Order's answer to a trail.
  • When a wolf kills a member, a player Temple Magus hears it in The Wolves Have Teeth: Let the hunters find it, or I will remember, which since 2.40 gives every living member of the temple a blood debt against the killer (Challenge).

Outsiders, and breaking the Order#

New in 2.39. An outsider knows of the Order if he knows a member's secret, has learned a temple (by beating a member on a hunt, catching one in An Outsider Asks Questions, or searching a Collegium's cellars), left the Order keeping what he knew, or is close family of someone the Order made vanish or wiped (for fifty years), or once the council has revealed itself. Such a man can take Break the Order of the Blue Rose, a Major decision, once in five years. It never gives the secret away. It needs:

  • a reason: he hunts witches (a hunter or witch hunter, or the Anti-Mage perk), leads a faith that condemns witchcraft, is the Chief Inquisitor, has left the Order, or is kin to someone it wronged;
  • to know the Temple Magus of at least half the temples;
  • a temple fallen to him or a hunter at his court (a sweep, or The Corridor walked), or a Temple Magus he knows for one in his dungeon;
  • the Grand Magus's chair empty, or its holder in his dungeon and known for what he is;
  • 1,000 prestige, or 1,000 piety for the head of a faith; the Order not broken in the last twenty years, and nobody else already moving against it.

It happens in two steps, and the price is taken only in the second. Tonight the Order's master hears of it (The Rose Burns Its Letters) and answers: Send the Adeptus Major (the stranger visits the breaker; take the gold or ask to join and the Break is off, lose the battle of wills and it fails, unless his mind is guarded), Hide what can be hidden (half the council's coffers are hidden from the Break), or Let the temples scatter (every temple closes and the council goes dark tonight, members keep their secrets and magic, and the Break finds nothing and costs nothing). Within thirty days the Break runs if he is still alive and free, still knows half the Temple Magi, the chair is still empty or its holder still in his dungeon, and he can still pay.

The Break: every member's secret is exposed (The Masks Are Torn Away), the council's treasury, or the half not hidden, goes to the breaker, the chair and every seat fall empty, every temple is closed (its Collegium stands on as an ordinary building), and the breaker gains a great deal of prestige, and piety if he heads a faith. For twenty years no temple gathers itself, and Found the Order Anew needs three known Mages instead of two. When someone does found it again, he may Send the Adeptus Major after the breaker (The Rose Remembers).

The Gnostic Council#

A Grand Magus and three seats, sitting over the world's magic. Until 2.38 it was Witchcraft's Gnostic Council with a Grand Magus in the chair; 2.38 gave it and its seats the Order's names, and with Supernatural: The Blue Rose on (the default) it belongs to the Order of the Blue Rose.

SeatGives
Grand Magus+3 diplomacy, +50% prestige, double diplomatic range. Appoints the other three, and can Call a secret Gnostic Council meeting every four years once all three seats are filled by three different people; since 2.40 an AI Grand Magus calls one every eight to ten years. With the Order on, the meeting is where he asks for Imperium or brings the Great Respondeo.
Keeper of the Reliquary+3 learning, +10% lifestyle experience, and Magical Research every five years instead of ten.
Adeptus Major+3 intrigue, +10% scheme discovery, can force a mage duel without losing face, and every Combat Magic project needs one breakthrough fewer.
Chancellor+3 stewardship, +1 influence a month, and Manage of Council Funds every two years (also the Grand Magus, while he holds Imperium). Since 2.40 his option to reveal the council only recommends it: I recommend that the Council reveal itself to the world.

With the Order on. Only members of the Order sit on the council. Invite to Gnostic Council, Request Gnostic Council appointment and Witchcraft's yearly offer of a seat all refuse anyone else, and leaving the Order, by any road, loses the seat. The chair is filled by the Order: while fewer than three temples stand, the Temple Magus of the oldest temple takes it; with three or more, a Conclave elects the Grand Magus from the Temple Magi, and fills the seats. Taking the chair forms the council if it never existed. A Mage Summit no longer founds the council or elects its chair. Once the Order owns the council, only members see its window, unless the council has revealed itself to the world. Since 2.39 the temples can also vote him out (Invocare), nobody can hold two seats (anyone who does keeps the higher one), and Make a donation to the Gnostic Council, Ask for the Gnostic Council's support and Request Gnostic Council appointment are shown only to members unless the council has been revealed.

On a save from before 2.38, a Grand Magus who is not a member is dealt with once. A player is asked in The Masks Bow to You to join a temple or give up the chair; an AI who can join is made a Magistratus of a temple, and one who cannot is removed from the chair. A player Grand Magus who is a member can Dissolve the Gnostic Council once every ten years: every seat falls empty, and each holder sent home thinks 15 less of him for ten years.

With the Order off, it is Witchcraft's council under the new names. It does not exist when the game starts, it is founded at a Mage Summit when a Mage Speaker with the Show superiority intent nominates himself and the vote passes, and whenever the chair is empty the next summit is an election.

The Grand Magus also fills seats with Invite to Gnostic Council (a Mage or Yaga; the invitee gains a lot of prestige), and a mage can ask with Request Gnostic Council appointment. He removes a member with Recall from the Gnostic Council, which needs 5,000 prestige, a hook, or an incapable member. Any member can Quit. A seat also qualifies you for the estate's Gnostic Council Archives and its top two tower levels.

The council keeps a treasury. Witches can Make a donation to the Gnostic Council every ten years (50 gold, plus 50 for a count, 100 for a duke, 200 for a king, 250 for an emperor), which makes a non-member a permanent Gnostic Council Supporter (+1 diplomacy). A supporter can Ask for the Gnostic Council's support once the treasury holds 200 gold: three years of protection against plots, research money and +20% magic experience, three years' access to a place of power, a court mage, or help in a war. The Grand Magus decides. With the Order on, members' dues go into the same treasury.

Draw on the Council's Treasury (2.40, in the council's decisions) is open to every seat: the Grand Magus, the Keeper of the Reliquary, the Adeptus Major and the Chancellor. In The Council's Treasury you take 100, 500 or 1,000 gold, each offered only while the treasury holds it, or close the ledger at no cost. The treasury must hold at least 100, you must not be in prison, and once you have taken gold you wait two years. Each other seat thinks 5 less of you for three years (Took From the Treasury), and every other seat held by a player is told. While the Grand Magus holds Imperium, nobody takes offence at his own draw. An AI seat draws only when it is short of gold, or at war and low on it; greed, ambition and debt make it likelier, and a content, generous or just one seldom does it.

Ask the Grand Magus to Fund Your Research (2.40, Supernatural menu, on the Grand Magus) lets a member of the Order ask for the council's money without being a donor. It sends Witchcraft's own letter, and the Grand Magus decides: granted, you receive a share of the treasury (at most 300 gold) and a Research Fund for three years, +1 learning and +20% magic lifestyle experience. An AI Grand Magus nearly always grants it when he thinks well of you, and nearly always refuses when he does not. You must be a Mage or be taking part in a Magical Research, hold no Research Fund already, and not be shut out of the magic community; the Grand Magus must be free, not at war with you and not too busy; and the treasury must hold more than 199 gold. Once every five years. A donor, who already has Witchcraft's own Ask for research funding, does not see it twice.

Reveal the Gnostic Council (2.40) is the Grand Magus's decision as well; the Chancellor can only recommend it, and the Grand Magus's answer to him is now I will weigh it. The tooltip says what it costs: every ruler learns the council exists and may fund it, witch hunters take an interest in its members, and anyone may now try to break the Order. An AI Grand Magus takes it only after the Chancellor has recommended it, and never while the council belongs to the Order.

Imperium#

New in 2.39, with the Order on. In the second part of Call a secret Gnostic Council meeting the Grand Magus can choose I bring a motion before the council., then I ask the council for Imperium. (The Council's Business). A player Grand Magus first pulls one lever (Before the Council Votes): call in his hooks (a voter he holds one on must grant it), give each seat holder a favour hook on himself (+35 to each AI's chance), pay each 100 gold (+25), argue the case (a diplomacy duel, +15 if it works), or let them decide.

The three seat holders vote: grant, refuse, or leave it to the others. An AI seat starts at 35, between 5 and 95, and moves with opinion, friendship and rivalry, fear (+20), the Grand Magus's standing and whether he broke the rules, whether he held Imperium by vote in the last ten years (−25), its own nature, and a world crisis (+15). More grants than refusals carries it; a tie refuses. Result: The Council Has Spoken.

Granted, he holds Imperium for five years: Witchcraft's old council-support modifier, renamed in 2.39, with +4 diplomacy, +4 learning, +1 monthly prestige and dynasty prestige, double monthly influence, double diplomatic range and +10% to the Ultimate Spell. While he holds it and the chair, Invocare cannot be called against him, he may Demand Submission from witches and Mages, he can Manage of Council Funds himself, and a player can proclaim a new Prerogative at once. It ends early if the temples vote him out, he steps down under Invocare, the Order is broken or scatters, or a staged rift is exposed. Witchcraft's own Extraordinary Measures still grant it for ten years.

Refused, the refusal stands for three years. If in that time a seat holder dies, a rift opens, the Blue Plague breaks out or hunters get halfway to breaking the Order, the council meets in an emergency session and votes once more, every AI seat 50 more likely to grant it.

Some Grand Magi make an emergency of their own. After a refusal he brought, a Grand Magus who can spend 20 power (or holds the Fors Factorum) can open a rift where the seat holder who argued hardest against him stands (Then the danger will come to them.). An AI does it only by its nature (callous, sadistic, ambitious, deceitful or arbitrary; never if honest, just or compassionate), and never against a friend, lover or close family. Thirty to ninety days later the dissenter gets The Ground Splits: close it (a Mage: 10 power and a wound), drive it back (any creature: a prowess roll), get everyone out, or run; no choice kills him. The Grand Magus carries the secret Staged a Rift. Exposed, at a Conclave or anywhere else, it breaks the Order's rules, every other seat holder thinks 40 less of him for twenty years, Invocare's ten-year wait is lifted, and his Imperium is void.

The Great Respondeo#

The council's first great work, new in 2.39: Respondeo for a whole realm. From fifty years into a game, once a year the Order looks for the independent realm with the most counties where a trail is live, at least five, with a member living in it. The Grand Magus brings it in The Council's Business (The memory of [realm] must be shifted.), and needs 20 power to cast it.

Only the three seat holders vote and pledge (The Council Pledges): the Chancellor's part is 200 gold from the council's funds, the Keeper's and the Adeptus Major's 10 power each. An AI seat always votes for it and pledges about half the time, more often if a member of his own temple lives in that realm or he is paranoid, less if compassionate. It passes on more votes for than against, and pledges are paid only then.

PledgesWhat the realm forgets
AnyEvery trail in that realm goes cold
Two or moreEvery member whose Blue Rose secret is known in that realm is known again only to his own temple
ThreeHunters the Order is watching there forget the temple they learned, which makes breaking the Order harder for them

The Grand Magus pays 20 power, a wound that never kills, major stress and a year of Spent on Respondeo. It can be cast once in twenty years, counted from a success. A guarded mind keeps what it knows. Under the Silence Prerogative every pledger and the caster earn 15 standing. Result: A Realm Forgets.

The Vade Maecum Infernal#

A demon's handbook, torn into four pages long ago. Put back together, a Mage can bind himself to it with a Contract: magic without cost and a life that is very hard to end, at the price of his first son. The souls of everyone who failed are still inside it. New in 2.38.

It sits behind Supernatural: The Vade Maecum Infernal: Scattered (the default: three pages start in treasuries), Lost (the three are lost and turn up over the years) or Off (no page is ever made). Under Scattered and Lost the fourth page starts inside Silverback's hide. A save from before 2.38 counts as Scattered.

The four pages#

PageWhere it starts
The Black Stone PageWith the highest-ranking theocracy, sealed in black stone. Open the Black Stone unseals it, for a holder with the Mind Control perk and some power
The Sleeper's PageWith the most learned ruler. Whoever holds it has Dreams Not His Own: +1 learning, −10% stress gain
The Strongbox PageWith the highest-ranking republic
Silverback's PageInside Silverback's hide. A Silverback remade after being lost gets it back

Each page is illustrious and gives +1 learning and +5% magic lifestyle experience. Only an adult with no creature or hunter trait who holds no other page can be given one at the start; if nobody fits, the page is lost. A lost page turns up years later with an AI Mage.

Getting the fourth page out of Silverback. A Mage who owns the hide and is not wearing it can Draw the Page from the Hide; the page is his, and that hide will never have him (since 2.39 this works on any hide that holds a page, and the refusal sticks). From a living Champion he can Ask for the Page, which an AI Champion will almost never grant, or Tear the Hide Away, a hostile spell that costs a great deal of power. The Champion holds on at 20 plus half his Hide Bond (between 5 and 95). If he holds, the caster is wounded. If not, the page is torn out and the Champion is wounded, or killed at the caster's hand if he was already maimed or badly wounded.

Finding the pages#

  • Use Magic Triangulation gains Seek the pages of the book, which names whoever holds the book, or else a page.
  • The Necrophone: Where is a page of the book?
  • Send the Golem to Search: your golem is gone two to five years and comes back with a name six times in ten, or cracked and with nothing (Golems). Come Home ends a search early, with nothing.
  • Every Champion of the six hears each page that comes within his reach, once: since 2.40 The Hide Finds a Page (it was It Wants to Be Whole). Since 2.39 he must first know what the book is (a member of the Order, a witch or Mage, someone who knows a page's holder or holds one); otherwise, when a page is held within his reach, the hide tells him first in The Hide Remembers, at most once in five years, and he hears the pages from the next year on. The wolves who wore the hides swore that the book would never be used again, and the hide keeps that oath: it hunts the page, it does not want it, and each hide says why in its own way. Find it gives +5 Hide Bond, tells him who holds it and gives him a claim on it; Not now costs 5.
