One to four months after he finds it, The Trail Ends at [holder]'s Door: We will watch it (he keeps the claim), Take it tonight (a werewolf with On The Prowl or a hybrid with Master of the Hunt goes himself, a prowess duel; seen, the holder knows someone came and the county remembers a wolf), Send the pack (an alpha sends a wolf, who bears the wound if it goes wrong) or Tell the temple (a member: his Temple Magus learns who holds it). Since 2.40 the hide leaves alone a page held in its Champion's own pack, or by a Champion whose pack is bound to his by a Blood Pact. A Champion who is handed a page gets The Page Calls: guard it (+5 Hide Bond) or let a Mage have it (−5, −15 for Silverback). A Champion holding two pages, or one whose hide holds a page while another is near, is wounded once a year.
  • The book itself, once whole, points its holder at pages, the Seal of Solomon or a rival's relics.
  • Witchcraft's Other worlds and Necromancy research (A Name in Your Notes), and a Champion following the ringing to a caster who holds part of the book.

A name is a claim (2.40). Every way of learning who holds a page or the book now gives a personal claim on what he holds of it, so you can try to steal it back or go to war for it, and a page or the book is reason enough for Seize Their Relics. A Mage who tears a page out of a hide claims the page he tore. When a running game loads 2.40, everyone who already knows a page's holder gets the claim. When the book's voice shows its holder a rival's relics, he gets a claim on one of them too.

Binding the book, and the Contract#

Bind the Vade Maecum needs a Mage holding all four pages, unsealed, or the whole book, while nobody else has a Contract running. Its tooltip names the son the book will ask for before you start: the eldest living son of your blood, bastards you never knew of included, or for a woman her eldest son. With no such son the book cannot be paid, and the decision says so. The pages become one book, which is protected while the Contract runs (Zecchia and Seize Their Relics leave it alone), and The Book Names Your Son.

You then have three years, shown in your Situations as The Contract:

  1. Name as Witness a courtier, spouse or vassal who agrees. He carries Bound as Witness (+15% stress gain, −20% magic lifestyle experience, −1 learning) until he breaks it with Break the Witness's Bond or the last page is burned. Since 2.39, while any part of the book survives, his spells can go wrong (The Spell Goes Wrong): 9.5% for each page not burned, +6 while a Contract runs, −4 at Secret Knowledge 50 and −4 more at 100, between 3 and 38. A curse or domination that goes wrong usually wounds or curses the caster, and one time in ten kills the target (an AI caster's misfire only wounds a player); a healing can give the target cancer or reopen the wound; a blessing curses the land; a summoning can let a demon through. If he dies or leaves you, the step is undone. A council member who thinks well of a man who is not cruel may offer himself (A Councilor Offers).
  2. Give the Finger: A Finger Short, −1 prowess, for life.
  3. Pay the Last Price: your witness alive, the finger given, and your son at your court or in your prison. Pay it and he dies, and you are bound. Give it someone else (another son, or a golem) fails: the book will not be fooled.

Each step works magic that nearby mages feel, and leaves a witch mark on your county. If the time runs out, or the book leaves your hands anyway, or you die before the end, The Book Takes You: your soul goes into it, and it stays whole for its next holder.

Bound to the Book#

The one who pays gets the trait Bound to the Book for life, with Less Than Human (+15 dread, −10 opinion from everyone) and the Arrogant trait unless he is Humble.

  • +4 learning, +25 dread, no death of old age.
  • Magic costs him nothing: every spell and ritual that would spend power is free.
  • Demons obey him. Command your minion works on any demon, greater ones included, and an AI demon always accepts. Summon Demon always offers him the sure option, and Belial goes home instead of turning on him.
  • Murder barely touches him: hostile schemes against him are cut hard in success, ceiling and speed, and he is immune to the strongest curses, domination and mind reading.
  • In battle he is never picked for a knight's or commander's death or wound. Now and then the battle log notes that the blow found the man beside him (The Blow Turned Aside).

Ending him. The sure way is The Unmaking, a hostile interaction for the Grand Magus with two other council members alive and in his reach. It costs him a great deal of power and them a little, and the bound man dies. A hunt fight against him cannot be won without the Enchanted Blade, and even with it he is far stronger unless one of the six hides' Champions came on the hunt. When a bound man dies, by any road, his soul goes into the book.

Who comes for it, and how it ends#

  • While the book exists and no Contract runs, every Champion and every pack alpha in the holder's reach gets a claim on it, and Stinks of the Book (−40) toward him.
  • With the Order on, a bound master who is cruel (sadistic, callous, arbitrary, deceitful or a kinslayer) has every Temple Magus and every council member claiming it, and a cruel member who binds it has broken the Order's rules. An ambitious Grand Magus wants it whoever holds it.
  • The book counts for Seize Their Relics, except while a Contract runs.
  • Tear It Apart (a holder who is not bound, with no Contract running): the book becomes four pages again. Scatter them all, or give one to a Champion in reach, whose hide then keeps it.
  • Burn a Page: a page holder who is a Champion or has one among his courtiers or vassals. When all four are burned the book is gone for good, every witness is freed, and the burner and everyone who promised the voices to free them get They Are Free.
  • Destroyed by any other means, it falls apart into four scattered pages.

Whoever holds the whole book hears it: about one year in three a voice offers learning, a rival's secret, where something is, or the temptation to bind it. A player can read The Names in the Book, the souls it has taken.

Golems#

A golem is a made servant, bound to its master by Witchcraft's master and minion bond, which keeps it loving him whatever he does. It comes from the Order's Make a Golem or from Witchcraft's Artificial Human research. It does not die of age.

Shaping (2.40). Make a Golem now asks first for The Golem's Shape: I will create a Man., I will create a Woman. or I will create it to my own image. When it wakes, the maker names it in Finishing the Golem and picks one improvement: its mind, its combat, a magic source, or, for a maker with the Genius trait, all of them. A week later, Witchcraft's Final Calibration gives the golem 20 in one skill and the maker a perk point. These are the same steps Witchcraft's Artificial Human uses, so the Order no longer makes a blank golem called Titan.

A golem is never turned. Since 2.40 a golem cannot become a vampire, werewolf, hybrid or hunter by any route: the offers, the requests, the bite, the blood and the embrace decisions all refuse it. A hide never asks to be worn by one, Compel Mortal does not work on one, and Command your minion no longer offers a golem the order to end its life. A golem turned before 2.40 keeps both traits.

No soul to sell. Since 2.40 no golem carries Soul Forfeit, the mark of a soul sold to a demon, which Witchcraft's making used to give it. It is taken off every golem once, and nothing adds it again.

Last Instructions (2.40). When a golem's master dies, his heir (his player heir if he has one, otherwise his primary heir) gets Last Instructions: Then serve me binds the golem to him with the same bond, and makes it his golem puppet if he has none, or I have no use for it leaves it in place, answering to no one. A golem out on a search when its master dies comes home. Each year a masterless golem among a player's courtiers, guests or vassals whose last master has died is bound to him (The Golem Answers to You).

Masterless golems. On a running save, a player is offered Take Up the Golem's Instructions once for each masterless golem at his court, among his guests or direct vassals, or of his house, a golem that holds land included, and the search its dead master sent it on is ended. A Mage or a witch can also take up a masterless golem at any time with Bind the Masterless Golem (Friendly, players only): the golem must be where you are, at your court, your guest or in your realm, and it costs the power of a spell. It serves you as it served its maker, and becomes your golem puppet if you have none.

The Golem Came Back Wrong, when a search goes badly, now leaves the golem Cracked for two years: −10 prowess.

The golem puppet#

New in 2.40. A player's golem is his puppet, of the mod's own kind, Golem, for as long as the master's bond holds: it is set when the golem is made or handed on, and Make Your Golem a Puppet (Supernatural menu) sets it for a golem you already have. The Golem slot in the base game's Puppets list lists every golem bound to you, wherever it lives, a golem that holds land included. A master has one golem puppet at a time; choosing another sets the first aside, still bound to him and still answering to Command your minion. The slot shows only to a Mage or a witch, to someone who holds a golem's bond, and to someone offered a golem in the last ten years.

Right-click someone and act as your golem for the deeds any puppet can do, and one of its own: Stand Guard Over Them (anyone, of any age, you included) sets it to guard one person for a year. Plots against them go 20% worse, and any puppet sent to seize or strike them finds it much harder. It guards one person at a time. The base game's gift, befriending (which needs a golem with the Befriend perk), learning a language, and sharing and exposing the secrets it knows also work, and a golem that holds land can imprison, release, pardon, torture (a torturer or sadistic golem only), pay ransoms and give vassal directives, as a ruler does. Right-click yourself to have it guard you, send you its gold, seduce or romance you.

Its own orders are on its puppet card:

OrderWhat it does
Return to ClayEverything it carries, its gold and its possessions, comes to you. Then it is only clay; the death reads was returned to clay
Hand Over What You CarryIt gives you all its gold and every possession it can give
Come HomeIt returns to your court. If it is out searching, the search ends and it brings nothing back, and an errand is called off
What Did You See?It tells you every secret it knows that you do not, as Command your minion does
Guard MeIt keeps watch over you for a year, and plots against you go worse. It stops guarding anyone else
Send the Golem to SearchIt looks for the pages of the Vade Maecum, gone two to five years, as the interaction of the same name does. Not while it is on an errand

Orders that need a ruler stay in Command your minion.

What any puppet can do#

New in 2.40. Supernatural has three kinds of puppet in the base game's Puppets list: the golem, the Sire Bond and the Compelled. Right-click a character, choose your puppet in the "interact as" row, and these deeds are open to all three. Only players hold these puppets, and a puppet is never another player's character.

DeedCooldownWhat it does
Seize and Imprison5 years against the same personAn adult. Your puppet goes after them, and within one to three weeks either carries them to your prison or comes back alone. You need land or an administrative seat, and so a prison
Strike Them Down5 years against the same personAn adult. Your puppet goes after them; if its blows land they are wounded, and now and then killed, though a player never dies of it
Loom Over Them2 years against the same personAn adult, at once. They take stress, you gain a little dread, and they may give in to you: a weak hook, likelier on the craven and the paranoid, less likely on the brave and the landed
Keep Them Company2 years against the same personThe first time, they may take a liking to each other; the next, they become friends
Seduce, RomanceThe scheme's own rulesYour puppet starts the base game's seduction or romance scheme as its own, on someone else or on you
Order a MurderThe scheme's own rulesYour puppet plots the murder as a scheme of its own, with its own agents. Found out, it is your puppet's murder, and through it yours
Hand It OverNoneYour puppet lays out up to three of the items it carries, finest first, and the one you choose goes to the target. Never a worn item
Send Me Your GoldNoneOn yourself: 100, 500 or 1,000 gold from your puppet's own purse, each offered only when it can pay, or all of it

Reach. Seize, Strike and Loom need the target where your puppet stands, in your realm or your puppet's, or of your puppet's court or family, and your puppet must be free, not away searching or on another errand. After sending a puppet against anyone you wait 180 days before the next hostile deed, or 90 after Loom Over Them, and the base game's multiplayer rules against murdering players and their families block Seize and Strike too.

Who learns it was you. Seize and Strike go out as errands, and when the puppet comes back a hidden roll decides whether the victim learned who sent it. Your intrigue counts double, your puppet's counts once and the victim's counts against you, and the chance is 25 points lower when the victim lives at your own court; it runs from 5% to 75%. Known, you gain dread, and the victim holds a grudge against you that is also a reason to imprison you. Unknown, you gain no dread, the victim blames your puppet instead, and the message says the puppet bore no master's mark. A Strike can go unsigned whatever comes of it; a Seize only when the victim got away, since one in your prison knows whose prison it is. Loom Over Them is always signed: the point of it is that they give in to you.

The AI. The AI holds none of these puppets. Instead, once a year an AI master who is callous, sadistic or arrogant and has a rival or nemesis has one chance in ten to send his golem, or a lover he turned, to strike that rival through the same errand. A player struck this way is only told, and never dies of it.

Places of power and magic buildings#

Academy of MagicTemple of the Horned GodHut On The Chicken's LegHouska Castle

Three special buildings are the world's places of power: the Academy of Magic, the Temple of the Horned God and the Hut On The Chicken's Leg. None of them can be built from nothing. Each appears once, through an event, in some ruler's capital; you get one by holding that county. Holding one while landed opens the Arcane focus to a witch, adds 5 to every standard power gain, strengthens the Ultimate Spell, and makes it a summit venue and an expedition destination.

BuildingHow you get itWhat holding it does
Academy of MagicAppears with the Ancient Singularity event+3 learning per prestige level, +50% magic experience, the Learn new spell decision, +3 research starting progress
Temple of the Horned GodAppears in a random AI ruler's capital+2 intrigue per prestige level, +10 life expectancy, +25% Demonic experience, a fort and garrison, the Summon Demonic Army decision, and the Grand Rite every two and a half years
Hut On The Chicken's LegBaba Yaga's family brings it+10% magic experience, +10% county control, plots against you slower and weaker, Claim the Yaga title, and a Yaga can raise the hut itself as a regiment
Houska CastleThe Crown of Shadows event opens a slot for it in Litoměřice's capital; whoever holds that county can build it for 400 gold+1 learning per prestige level, +25% magic experience, +2 on every Demonology gain. Not a place of power
Mage's Study, then Laboratory, then SpireBuilt in a holding: 300, 600, then 900 gold and 1,000 prestige. Needs the Counter-spells perk or the Mage perk, or the Open-Minded Lords legacy for the upper two; the Laboratory does not accept the Mage perk alone, and the Spire needs the Mage Rulership tradition. One tower eachMagic experience +10% / +30% / +40%, wider diplomatic range, county control, development and income, and +3 / +4 / +5 research starting progress. The Study and Laboratory count as a tower for Magic Triangulation; as the files stand, the Spire does not

An estate has its own mage tower, four levels at 100 to 400 gold and the same in prestige. An arcane-focus character can build the first level; the rest need a true mage. Every level displays as "Mage's Laboratory". Levels 3 and 4 need you to be independent, under Mage Rulership, or on the Gnostic Council. It is worth +2 to +5 research starting progress and some magic experience, and it holds internal buildings of its own: a Triangulation Device (this is how a mage without a holding tower gets Magic Triangulation, and its second level makes it free), a Rune-Ward Cache and Spellbound Vault against artifact theft, an Affiliates Network, the Gnostic Council Archives for members, and Accursed Tomes if you own the Black Grimoire or a magical elaboration.

A landless adventurer gets the craft through a camp purpose, Secret Seekers. It costs 1,000 prestige to adopt, needs a witch, gives +10% magic experience, and is the route to all but one of the magic task contracts. Besides the base game's Scribes, Juicy Rumors and Subdued Gear upgrades, it unlocks two camp buildings: the Fortune Teller's Tent (gold, travel safety, free scrying) and the Cult Caravan, six levels of dread and conversion, which in turn opens the Portable Chapel (the camp can host the Grand Rite, and a better cult priest), Combat Equipment (the cultists regiment), Dark Missionaries (camp conversions and Spread the Cult) and the Cult Archives (+3 learning, +3 research starting progress). Leave the Seekers and the witch buildings are torn down.

The Arcana legacy#

Witchcraft's dynasty legacy, "Gathering lost knowledge and searching for true power." Five perks, priced like any other legacy:

PerkWhat it does
1. Open-Minded LordsNeeds a witch. Your dynasty makes better court mages, imprisoning someone can win you their magical artifact, and you can build the Mage's Laboratory and Spire without the Counter-spells perk.
2. Magic Theorists+5 starting progress on Magical Research, and the Esoteric Training decision.
3. Knowledge SharingEvery expedition destination is open to the whole dynasty, +10% magic experience, and the Mage Summit at half price.
4. Magic PractitionersNew mages start with 10 Secret Knowledge, and mages can cast Aura Reading.
5. Enchanted Blood+5 Magic Potential, once, for every member of the dynasty who has the Source when it is taken, a better chance of strengthening genetic traits, and stronger Source children after. If an AI takes it, that AI becomes a Mage.

Hooks, court positions and contracts#

Magic Domination is a strong hook that never expires. The Domination spell sets it, along with a master and minion relation, ends the victim's plots against you, and can turn their culture and faith to yours. It is what Command your minion uses. An AI mage at war will dominate a neighbouring ruler of his own rank and drag him in, up to four minions.

The AI needs a reason. Before 2.39 much of Witchcraft's AI acted on power or chance alone. Now:

  • Mage Duel: an AI challenges only for a grudge (it thinks −50 or less of you, you are rivals or nemeses, or at war), or, against someone it does not spare (friend, lover, spouse, family, or someone it likes), for Baba Yaga's feud, against a demon, out of greed for a magic item (a greedy Mage, or one with a claim on it), or, if ambitious, arrogant or sadistic, against a weaker Mage. Two members of the Order duel only over a grudge. Refuse a challenge and that AI gets Refused My Challenge (−10) toward you, and does not ask you again for five years.
  • Your magic items: one score of reasons decides every AI road to them; see who comes after your items. Domination is used on a player only by a rival, a nemesis or an enemy at war.
  • Demand Submission: the Grand Magus's version needs dislike, a rivalry or an ambitious or arrogant nature; the coven Grand Master's is a fifth as likely as before. A Grand Magus who is refused answers by his nature: shun you, forget it, or (if vengeful, wrathful or sadistic) have the Adeptus Major arrest you.
  • A demon's sudden rival is now someone in its reach whom it does not like, and can be any witch hunter, not only a player one.
  • Consume (the child sacrifice) is a fifth as likely, more for the sadistic and callous, never on the AI's own family, never on a player, and never on a player's child unless that player is its rival, nemesis or enemy.
  • Inquisitors join a war against any Mage now, not only a player one, but only if zealous or hostile to him, and then three times in ten.
  • Court positions (2.40): the AI never appoints a Court Mage, Dark Champion or cult priest who is at war with it.

Dark Champion is a court position that opens when you hold the Crown of Shadows or a demon's Deals with Mortals perk. The candidate needs a vice and something dark: a murderer, corrupted, a cannibal, a True Witch, a demon, a Mage, a hunter, or your minion. On the Special Mission task he can be sent to assassinate, plunder, abduct or rescue.

Appointing a Priest of the Cult makes him a witch

The camp officer that the Cult Caravan opens is made a witch on appointment, and learns your own witch secret. Choose him knowing that.

The fifteen magic task contracts (Secret Seekers camps; The Secret Library also under Scholars)
ContractWho can take itPays
Inconvenient WitchAnyonePrestige and provisions; failure costs prestige
Searching for IngredientsAnyone, for a herbalist or witch employerGold and prestige
Demonic PossessionA playerGold and prestige
Ruthless RivalryA playerGold and prestige
Task For The HealerA playerGold and prestige; failure costs nothing
Magic AnomalyA true mage with room for more power, once per dynastyPrestige and an unknown reward
The Secret ScrollA player, while no Ancient Scroll existsThe Ancient Scroll
Secret MissionA True Witch or true mage, once per dynasty200 gold and prestige
Monster HuntA player who is not a demonLarge gold, prestige and provisions
Haunted ManorA playerLarge gold and prestige
Rumors About a Magic ArtifactA player without a magic artifactWhere the artifact is; on a critical success, a claim on it
Lifting the CurseA playerGold and prestige
The Secret LibraryAnyonePrestige and a perk point or skills
Evaluation of the Court MageA witch, Mystic, True Witch, Mage or arcane focusGold and prestige; on a critical success, the court mage's seat and a fief
Carnivorous PlantsAnyoneGold, prestige and provisions

Most of these also pay provisions on success.

The endgame: crises and the two wars#

Witchcraft has an endgame, timed by the Witchcraft: Ending crises rule. At the default, "At any time", the first crisis can come ten years in; the other settings delay it 50 to 200 years, and Never turns it off. After that, each year there is a chance that a crisis starts, if a player has become powerful enough to provoke one:

  • The Grand Coalition: the world's leading witch hunter raises a coalition against a player Mage who rules an independent empire and has drawn too much attention. It never fires while the Witch Hunters Activity rule is on Low, which is the default.
  • Bael: the demon comes for a player mage past 50 Secret Knowledge who holds two magical artifacts, unless the Bael rule is off.
  • Return of the Grand Magus: Abdul Al-Hazred, author of the Black Grimoire, arrives to reclaim the title from a player who holds the Gnostic Council's Grand Magus seat, has 70 Secret Knowledge and two magical artifacts. He demands your artifacts, then sets out to destroy your dynasty: a summoned assassin, a curse on your heir, a double who sits in your council and spends your money, a bounty on your family. He can be stopped. Its rule's "Cannot appear" setting does not block achievements.

There are two casus belli. Demonic Invasion belongs to a Greater Demon with a full Reputation track, costs 2,000 prestige, and on victory takes the titles and every magical artifact, relic and page; a defender who has sold his soul is executed, anyone else goes to the dungeon. Losing it, the demon pays and is imprisoned. War Against the Witch cannot be declared: it comes from the Grand Coalition, or from a witch hunter's Stop The Witch when he chooses to raise a coalition. If the coalition wins, the witch's house loses its coven, every magical artifact, relic and page is taken, the witch is burned, and the witch's adult heir and every adult house member who is not an independent ruler go to the dungeon. A third, Seize Their Relics, came with 2.38: a player can declare it at any distance, and a greedy or ambitious AI Mage against a neighbour.

The smokeDemons and House Hellion#

A demon is a thing that turns up in a court, carries −20 opinion with everyone, and is very hard to be rid of. The mod has no underworld to visit: demons are met as characters.

Demonic HeritageMinor DemonGreater DemonCrossroad DealmakerDemonic lifestyle

Like the craft, most of this layer is upstream Witchcraft. What Supernatural added in 2.35 is the part that lets a hunter do something about it.

Becoming one#

Start as one: Minor Demon is in the ruler designer. Otherwise there are three roads. Be born to a demon: the child may carry Demonic Heritage, which turns into the real thing in their twenties. Carry unstable Source blood: a Magic Eruption answered with I must harness this power at all costs can end with you a demon. Or finish the Ascendancy magic special project. The rule Supernatural: Mortals Becoming Demons (new in 2.39) can close these roads to the AI: under Players Only no AI character ever becomes a demon, by heritage, eruption, Ascendancy or the AI's own research, and a demon-blooded AI stays demon-blooded. Summoned demons still appear. The default, Default (Everyone), is the old behaviour. A demon you meet may also have been summoned rather than made; that is a different thing (see Summoning).

The three demon traits#

TraitWhat it is
Demonic HeritageA dormant marker on a demon's child. +1 intrigue and nothing else: no immortality, no dread, no opinion penalty, and it is not a crime under any faith. It means this person may become a demon later.
Minor DemonThe real thing. Immortal, +10 health, +3 intrigue, +3 prowess, +10 baseline dread and −20 general opinion. It is what unlocks the Demonic lifestyle. Under a rite that shuns or criminalises witchcraft it carries a further −30 crime modifier and legal grounds to imprison, execute or banish.
Greater DemonRenames itself by its Hell Powers track, becoming Prince of Hell or Demon Princess at a full track. Below that it reads Greater Demon, then Demon above 69. It is what the demon tree's capstone grants, and taking it removes Minor Demon. It carries the same −20 general opinion.

Demons are opposite to every creature in the mod. Vampire, werewolf and hybrid all list them as incompatible, and a witch hunter is −30 to them.

Children#

Demons can have children. That is governed by its own game rule, Supernatural: Demon Inheritance/Pregnancy, separate from the werewolf one. At its default two demons have a 75 per cent chance of a demon-blooded child, and a demon with a human, a werewolf or a hybrid has 50. A Greater Demon parent adds 15 to either. That child is demon-blooded or human, never a wolf. Turn the rule off and demons rely on adoption, which is what the mod did before the rule existed.

Not every pregnancy holds. When either parent is a creature, some pregnancies end when they would start to show. Between two demons that is about seven in ten at first, and more with each demon-blooded child they have, so a demon couple raises a handful of children over a long life, not a dozen.

Summoning#

The door is the Summon Demon decision, which needs the black-magic tree's Summoner perk, enough stored magical power, and eighteen months since the last attempt. It is always a black-magic act.

The ritual can go wrong. The binding botches 30 per cent of the time. For a Mage with 35 or more on the demonology track that falls to 3. A botch gives you a raging demon and a choice: wrestle it, at a one-in-ten chance it kills you outright, or duel it on learning, where a loss is fatal one time in ten. Back out and you lose nothing but the attempt.

What you get, if it works, is rolled. Most of the time it is an unnamed minor demon. The rest of the table is named singletons: Belial, Lilith, Mammon, Samael, Moloch, Abaddon, Hecate, Nyx, Asmodeus, the Blob, Harmonius and Eshu, and each of them is gated on there being no living character already carrying that name. Kill Belial and Belial can be summoned again; leave him alive and his branch is dead weight. Several are player-only, and several need a Mage. Since 2.38 the table also holds Zecchia, a thief who comes back each time she is called. A man Bound to the Book is always offered the sure option.

A botched-but-recovered summoning can only ever produce a minor demon. The named branches all require a clean binding.

The named ones are not equal. Asmodeus is the extreme case: he arrives with a landless empire title, an army of demonic knights, 50,000 gold and five prestige levels. Lilith is the only one with a life of her own. She re-ages herself to eighteen every few years, heals whatever ails her, finds a new lover when her last one dies or leaves, and periodically schemes to murder his spouse. Killing her clears her blessing and puts her back in the summoning table. The chain never ends; it resets.

A failed binding produces a different creature entirely: a demon that escapes, becomes your nemesis, rivals your heir, takes an exile title and starts a murder scheme against you. As of 2.35 it also leaves a mark on the county, which is what lets a hunt come looking for it.

Zecchia#

A minor demon who steals for a fee, new in 2.38. She is one woman, a Minor Demon of House Hellion with the Source, greedy and fond of drink, who comes back each time she is called; if she is ever killed, the next summoning makes another. She moves things, never people or titles.

There are three ways to reach her:

  • Summon Demon. She is one more entry in the roll (8 of 137 when every entry is open), for a clean binding while she is not already working for someone. The first time, A Thief Answers.
  • Study the Black Grimoire can turn up A Name in the Margin: her name.
  • Summon Zecchia, a decision for anyone who knows her name, every two years: a Mage pays a standard spend of power, and a wearer of the Seal of Solomon pays nothing. Her name passes to your heir when you die. A member of the Order who calls her loses 10 standing (Rule One), unless the Order is treating with the Pit.

How Much Will You Take? One to five things; four and five need 400 and 500 gold in hand, and a Chancellor can put it on the Gnostic Council's funds (The Council will pay). She asks a personal price as well: a long drink (major stress and some gold), something of yours (your best ordinary artifact, or 100 prestige), or a lock of your hair. Then you have ninety days to point her at someone with Send Zecchia After Their Treasures.

Ninety days later, Zecchia Delivers: up to the agreed number of the target's items, the best first (for an AI hirer, the item he hired her for goes first), pledged and lent ones included. Since 2.39 the victim is told only "It was taken in the night. Nobody saw who took it.", and gets no claim on what she took, because the claim would have named the hirer. She will not take a hide, the Seal, the book while a Contract runs, a dynasty or house banner, or anything tied to a title. Her fee is paid then: 100 gold for each thing agreed, plus 50 to 200 for each thing she brought, by its rarity. The same hirer cannot set her on the same victim again for ten years. If you never name anyone, Zecchia Is Getting Bored: name them now, pay half and let her go, or ask for more than you agreed, in which case she robs you instead.

An AI Mage hires her only for a reason: an item he has a claim on, a page whose holder he knows, or a rival with something to take (who comes after your items). A later victim who goes to her while she holds your lock of hair can ask Who hired you against me?, and she tells him. Sending her after a member of your own temple, or one you know is a member, can mark you as having broken the Order's rules.

House Hellion#

Nearly every demon in the game belongs to one house. The dynasty is displayed as Hell and the house as Hellion, two different words for the same family, which is the single most confusing thing about this content. A second house, Maledictio, belongs to the same dynasty and holds one demon. Both carry a white-and-red snake on black.

Sixteen of the seventeen demon templates place their character into House Hellion, which is why the house grows over a long campaign: every summoning, every escaped binding, every demonic courtier adds a member.

Fixed in 2.35 (10 September 2026). The invented-witch template used by witch hunts also placed its character in House Hellion, so every woman a hunt turned up joined the demons' house. A live campaign came back with 246 living members, almost all of them invented witches. Witches are now born without a dynasty. Demons keep the house.

The Demonic lifestyle#

Seventeen perks in one tree with two arms, one for war and one for guile, meeting at a root that hands out +10 to every skill and +10 prowess, the largest flat stat block anywhere in the mod. The war arm ends at Greater Demon; the guile arm ends at Crossroad Dealmaker and the cyrograph.

Two focuses, Manipulation and Destruction, and both carry +30 per cent stress gain. The Demonic lifestyle is the only one in the mod that taxes you for being in it.

It is also designed to be finished and abandoned: taking both capstones clears your focus and closes both focuses to you permanently. And because summoned demons are handed all seventeen perks the moment they appear, the lifestyle is in practice only for a mortal who became a demon. A Prince of Hell has already finished it before you meet him.

Exorcism#

Before 2.35 the mod's only answer to a demon was killing it, and demons are immortal. Upstream's Banish Demon spell does not banish. It kills, and every greater demon is immune to it. Meanwhile a greater demon can recruit another demon into its court every four years, forever. The population compounded and had no drain.

Exorcism is that drain, and it is the hunter's verb rather than the mage's: it costs no magical power at all. It sits inside the demon hunt, as one of three things you can do at the confrontation.

The numbers: what an exorcism is worth to attempt

The chance starts at 40 and is clamped between 5 and 90.

TermValue
Monster-Hunter track+0.5 each, so up to +50
Witch-Hunter track+0.2 each, so up to +20
Caught mid-feeding+15
One other hunter attending+10
Two or more other hunters+20 in total
The demon is a greater demon−25
Its Hell Powers−0.2 each, so down to −20
Its reputation−0.1 each, so down to −10

A career hunter against a plain minor demon is capped at 90. The same man against a Prince at high powers is about 50. A green hunter against that same Prince is 8.

The witch-hunting experience, 5 to 12, is paid either way. A man learns the words by saying them at something that did not listen just as surely as at something that did.

What success looks like depends on what the demon is. A summoned demon was never a person, and the body it was wearing simply collapses. A made demon, a mortal who was turned, has someone underneath, and the exorcism gives them back. The game answers that question by asking whether the demon has a father and a house of its own, because every summoned template is fatherless and Hellion.

Named demons cannot be exorcised.

Failing does not kill you. It drops you into an ordinary fight at a 20-point penalty, with the way out still open.

After a failed exorcism there are two other ways out. Breaking it off, Hit it hard enough to get out of the doorway, ends the hunt as a withdrawal: you spend a year recovering at −2 prowess, the demon becomes your rival and hates you for a decade, and it is either pushed into a neighbouring province or, if it holds land and cannot move, marked as driven off for five years. The county's trail is wiped either way, and the county still counts one refusal. And the bargain is the one where you sign its cyrograph: you are healed, de-aged and improved across every skill, and it takes your soul on a ten-, twenty- or thirty-year timer. A hunter who later learns to put demons down will refuse to pay when the bill arrives, and the demon has a war that executes soulless defenders outright.

The hunt

The people who hunt these things, and the trail, the three weeks and the door.

HuntersThe hunt

The other sideHunters#

A hunter is the only playable role in the mod that is not a monster, and the game will not let you be both. Vampire, werewolf, hybrid, demon, witch, necromancer and warlock are all written into the Hunter trait as opposites, so the engine refuses the pair outright.

HunterKnown HunterTrue Witch HunterHunter lifestyle

The trait gives +3 diplomacy, +2 learning, +4 prowess, +2 advantage and +5 life expectancy, and it has two tracks rather than one. They are not variations on a theme:

TrackWhat filling it makes you
Monster-HunterA fighter. Across the full track: +12 prowess, +10 diplomacy, +5 advantage, +4 learning, +15% knight effectiveness, +10 general opinion and 15% cheaper holy orders. It is what the hunt itself reads at almost every point.
Witch-HunterA schemer. +14 on hostile scheme success, +10 baseline dread, +8 life expectancy, doubled diplomatic range, better piety, and four supply-cancelling terrain traits. It is what exorcism and the witch hunt read.

A hunter is never born one. The routes in are the ruler designer (100 points), the Supernatural: Trait Distribution roll on AI adults at game start and again each year, training, a handful of story events, and the Become a Hunter decision, which needs the Supernatural: Embrace Decisions rule set to On.

The numbers: every rung of both tracks

Both tracks have rungs at 25, 50, 75 and 100, and each rung adds 5% hunter lifestyle experience.

RungMonster-HunterWitch-Hunter
25+2 diplomacy, +3 prowess, +2 advantage+2 intrigue, +2 learning, +2 stewardship, +4 hostile scheme success, +4 life expectancy, two terrain supply traits
50+3 diplomacy, +3 prowess, +1 advantage, +15% knight effectiveness, +10 general opinion+2 learning, +3 prowess, +4 life expectancy, a terrain supply trait
75+2 diplomacy, +3 prowess, +1 advantage, +4 learning, −15% holy order hire cost+3 stewardship, +1 advantage, +10 hostile scheme success, +10% piety, +10 baseline dread
100+3 diplomacy, +3 prowess, +1 advantage+2 diplomacy, +1 advantage, doubled diplomatic range, a terrain supply trait

Two of those story events are how word of a witch or a monster reaches a court. Witch Spotted opens in a market, and only for a ruler whose rite does not accept witchcraft; Monster Sighting opens in a tavern. Witch Spotted looks for a real witch before it invents one: the culprit of a witch trail in the county, then someone at the ruler's own court, the local lord's court, the lord himself, the liege's court, and a witch whose secret the ruler knows. Burn the witch at the end of the first, or kill the creature at the end of the second, and a character who was not a hunter becomes one. A hunter gets one or the other in up to one year in five. Everyone else gets them in fewer than one year in fifty. That split came in 2.35a: 2.35 briefly gave the whole map the hunters' rate, and the world filled with new hunters and werewolves.

Training one#

Two interactions, and neither costs anything. Train as a Hunter is the offer. Ask to be trained as a Hunter is the mirror, and it is player-only: the AI never asks, and to use it you must already know the other person is a hunter.

The trainee must be at least 13, must not already be partway through someone else's training, and must not have turned you down in the last five years. All three are recent. It used to be possible to train a four-year-old, and pressing the button twice used to queue two copies of the whole chain onto the same person.

Training starts with the specialisation, monsters or witches. It then runs 75 to 115 days before the event that decides whether it took. The base odds are four in five, better for the young, strong and healthy. A failed roll wounds the trainee and leaves them an apprentice for ten years. Nobody dies of training. An AI hunter only ever offers this to someone who already likes them.

An apprentice can be brought on a hunt hosted by their trainer. If that hunt finds its creature or drives it off, they graduate at the door with a head start on the track. If it ends in a withdrawal they do not: there was no lesson in it.

The lifestyle#

Two focuses, Monster Hunter and Witch-Hunter, worth +3 martial and +3 intrigue respectively, and those attribute points are the whole mechanical payload of a focus here. Everything else in a focus is the AI deciding what kind of hunter it wants to be.

Two trees of eight perks each. The Monster Hunter tree has two roots that converge on The Witcher. The perk adds +35 life expectancy and grants the True Monster Hunter trait: +5 prowess, +3 martial and half a prestige a month. Stacked with Built Different's +10 and the trait's own +5, a finished monster hunter lives fifty years longer than he would have.

The Witch-Hunter tree ends at the Witch-Hunter perk, which grants the True Witch Hunter trait. Several of its perks unlock things:

  • Anti-Mage, the root, opens witch trials and the old witch-hunting decision.
  • Combat Spells reaches the Witcher's elixirs, letting a hunter brew potions without being a witch.
  • Protection Runes grants the witch tree's own protective perk, which more than thirty magic gates read. It did nothing at all until it was noticed that the two perks' names differ by one letter.
  • Hunter Academy unlocks the regiment.

A hunter can never take a witch, mage or warlock capstone. The perks refuse to be picked rather than failing silently, which is what they used to do.

Hunter Trainees#

The hunter's regiment. Skirmishers, 50 to a stack and ten sub-regiments at the cap, needing both the Hunter trait and the Hunter Academy perk to raise or reinforce, so an heir can field an inherited regiment but cannot replace its losses until he has earned the perk himself.

They do not out-fight monsters and are not meant to. One vampire sub-regiment hits for 1,850 and takes 1,750; a full trainee stack hits for 700 and takes 900. The counter is the point: a quarter against monsters and animals, and a tenth against demonic knights. A maxed hunter's guard neutralises one monster regiment, not an army of them.

Fielding them is also one of the ways to bring the county's men to the door.

Being known#

Hunters have secrets too. Depending on what your rite, or a liege's above you, holds about hunters, you either carry a hidden Hunter secret or the open Known Hunter trait. The trait is +15 opinion between known hunters and −20 with vampires, werewolves and hybrids, and gives a rite that favours creatures a crime to charge you with.

A rite takes the same four positions on hunters as it does on each kind of creature. What your rite thinks of you has them, and The known traits compares all five.

The trailThe hunt#

The hunt gives each kind of creature a way of leaving evidence in the county it fed in, and builds a three-week activity around a hunter riding there to find out what it is and who it is. Both sides are playable. The creature gets a screen at every point the hunter does.

Trails and what leaves them#

Everything here runs on one thing: a county that carries Something Stalks Here. The modifier is worth −5 county opinion and nothing else, and it does not suppress growth. The cost of a creature living somewhere is the drain each incident does, not a standing penalty.

A county holds four facts under that modifier: who the culprit is, what kind it is, how many incidents there have been, and whether the lord has been told. The kind is read by a hunter who has learned to read wounds when he plans a hunt, and by your Trailsman in the Supernatural Trails window; everyone else sees a county with a problem.

The first incident is usually only a whisper. It leaves no modifier, no map pin, no culprit and nothing a Trailsman can read, and it expires in a year. A second incident inside that year opens a real trail immediately. A first incident can also be noticed outright on a roll: 30 per cent for a body, 12 per cent for a prowl. Once a trail is open, every later incident escalates it without a roll, up to a cap of six.

The numbers: what each incident costs the county
KindA killA prowl
Vampire−5 county control−1 control
Werewolf−2 control, −1 prosperity−1 control
Demon−4 control, −1 prosperity−2 control
Witch−2 control, −1 prosperity−1 control

A demon does not feed, it ruins, so the county pays for it twice. A witch is the quietest of the four. The county loses faith in its lord faster than it loses people.

What counts as an incident. Killing on a feed, a feed somebody talks about (a witness who got away, a compulsion that did not take, or about one clean feed in ten), a werewolf's border run, a demon escaping a botched binding, a witch's plague spell, the corruption scheme, a body you chose to leave as your own, and a body you pinned on someone else. Roughly two dozen places in the game write a mark. Since 2.36 a feed nobody talks about leaves no mark at all, and the first warning that a county remembers you reads differently when what it remembers is talk rather than a body.

What does not. A death in a tournament. A death in war, or with either party in an army. Killing a prisoner. And any death inside a hunt. A body that turns up inside a supernatural hunt is the hunt's business. Hunters never leave marks, because a hunter is not a creature. Since 2.35a the killer also has to be in the same province as the victim, so a murder scheme carried out from somewhere else no longer counts as a creature's kill. A murder in your own hall still does.

Forgetting. There is no decay event. The modifier is added for five years and refreshed on every incident, so a trail dies five years after the last thing that happened. You can shorten it deliberately: leaving the county clears one mark, paying the lord off clears one and leaves him a debt he will remember if a hunter later proves what was living there, and a compulsion in a county carrying your own trail can bury an incident outright. Since 2.40 Timber's Champion can wipe out the trail that points to him with Cover Your Trail, and a sire-bound lover can talk the lord out of one mark (Cover My Tracks).

And a trail whose culprit has died goes cold: no hunt can ever answer it. When the culprit dies the game looks for someone in that county who could plausibly have been doing it, whether a creature or a witch in the holder's court or the holder himself, and hands the trail to them, re-deriving the kind. If there is nobody, the trail is wiped.

A census on a live 346 AC save, taken before that change, found 23 trails in the world and only four of them warm: nineteen counties carried a mark no hunter could answer, for five years each.

Supernatural Trails#

New in 2.40. Supernatural Trails is a window listing every county that carries Something Stalks Here. Open it with the Trails button in the column of Supernatural buttons on the left of the screen. The button shows only to those who keep count of trails: vampires, werewolves, hybrids and demons, witches, Mages, members of the Order and hunters. The trails themselves are no secret, since every county listed shows its modifier to anyone who looks.

The window groups the trails by how bad they are, worst first:

BandWhat it means
BloodiedA death is on record: a killing while the trail was open, or one in the year of talk that opened it
TroubledThree or more incidents on record, and no death. At the third the county's lord is sent word, unless the culprit holds the county himself
RumouredOne or two incidents on record, and no death

Each row gives the county and its holder (its coat of arms opens the county), the band and the number of incidents, which anyone may know. Two more lines need a reason to know them:

  • Left by (a vampire, a werewolf, a demon or a witch) and Last seen (the year of its latest incident) are shown for every trail while you have a Trailsman in your court or camp.
  • Without one, you can read only your own trails, which say Left by: you with the year, and a trail left by a living member of your pack, coven or temple whose nature you know, which shows what left it but not when.
  • Everything else reads unknown. The culprit is never named.

A line under the window's introduction says which of these applies to you. Trails opened before 2.40 show Last seen: unknown until their next incident.

A hunter who can host the hunt (a grown hunter who holds land or leads a band of adventurers) gets Plan a Supernatural Hunt, which opens the hunt; when he chooses where to ride, the trail counties he can hunt are the ones he can pick. Anyone else is told who can ride after a trail. The hunt's planner is the hunter reading the ground for himself, so a skilled hunter still sees there what kind of creature left a trail, with or without a Trailsman.

The list is read fresh each time you open it. A trail leaves it when it goes cold, five years after its last incident, or when it is cleared. The game also keeps the three bands as a coloured map in a Situation, but players do not see it in the Situations window; in observer mode it is there, with its map. Since 2.40 the column of buttons closes up, so the buttons you have sit together with no gaps, and the Gnostic Council's button sits at its foot, no longer hidden behind the Order's.

Riding out#

The Supernatural Hunt is an activity. You need the Hunter trait, you must not be recovering from your last one, and there must be something within reach worth hunting. The button greys out and tells you which of those is missing. Since 2.35a a hunt ends if its host is not a hunter.

It costs half what a vanilla activity of its size costs, an eighth for a landless adventurer, and nothing if you are riding under a contract. The cooldown is two years.

There are two kinds. A Monster Hunt needs a county that carries a trail left by a living creature. A Witch Hunt can be held anywhere in your own lands, a vassal's or your liege's, and if you ride it to a county that carries a witch trail with a live culprit, it runs the whole monster spine instead of the thin version. Since 2.40 the thin version always opens with A List of Names in the hunt's window: the county's grudges, and somebody on the list may be real. If the list led to a real woman, the conclusion says so. The planner's description of the Witch Hunt explains both versions.

Where you can ride. A hunt is held at a county's capital, because a trail is a fact about a county. You can reach anywhere within your court's ordinary range, or anywhere at all under your own top liege, so a duke can hunt across the kingdom, a king across the empire. A landless adventurer's reach is five times a landed hunter's radius.

What you are allowed to hunt. A creature that has beaten hunters before is gated by reputation. A legend of 2 or more wants a Monster-Hunter track of 40, or two hunters riding with you. A legend of 4 or more wants a track of 75, or 40 and three hunters. The hunters counted are the ones in your court. A landless hunter, or one with fewer than two trails in his realm, is let through either gate. And a county that has been hunted in the last four years is closed while it catches its breath.

What you take with you. One kit and one approach:

ChoiceWhat it does
Travel LightNothing. The default.
Stakes+15 at the door against a vampire or a hybrid, and neither can look you in the eye. −10 against a werewolf.
Silver+15 against a werewolf or a hybrid. −10 against a vampire, and a vampire or hybrid of any rank can look you in the eye.
Hire Local Guides+15 tracking.
Ask QuietlyNo change. The creatures may still hear that a stranger is asking.
Ride In Openly+10 tracking. Every creature in the county will hear of you.
Read the Signs+5 tracking, +5 at the door.

Against a hybrid either kit works: stakes and silver are each worth +15. An AI hunter who can read the trail picks the right kit; one who cannot, guesses.

Up to four other hunters can ride with you, drawn from your friends, family, court and the hunters whose secret you keep. Each one is worth +10 on the tracking bar up to three, and another blade at the door. An apprentice who rides with their trainer can finish their training on the hunt.

The three weeks#

The tracking phase runs three weeks and the whole of it feeds one number, shown as a Tracking bar with its own breakdown. It starts at 35 and is clamped between 5 and 95.

The numbers: every term in the tracking bar
TermValue
Base35
Your Monster-Hunter track+0.5 each, up to +50
Your reputation+3 per rung. Only the first rung can be reached at present (Known issues).
The creature's legend−3 per rung. Four rungs can be reached, so down to −12.
Hunters riding with you+10 each, up to +30
Local guides+15
A county that remembers too little+8
Riding in openly+10 (reading the signs, +5, unless a local hunter rides with you)
A local hunter riding with you+10
You know what the creature is+20
The creature's power−0.25 each, down to −25
The trail has gone quiet: it left the county−25
The culprit is a hide's Champion−15, and −10 more for Timber's Champion in no pack
You keep a hide yourself, and the culprit is a wolf+10
What the three weeks taught youwhatever the events added

What fills those three weeks is a weekly pool of events. A survivor who can be listened to or pressed. What the graves say. A mob that has picked the wrong man: let him go, or let them have him for 25 prestige and two years of a county that remembers. A fellow hunter's tale. A county so crowded with creatures that you can be counted yourself. Another hunter already working the same trail, who can be ridden with, questioned, or warned off.

The field events are the ones that read the kind. By the Moon tells you when a wolf will be out, worth +20, or you can warn the county and lose ground instead. Not a Drop points a vampire hunt at the people who survived. Two Different Animals is the hybrid's tell, and getting it right is worth 15 while guessing two creatures costs you. They Are All Very Sure is a county that has been compelled, so stop asking what happened and start asking what they cannot remember.

And when your bar reaches 60 on a monster hunt, the creature is told, wherever it is. He Asked About You By Name gives it three choices: run now, and the hunt ends with nothing but two years of not feeding; misdirect him on an intrigue duel, which takes 20 off everything he has built; or let him come. A demon gets It Knows Your Name instead, and argues.

The door, from both sides#

The confrontation phase opens on Cornered. If the county is crowded, you are told how many will stand with the thing before you choose how to go in:

  • Strike now.
  • Wait for daylight: +15 against a vampire or hybrid, nothing against a wolf.
  • Bring the county's men: +10, if the lord likes you, you field Hunter Trainees, or the county pledged its men during the tracking.
  • This is practically a nest: always available. You withdraw, lose 25 prestige, and the county counts a refusal against you.

Then the creature answers, in Someone Is Asking Questions. There are eight answers, and most have a condition:

AnswerWhat happens
Let them comeThe fight.
Go to groundAn intrigue duel. Hidden: you are gone, one mark comes off, your kin are warned, and you cannot feed for two years. Found: the hunter goes into the fight +15 up.
There is worse than me in this landA diplomacy duel, available only when a neighbouring county is carrying three or more incidents from another living creature. You do not deny what you are; you tell him where the bodies are stacking higher, and it is true. He leaves and rides there instead. Refused, you have told a hunter you keep count, and the fight is harder.
Look them in the eyeVampires and hybrids only, and never if he brought stakes. If he brought silver it is open at any rank. Otherwise you need a full Vampirism track and a hunter below 40 on his Monster-Hunter track. Costs 20 track experience and pays 8 to 15 back. He turns around.
Let it outWerewolves and hybrids. Thirty days in full wolf form, +15 in the fight, and every hunter at the door learns what you are. For a hide's Champion it kills the hunter with no fight.
Send [name]Your progeny, or failing that the one you clawed, goes instead. A player in that position gets their own screen, and can refuse.
Make them an offerNeeds a track of 75 and 100 gold in hand. He gets 50 gold and a stipend for five years, and you get a strong hook on him. If he refuses, he gains 25 prestige and five points in the fight.
[Champion] stands over meOnly while Silverback's Champion has his guard on you through Stand Over Them. He fights the hunter in your place.
The numbers: how the fight is scored

A prowess duel, both sides starting at 50 and floored at 10.

The creatureThe hunter
Prowess difference, up to ±25The same, reversed
Its track × 0.6, up to +60Monster-Hunter track × 0.6, up to +60
+15 shapeshifted, or Evolved and not shifted+10 for True Monster Hunter
+10 hybridEverything he brought (kit, daylight, the county's men, a local hunter, a refused bargain) comes off the creature's side
+10 forewarned
−12 per kin standing with it, −6 per stranger, first three only

A hide's Champion then multiplies his whole side by his Hide Strength, ×1.5 to ×3. A True Alpha with no hide multiplies his by 1.25.

Losing does not have to be the end. Overmatched lets you fall back for a wound, a year recovering and some experience, or press on at a further 20-point penalty, where losing means you do not leave.

Since 2.40 Overmatched, Gone, Turned Back, This Is Not The Man, The Price of Silence and the decision on the witch's fate open in the hunt's own window while the hunt runs, and on the map once it has ended. The text at the door counts the hunters you brought: alone, with another hunter, or with a band. A player creature who has not answered Someone Is Asking Questions gets about a week more, once, before the hunt goes on without him.

Winning gives you a screen of your own, It Is Done. You can finish it, or take it alive if you hold land.

Who stands with the creature#

When a hunt reaches its end in a county, the game walks that county's holder, courtiers, guests and any player standing there, and asks of each one: are you a creature, are you not a demon, are you not going to ground yourself, and do you have a reason to stand. Kin qualifies, meaning anyone linked by claw or by blood, and since 2.37 a wolf of the same pack. So does the defender being a legend, and so does not disliking them. Vampires stand with werewolves under the same rule; kind does not matter at the door.

Only the first three joiners change the fight, and only by a fixed amount: kin are worth 12 each, strangers 6, so three kin take 36 points off the hunter's side. A fourth joins the tally only if the defender is a legend in his own right. For scale, a hunter's kit is worth 15, daylight 15, the county's own men 10, and each hunter who came with him 5.

Going to ground sends word up and down the line. You can warn your get, warn the one who made you, warn all of them, say nothing, or point the hunter at your own progeny, which writes a real mark on their county with their name on it and costs you stress to do. Werewolf kin travel the same machinery as vampire bloodlines here: where the code looks for a progeny and finds none, it falls back to whoever you clawed.

What the hunt was worth#

Eleven outcomes, and the conclusion screen, What the Hunt Was Worth, has its own text for each. Since 2.40 a player who rode as a guest gets an ending of his own, Riding Home. The county's recovery is scaled to what was living in it:

OutcomeThe countyThe hunter
Slain or exorcisedVampire or hybrid: +5 control. Demon: +4 and a point of prosperity. Werewolf or witch: +2 and a point. The whole trail is removed.Prestige scaled by what it was, 15 to 35 Monster-Hunter, and the bounty. Guests take 5 to 15.
Proxy slain+1 or +2 control, one mark cleared, the culprit still out thereThree quarters of the prestige, 10 to 25 track
FledNothing. The trail stays.+25 prestige, 5 to 10 track
WithdrewA refusal counted against the county, unless you walked away in the first week, which costs nothing3 to 8 track. Apprentices do not graduate; there was no lesson in it.
ColdNothing5 to 10 track
CompelledNothing. The trail stays.3 to 8 track
BoughtNothing. The trail stays.The creature's gold and stipend, 5 to 10 track
Hunter beatenNothing. The trail stays.Medium stress
WitchNothing10 to 20 Witch-Hunter track
BargainedNothing. The trail stays.Medium stress, and the bargain he signed

The bounty starts at 40, adds five per point of county development and a little over one per point of the creature's power, and is floored at 40 and capped at 320. Against a hunt that costs about 45, a poor county's ordinary vampire pays around 90 and a rich city's elder pays a few hundred.

A refusal is a hunter who withdraws at the door, or dies there. Three of them inside ten years and the county picks up No One Will Come for ten years: −8 county opinion and slower control growth. AI hunters then stay away unless their Monster-Hunter track is 75 or more; a player can still ride there. The creature it failed to catch gets They Stopped Coming, which adds to its dread and its legend.

Killing a culprit can leave children behind, and the hunt asks what you do about them.

Names the world uses#

Two reputations come out of a hunt, and both are read off what the characters remember doing. A hunter's name climbs at one, two, three, five and eight creatures slain. A creature's legend climbs by besting hunters and, worth double, by killing them. Each can end in a nickname:

NicknameWhoHow
the UnhuntedA creatureA legend of three, checked when it beats a hunter. From then on only serious hunters are allowed to come for it.
the BeastbaneA hunterA name of three. It cannot be earned at present: a hunter only records his first kill, so his name stops at the first rung (Known issues).
the WolfA hide's ChampionTen knights killed in battle, if he has no other nickname.

A fourth nickname in the files, "the Timeless", has no text and is granted by nothing.

Covering your own kills#

When a player's creature kills someone in person, the next morning brings What They Will Find, and up to four answers:

  • Make it look like nothing. A roll on your strength and your talent for lying. Clean, and there is no mark at all.
  • Find whoever would have had to write this down. No roll, and it costs 40 to 250 gold by the county's wealth. The lord takes your money, the county takes a mark off, and he remembers who paid.
  • Let them look at [name]. Offered when there is someone to frame. The same roll fifteen points harder, and a success writes the mark against another creature carrying their kind, so a vampire who dresses a body as a wolf's work sends the county's next hunter to a wolf's door. You cannot frame your own maker, progeny or the one you clawed.
  • Let them know it was me. No roll. The trail is yours and it is loud: three incidents at once, and every soul in the lord's hall is told what walked through. Hunters will come.

A botched feeding has its own answers, one of which is to silence the witness.

Compulsion works the other way. It is not an incident. Used in a county carrying your own trail it cleans, at 30 per cent with no power behind it and up to 80 at a full track. Used badly, and low power is the main way to be sloppy, it leaves a hole instead. Three holes inside five years and the county picks up Nobody Here Remembers, and its lord starts asking why, which is its own investigation and can end with a real trail carrying your name. A favour asked carelessly through the Sire Bond can leave the same hole.

The Trailsman#

A court position, and a camp officer version for landless adventurers. The employer does not have to be a hunter.

The Trailsman walks your counties and reads what has been left in them. Bodies found at the edge of a village, herds taken in a way no wolf takes them, a road nobody will use after dark. He goes and looks, and he comes back and tells you where. A good one comes back with a name.

Aptitude runs on prowess and martial, with +35 for holding the Hunter trait (the tooltip calls it "Has hunted one before"), +15 for a True Witch Hunter who lacks it, +15 for a lifestyle hunter, +10 for patience, +5 for Athletic at court, and −40 for being a creature, because a creature does not report on its own kind. The AI appoints one too, and is far keener to when a county in the realm carries a trail.

Three tasks:

TaskWhat it doesChance a year
Read the CountrysideFinds a trail you have not been told about. Your priests only write once a county has lost three people; he goes before that.40, up to 70 at high aptitude
Follow the TrailPuts a face to a trail you already know about, and the only way short of hunting it yourself. Costs double.20, up to 65
Beat the BoundsCounty opinion and faster control recovery, scaling with aptitude. Finds nothing. It is the standing answer to what a trail drains.—

Since 2.40 a Trailsman in your service also reads the Supernatural Trails window for you: what left each trail and when it was last seen. Without one you can tell only your own trails and those of a known member of your pack, coven or temple.

He will not report a trail while your hunt is on cooldown, so you are not told about a monster you cannot ride for.

Summons, bounties and contracts#

On the third incident, the county's lord is told. Something Stalks gives him five answers: look into it himself, put his spymaster on it, post a bounty, send for a hunter, or write it off as superstition and keep bleeding control.

Looking into it is an intrigue duel against how well the creature hides, which scales with its power. Winning reveals the culprit to him by name. The spymaster runs the same duel with a bonus for being a schemer, and a Trailsman turns the compelled-county version into a far better one.

Post a bounty creates a real task contract that any landless adventurer hunter can take off the board: the county's menace, with a purse on it. That needs the landless expansion.

Send for a hunter needs nothing. Three letters go out with names on them, and the lord is shown three men in the order of what they are asking for. The roster is built nearest first: the man in your own hall, then the men who owe you, then the world. The fee starts at 60 and rises with the hunter's Monster-Hunter track, between 40 and 150 in all. It does not depend on the lord's purse. The hunter can accept, refuse, or write back naming a higher price, which doubles it.

A county can only be answered once, because the first lord to write stamps it for ten years, so two lords can never both have a man on the same trail. And a hunter who holds both a bounty contract and a summons for the same county is paid twice, which is deliberate.

Across every kind

What applies to every creature: secrets, what a rite makes of you, lifestyles and regiments.

Secrets, faith and fulfillmentLifestylesRegiments

Being found outSecrets, faith and fulfillment#

Every creature and every hunter lives behind a secret until it is known. What happens then depends on the rite: each rite takes a position on each kind, and that position decides the opinion, the crime and, since 2.36, what your own rite makes of how you live.

Secrets and exposure#

What happens when you turn depends on what your rite thinks of you. The rule is the same for every kind; a vampire is the example here. If your rite, or a liege's above you, shuns or criminalises vampirism, you get a hidden secret, and the people who made you are told about it so your own spymaster stops rediscovering it forever. If nobody around you cares, you get nothing at all, and you go on being a vampire that the game has no public record of.

There is a slow fuse as well, for every immortal. A supernatural who has been immortal more than eighty-five years and is over a hundred and twenty-five rolls three per cent a pulse that somebody finally works out why the lord has not aged.

Membership of the Order of the Blue Rose is a secret of the same kind, Member of the Hermetic Order. Exposed, it marks the county the member is in for witch hunters.

The known traits#

Each kind has an open trait for the state after the secret: Known Vampire, Known Werewolf, Known Hybrid and Known Hunter. The opinion on a known trait applies only between two holders of the same trait, and against the one enemy the trait names. What everyone else thinks comes from the rite, and is the same for every kind.

Known traitWith others who hold itWith its enemyBaseline dread
Known Vampire+20−15 with hunters+15
Known Werewolf+25−30 with huntersnone
Known Hybrid+35−10 with hunters+20
Known Hunter+15−20 with vampires, werewolves and hybridsnone
A demonNo known trait. The demon traits carry −20 general opinion from everyone, known or not.

Being known costs a vampire or hybrid at the table too: −15 on every feeding success roll, and an AI vampire in that position is far less willing to host one. A known Champion carries extra dread.

What your rite thinks of you#

Each rite takes one of four positions on each kind of creature, set as a doctrine: Criminal, Shunned, Accepted or Virtuous. Since CK3 1.20 these sit on the rite rather than the faith, so two rites of one faith can disagree, and a new rite gets them too. Criminal is −30 opinion from everyone not of your kind and a legal reason to imprison or execute; Shunned is −15. Under Criminal, since 2.36, a known creature is a sin of that rite as well, which costs it another 20 with the faithful. Virtuous makes Known Vampire a virtue of the rite and costs a great deal of piety to adopt.

Your rite and fulfillment#

Crusader Kings III 1.20 added Spiritual Fulfillment, a measure of how well a character's life fits their faith. Since 2.36 a creature's own rite weighs three of the things it does: a feed the victim lives through, a kill by fang or claw, and turning someone into your own kind. This applies to vampires, werewolves and hybrids alike, each judged by the rite's doctrine for its own kind.

Your rite holds your kindWhat the act does
VirtuousRaises fulfillment: 3 for a feed, 5 for a kill, 10 for a turning. Known or not.
CriminalLowers it by the same amounts, but only once you are known for what you are. A creature behind a secret lives as a member of its faith in good standing and is not charged.
Shunned or AcceptedNothing.

How hard it lands depends on how much you care. Vanilla's own multipliers apply, so a zealous character feels a loss twice over, and a cynic is never charged at all. A known creature whose rite outlaws its kind also cares less about that rite: −25 per cent on every fulfillment gain and loss, −10 per cent if the rite only shuns it. A toast tells you why each time it happens.

Hunters are weighed the other way round. Putting a creature down is righteous where its kind is outlawed and sacrilege where it is revered, 3 either way, whether or not the creature was known: a slaying is the creature unmasked. And since 1.20, choices in the mod's events count through your personality as vanilla's do, so an option that goes against a virtue of your faith costs a little fulfillment, creature or not.

ProgressionLifestyles and perk trees#

Six lifestyles, ten trees, and they are not priced the same. What a lifestyle costs per level, and how many trees it has to spend those levels on, is most of what separates the creatures in a long game.

LifestylePer levelBase gainTreesEnds at
Vampire1,50030Vampirism, 10 perksTrue Vampire
Werewolf1,10027Lycanthropy, 8 perksTrue Werewolf
Hunter1,40025Monster Hunter and Witch-Hunter, 8 eachThe Witcher / Witch-Hunter
Hybrid1,50030Vampirism and Lycanthropy, 9 eachBoth capstones, then the master focus
Magic1,00025Witchery 8, Arcane 7, Black Magic 6One of True Witch, Mage or Warlock
Demonic1,00025Demon, 17 perksGreater Demon and Crossroad Dealmaker

Magic and Demonic are the cheapest per level, at 1,000. The werewolf is the cheapest of the three blood lifestyles and has the least to buy. The vampire and the hybrid cost the most per level, and the hybrid has eighteen perks to get through (The hybrid lifestyle). The demon's tree is the largest single tree in the mod, and taking both its capstones closes the lifestyle behind you (The Demonic lifestyle).

The vampire, werewolf and hybrid lifestyles each have a master focus that unlocks only when you hold the capstone: Ancient Vampirism, Indomitable Lycanthropy and The Immortal Hybrid. It grants a broad spread of attributes and no further lifestyle experience.

Two problems in this area were fixed and are worth knowing about because they change how the AI behaves. Before 2.32 only the root perk of each tree carried a weight, so an AI entered a tree and stopped; every perk now carries one and the AI climbs. And a hunter holding a magic artifact used to be outbid into a magic focus, because the hunter focuses sat at a flat weight while the magic ones stacked past it. The creature focuses now all multiply themselves down for a hunter rather than zeroing, so a hunter can end up there, it simply stops being his first pick.

Every perk, tree by tree#

All ninety perks and sixteen focuses, read from the files. "After" is the perk you need first; a perk with two parents needs both. A capstone is marked with its icon. Where a perk's tooltip promises something the mod never does, or does something the tooltip does not mention, it says so here.

WarRegiments#

Ten men-at-arms regiments: five from the Supernatural layer, five from Witchcraft. Artifacts and relics, which used to sit here, are now under Magic: Magical artifacts. They are bought in three currencies, gold, prestige and piety, and only one of them is free to keep.

RegimentWho can raise itCostDamage / toughnessStack
WolvesAny werewolf or hybrid65 prestige100 / 7510
WerewolvesLycanthropy 50, or a hide's Champion100 prestige215 / 1855
BatsAny vampire or hybridGold5 / 5250
VampiresVampirism 50100 piety185 / 17510
Hunter TraineesHunter + Hunter Academy60 gold14 / 1850, ten sub-regiments
CultistsA coven culture, or a camp that unlocks them50 gold15 / 14100
Skeleton WarriorsA necromancer200 prestige20 / 14100
Demonic KnightsThe demon tree's Infernal Guardians1,000 prestige300 / 80100
Battle GolemsA finished golem project1,000 gold250 / 100100
Hut On The Chicken's Leg (Baba Yaga's hut)Yaga, with the house1,000 gold1,000 / 10010, exactly one ever

Vampires are the only thing in the game maintained in piety as well as gold; wolves and werewolves are maintained in prestige. Skeletons cost nothing to keep at either maintenance level, so a necromancer fields a thousand free skeletons for prestige alone. Demonic Knights, Battle Golems and the hut cost nothing while they are not raised.

The creature regiments cannot be borrowed through a vassal, because every one of them refuses to be raised through a subject.

Two counters in the files do not work, and the files say so. Bats claim a bonus against werewolves and werewolves claim one against vampires, but a counter key has to name a regiment type, and both of those are keys whose type is Monster. They are inert. Hunter Trainees' counters are written correctly and do work.

Rules, decisions, compatibility, languages, troubleshooting and history.

SetupGame rules#

There are 27 rules across 98 settings, in two tabs: sixteen under Supernatural and eleven under Witchcraft. None of the Supernatural settings blocks achievements; seven Witchcraft ones do.

These are the sixteen Supernatural rules. In game each name starts with "Supernatural:", as in Supernatural: Embrace Decisions.

RuleDefaultWhy you might change it
Embrace DecisionsOffTurn it on and you get the ten decisions that let you become a vampire, werewolf, hybrid, hunter, witch or Mage at will, and stop being one of the first four. With it off there is no cure for anything.
Trait DistributionCounts and belowSets the highest rank of AI adult that can be rolled into a vampire, werewolf or hunter. The roll runs at game start and again every year. On allows every rank; Off stops the rolls, which leaves the eight Mikaelsons, six of them creatures.
Confer DeathOnSeven settings from Off through NPC Only to More Death. It governs the two hybrid turnings only. It does not reach the vampire embrace, the werewolf wound or hunter training.
AI ConferOnWhether AI creatures turn and train other people, and which kinds are allowed to. Set it to Off for a world where you are the only one spreading anything.
Inheritance/PregnancyWerewolf Trait OnlyEight settings covering who can have children with whom and what comes out. One setting is labelled Skip this Setting and means it.
Demon Inheritance/PregnancyOnSeparate from the above, with four settings. Off is the original behaviour, where demon pregnancies were always lost.
Mortals Becoming Demons (2.39)Default (Everyone)Players Only: no AI character ever becomes a demon. Demon-blooded AI stay demon-blooded, and demons summoned by magic still appear. Becoming a demon.
EventsOnFour settings. You can have embrace events only, everything except embrace events, or none.
PalenessVampiresWhether turning lightens your skin, and whether that applies to hybrids too. How a vampire looks.
AgeingStops at portrait maturityHow an immortal's portrait behaves over centuries.
Visual ChangesOffTurns on five cosmetic right-click options. Off by default, so most players never see them.
Reset Lifetime EventsEvery 50 yearsLets once-per-lifetime event chains come round again for immortals, who otherwise exhaust them. Since 2.39 it also resets rite edits and founding a faith.
The Six HidesScatteredOff, Lost, Scattered or Awakened: whether the hides exist at all, and whether they start in shrines, on werewolves or lost. Detail.
PacksOnOff removes packs and everything between them. The Werewolf Gathering still runs, and wolves can still Challenge each other.
The Blue RoseOnOff means no temple of the Order is ever founded, and the Gnostic Council works as Witchcraft ships it. Relics, covens, Zecchia and the Seal of Solomon remain. Detail.
The Vade Maecum InfernalScatteredOff, Scattered or Lost: whether the book's pages exist, and whether three start in treasuries or lost. Under Scattered and Lost the fourth is in Silverback's hide. Detail.
