ButtonsDecisions and interactions#

A hundred and fifteen decisions, five of them golem orders given from the Puppets list, and 110 character interactions. Three of the interactions replace base-game ones, and since 2.38 so does the coven-founding decision. Almost none of it costs money. The currency in this mod is cooldowns, risk and exposure.

Embrace, abandon and feeding#

Since 2.40 Supernatural's own decisions, 63 in all with the hide, pack, Order, coven, relic and book ones, sit in five groups of their own in the decisions panel, and each group shows only to a character with something in it:

GroupWhat is in it
The Order of the Blue RoseThe Order's decisions, from Seek Out the Order and Change Your Temple to Respondeo, the Fors Factorum, Invocare and a Conclave (below)
Wolves and the HidesBecoming a werewolf and its cure, Satiate Hunger, the pack, and every Champion's decision, Cover Your Trail and Listen Again among them (below)
Vampires and HybridsBecoming a vampire or a hybrid and the ways out, Satiate Thirst and Satiate Hunger/Thirst
HuntersBecome a Hunter and Abandon Being a Hunter, and Witchcraft's Become a Fallen Witch Hunter and Begin Witch Hunt, which now sit here
Magic and RelicsEmbrace Witchcraft and Embrace the Mage, the coven, Make a Golem, the relics, Ward the Chains, Zecchia and the Vade Maecum

Two more sit under the base game's Major decisions: Break the Order of the Blue Rose, and the mod's easier Found Witch Coven, which takes the base game's place. The orders you give a puppet, a golem's six, a sire-bound lover's two and a Compelled's two, are on its puppet card instead (What any puppet can do). Ten of the decisions are the embrace decisions: a way in for each of six kinds and a way out for four, all behind the Supernatural: Embrace Decisions rule set to On. Every way in needs an adult, and the AI can never take any of them.

How far in. The ways in for a vampire, werewolf, hybrid and hunter then ask how far you have come. What the Blood Makes of You offers As a fledgling., Halfway into it. or All the way.: the creature's track at 0, 50 or 100 (a hybrid's two tracks both). What the Training Made of You offers 50, 75 or 100 on the Monster-Hunter track. Before 2.40 the level was added before the trait existed, so the choice was lost; the same fault reached the random embrace events, a werewolf child's head start at birth and the yearly trait rolls, and since 2.40 all of them give their level. Characters made under the old fault are not topped up. While a picker is open its decision is hidden, so it cannot be taken twice, and a golem cannot take any of them (Something already in your blood will not let it in.).

KindThe way inThe way out
VampireEmbrace VampirismAbandon Vampirism
WerewolfEmbrace LycanthropyAbandon Lycanthropy
HybridBecome a HybridAbandon Being a Hybrid
HunterBecome a HunterAbandon Being a Hunter
Witch (2.39)Embrace WitchcraftNone
Mage (2.39)Embrace the MageNone

The other three are the feeding decisions:

DecisionWhoYour cooldownWhat it does
Satiate ThirstVampire7 yearsThree days later, the feeding chain
Satiate HungerWerewolf5 yearsThree days later, the hunger chain
Satiate Hunger/ThirstHybrid, while either track is under 1007 yearsA coin flip between the two, or the chain whose track is not yet full

None of them costs anything, and since 2.39 a child cannot take them. An AI creature runs on its own fourteen-year cadence rather than your cooldown.

These were broken in instructive ways. The vampire decision was entirely commented out before 2.32, so pure vampires had no feeding decision at all, and the familiar coin-flip belonged to the hybrid. All three then had their AI check interval set to zero, which means the AI never looks at a decision: twelve years of a fresh campaign with about ninety-six vampires in it produced zero feeds and zero trails.

Hides and packs#

Thirteen decisions and eight interactions belong to the hides and the packs. The detail is in The Six Hides and Packs.

The detail is in The Order of the Blue Rose, Covens, Relics, Zecchia and The Vade Maecum Infernal.

Interactions#

The Supernatural layer's own interactions are:

  • Eleven for turning and training. Vampire: Offer Blood, Turn Vampire, Ask to be Turned, Ask to Not be Turned. Werewolf: Offer Gift, Force Gift, Ask for the Gift. Hybrid: Make Hybrid, Ask to be Turned. Hunter: Train as a Hunter, Ask to be trained as a Hunter. Only the werewolf has a forced version. Since 2.39 the five that turn someone are never shown on a child, and a child cannot use them or ask to be turned; hunter training keeps its floor of 13.
  • Three for vampires and hybrids: Invoke the Bond; Compel Mortal, which gained Release Me in 2.39 and since 2.40 makes a player's victim his Compelled; and, since 2.40, Sense the Sire Bond.
  • Shapeshift, for werewolves and hybrids.
  • Five cosmetic right-click options, hidden unless you turn the Visual Changes rule on.
  • Eight for hides and packs, Offer Pack Terms among them.
  • Twenty-four for the Order, relics, golems and the book (eighteen from 2.38, Invite into the Order, and since 2.40 Take the Beetle into Custody, Make Your Golem a Puppet, Bind the Masterless Golem, Ask to Join Their Temple and Ask the Grand Magus to Fund Your Research).
  • The puppet deeds (2.40), used by acting as a puppet: the nine any puppet can do, Stand Guard Over Them for a golem, four favours through the Sire Bond, and seven commands for a Compelled.
  • Four for the 2.39 items: Swallow the Beetle, Slip the Beetle and Feed It to the Prisoner, and Commission Warded Chains.

The Witchcraft layer adds fifty-four more, and since 2.38, 27 of them, the friendly and diplomatic ones, sit in the Supernatural menu. Most are spells; the rest include the witch trial, the Gnostic Council appointments, Join the Mage Summit, Drink the Potion and the cyrograph proposal. All fifty-four, with who can use each and its cooldown.

Until 2.37 the mod also shipped a cross-species duel interaction as 462 lines of comment that nothing used. That file is gone; between wolves, Challenge is the duel now.

As the mod standsKnown issues#

Things in 2.40 that do not work the way the game's own text suggests. Each is described where it belongs; this is the list.

  • The Beastbane cannot be earned. A hunter records only his first kill, so his name stops at the first rung. The same limit caps the reputation term on the tracking bar at +3. Names the world uses.
  • "The Timeless" is a nickname with no text, and nothing grants it.
  • Two regiment counters are inert. Bats against werewolves and Werewolves against vampires do nothing. Regiments.
  • Confer Death does not cover the werewolf wound, the vampire embrace or hunter training, although its description says "turned, or trained". The bite.
  • There are no fangs, and the "Supernatural: Visual Fangs" rule that the text files describe does not exist. How a vampire looks.
  • The in-game notes for 2.35 mention dogs and "Hunter Academies". There is no dog kit, and the perk is Hunter Academy. 2.35.
  • The new text of 2.39 and 2.40 is in English for Russian players until it is translated. Languages.
  • The AI never shapeshifts. Shifting.
  • The Blue Rose rule promises six temples at the start. Temples are seated around AI Mages, and Witchcraft makes none at the start of a game, so they usually appear at most one a year. Temples.
  • Respondeo's five-year wait starts even if you choose Not now. Respondeo.
  • A coven whose panel ends for lack of witches keeps its house modifier, and its panel never comes back. Covens.
  • The Echo Pendant does not send a curse back, as its text says. It blocks ordinary curses and nothing more. Relics.
  • A page a Champion chose to guard can still be found by the Necrophone, a golem, Magic Triangulation's answer and the book's voices. Finding the pages.
  • With The Six Hides rule off, the Vade Maecum can never be bound, because its fourth page lives in Silverback's hide. The pages.
  • A man Bound to the Book may be out of a hunter's reach. As the files read, a hunt corners only vampires, werewolves, hybrids and demons, so a bound master who is only a Mage may leave the Unmaking as the only designed way to kill him, short of taking him prisoner and executing him. The Bound to the Book text also says a Champion can kill him; nothing gives a Champion a way to. This has not been confirmed in game. Bound to the Book.
  • A wolf already in a wolf form when his gaoler gets the Warded Chains keeps that form. He still cannot change shape or use Break the Chains.
  • The pack invitation's requirement still says you have not invited this wolf in the last two years. The wait now starts only when a wolf refuses. Founding, joining and leaving.
  • The Invocare coffers say they buy "the three masks least sure of their vote". Only AI voters can be bought. Invocare.
  • Zecchia asks for "a hundred gold for each thing now", but takes her whole fee at delivery. Zecchia.

From the Witchcraft layer, which Supernatural carries as its author wrote it:

  • Magical Experiments removes the Mage trait from anyone who already had it from an event. The magic trees.
  • A False Mage can still take Warlock or True Witch. The one-capstone rule looks for a true Mage, so a pretender is not stopped. The magic trees.
  • Lifestealer gives a True Witch nothing but its tooltip. Black Magic perks.
  • The Magic Triangulation perk does not unlock triangulation. The decision needs the Mage or Warlock perk and a tower. Arcane perks.
  • Cheat Death is out of reach for a Necromancer, though its requirement names one: the project is only offered to characters who are not. Projects.
  • The Mage's Spire does not count as a tower for Magic Triangulation, where the Study and Laboratory below it do. Magic buildings.
  • The Warlock trait's own Demonology and Power Saturation tracks never fill. The trait shows rewards on them, but nothing adds experience to either.
  • The Mage Summit's Extraordinary security costs 300 gold and does nothing. Mage Summit.
  • The expedition planner is looser than the rule. Once you know one destination, it can offer any place of power, including one you have already visited. Expeditions.

Other modsCompatibility and what it overrides#

Supernatural runs on the base game and needs nothing else. The compatibility questions that matter come from what it replaces. The mods you must not run beside it are listed under Install and load order, with the load order.

What it replaces#

CK3 resolves mod files by path, last loaded wins. A mod file sitting at the same path as a base-game file replaces that file entirely. Anything the vanilla copy defined and the replacement does not is simply gone. Most of Supernatural is new filenames, which collide with nothing. The exceptions are the places it deliberately steps on vanilla: the birth on-actions, the death on-actions, by key, the base game's Found Witch Coven decision, since 2.39 the base game's holdings file (copied word for word from 1.20.1.1, with the Collegium added to the castle, city and temple building lists), several character interactions including disinheritance, some scripted relations, travel options, a handful of secret effects, and a set of portrait and accessory gene files. The mod ships its own list of these.

Two mods that both replace the same vanilla file are mutually exclusive in practice. That, rather than anything specific to witches, is why Witchcraft and V&L cannot sit beside this one.

Mid-save#

Adding it to a running campaign works. 2.36, the move to CK3 1.20, asked for a new game; the other releases have been safe to add to a save, and 2.39 is built for it, though its tests were run on games started with it. 2.40 was built to be carried into a running save, with one-time repairs: every Reliquary list is rebuilt and empty Reliquaries get their first relics at the first new year; anyone who already knew a page's holder gets the claim; a golem left without a master is offered once to the player whose court, guests, direct vassals or house hold it, and comes home from its dead master's search; a player whose turned lover loves him is offered the Sire Bond; a Fors Factorum cast earlier pays its price once under the new rules; a hide stuck on a wolf it refused moves on at the next yearly check; and the Supernatural Trails window lists the trails already open the first time it is opened. A repair that waits for a new game to leave the lobby also runs in a player's first three months on a loaded save. It is built for it in places: existing saves get a one-time repair pass for secrets nobody was told about, creatures that predate the disease-immunity flag are retro-fitted on a yearly pulse, characters stuck mid-transition are released, and the hunt's availability check is written to fail open on a continued save rather than grey the activity out forever.

Two things do not carry over. Game rules are chosen when a save is created, so a campaign started without Supernatural has no values for its twenty-seven rules. The ones added in 2.37 to 2.39 are read so that a save from before them gets their defaults: the hides scattered, packs on, the Order on, the Vade Maecum scattered and demons for everyone. On such a save the Order seats its temples, and the relics, the Seal of Solomon and the pages are placed within the first year; under 2.39 the Homunculus Beetle is placed and the standing AI temples get their Collegia in that first year too. And the mod adds portrait and accessory genes, which is the usual CK3 hazard around adding or removing a mod mid-save.

Removing it from a save is not supported and there is no uninstall path. Most of what strands is mod-created and disappears with the mod. The exception worth knowing: the mod sets its four creature doctrines on every rite in the save, at game start, whenever a rite is created and once a year, and those are vanilla rite objects that persist. Nothing takes them off again.

Multiplayer#

Every player needs the same mods at the same positions. The mod honours CK3's own multiplayer murder-scheme protections in its own scheme, and carries no other multiplayer-specific code.

TranslationLanguages#

Eight language folders ship. English is maintained, and two others carry real translations that are behind it.

LanguageState
EnglishComplete and maintained. 96 files.
SpanishReal translation, contributed by Luke0704, and behind the current English text. Since 2.36 everything newer is there in English, 2.40 included.
RussianReal translation by Adrian, shipped in the mod, covering everything up to 2.38. The new text of 2.39 (fourteen files) and 2.40 (nine files) is there in English until he translates it.
French, German, Korean, PolishEnglish text under a translated header, with every key present, 2.40 included.
Simplified ChineseThe same English text again, with every key present, 2.40 included.

A player in French, German, Korean, Polish or Chinese sees English, not missing keys. The Workshop page does not claim any language support, so it is not a broken promise. But if you play in French, German, Korean, Polish or Chinese, expect English.

When something looks wrongCommon questions and troubleshooting#

Short answers for when the game does something you did not expect. How each system works is in its own section; Quick answers covers the questions asked most.

The launcher does not show the mod after a manual install.

Check that both supernatural_mod.mod and the supernatural_mod folder are in the CK3 mod folder, and that the .mod file's path= line is not pointing at a folder on another machine. Install and load order has the detail.

Where are the decisions to become a vampire or a werewolf?

Behind a game rule that is off by default. Supernatural: Embrace Decisions ships as "Default (Off)". It gates ten decisions: six to become a vampire, werewolf, hybrid, hunter, witch or Mage, and four to stop being one of the first four, three of which are the cures. Turn it on at game setup if you want to pick what you are. The ten decisions by name.

Why is there no temple of the Order in my game?

The Order seats its temples around AI Mages, and Witchcraft makes none at the start of a game. Until a Mage appears, there is nobody to seat. Once there is, the Order founds at most one temple a year until six stand, so on a new campaign expect them over the first years. With no temple anywhere, a landed Mage who knows two others can use Found the Order Anew. Check also that Supernatural: The Blue Rose is on. Temples.

Why can I not start Magical Research?

It needs a true Mage, meaning the trait without the False Mage secret, an adult, holding at least a county, off its ten-year cooldown (five as the Gnostic Council's Keeper of the Reliquary). A Research Expedition also needs a place of power you know about and have not visited. Magical Research.

The Arcane focus is greyed out. What am I missing?

One key on top of being a witch: a magic artifact (the Enchanted Ring and an awakened Singularity count), the Source, the Magical Experiments perk, a place of power held while landed, a Gnostic Council grant, Research Cooperation from a summit, a house with the academy, an event that opens it, or, as a True Witch, an academy standing anywhere in the world. The magic trees.

Do vampires have fangs?

No. The mod has no fangs portrait and no fangs game rule. If you have a fangs option in your barbershop, it belongs to another mod. How a vampire looks.

Why will a hide not take me?

Because of your traits. It Will Not Have You says which, and that hide will not try you again for five years. What each hide looks for.

I have played for decades and never seen a hunt. Why?

Two things, and 2.35 fixed the larger one. Before it, the AI checked the feeding and hunting decisions on an interval of zero, which in CK3 means never, so almost nothing wrote trails and there was nothing to hunt. The second is structural and still true: a hunt needs a warm trail, meaning the culprit is still alive. Since 2.35 a dead culprit's trail is handed to another creature in the county where there is one, instead of sitting there unhuntable.

Why did a witch from my dynasty disappear?

A bug in 2.35, fixed in 2.35a. Witch Spotted looks for a real witch before it makes one up, and she can be anyone in reach: the culprit of a witch trail, someone at the ruler's own court or the local lord's, the lord himself, someone at the liege's court, or a witch whose secret the ruler knows. The two options that turn the hunt down still carried a clean-up meant only for a made-up woman, so they killed the real one. That is why witches married off into other courts were vanishing. Since 2.35a, turning the hunt down leaves her alone. Anyone lost before the fix stays lost.

Ask to Borrow never works, or its button stays greyed.

Before 2.40 the borrowing window opened on the first item the lender owned, often a hide or the Beetle, which could not be picked, so the button stayed blocked as soon as members held more than one item. Since 2.40 the window lists only what can be lent to you, and when nothing can, the requirement says why. An item must be pledged to your own temple's Reliquary, not on loan and not lent in the last three years; the Powder and the Fors Factorum are never lent, the Beetle only to a Magistratus or higher, and you must be a Medicum or higher. Lending.

Why is my temple's Reliquary empty?

Before 2.40 the relics were handed out once, when the Order first seated its temples, and on most games no temple stood yet, so only a handful of relics existed in the whole world; a pledged item also fell out of the Reliquary whenever it changed hands. Since 2.40 each temple with an empty Reliquary gets its first relics on a 1 January once six temples stand or ten years have passed, a running game included, and pledges survive an heir of the same temple. Where relics come from.

I chose "All the way" when I took the embrace decision, and my track is still at 0.

A bug fixed in 2.40: the level was added before the trait existed, so it was lost. The pickers now give the level you choose. A character made under the old bug is not topped up, because the game cannot tell what was picked. How far in.

I turned my lover, and there is no Sire Bond.

A vampire needs Vampire's Kiss to hold the Sire Bond; a hybrid needs nothing. The one you turned must love you (a lover or soulmate), be an adult, still be a vampire or hybrid, and not be played by a person. The bond then forms by itself within days, and on a save from before 2.40 within your first three months. If you already act through another, or once let the bond go, use Sense the Sire Bond on them. The Sire Bond.

The Trails window says "Left by: unknown" for every trail.

That is how it works since 2.40. What left a trail, and when it was last seen, is read for you by a Trailsman in your court or camp. Without one you can read only your own trails and those left by a member of your pack, coven or temple whose nature you know. Supernatural Trails.

Compel Mortal no longer offers a list of commands.

Since 2.40 a player's compulsion, when it takes, makes the mortal your Compelled puppet for one command. Act as them, by right-clicking yourself or someone else and choosing them in the "interact as" row, or use End Your Life or Let Them Go on their puppet card. The Compelled.

Why is my world full of new hunters and werewolves?

Also 2.35, fixed in 2.35a. Two yearly events, Witch Spotted and Monster Sighting, were made far more frequent so hunters would hear of work, and the faster rate reached every ruler on the map, not only hunters. Burning the witch or killing the creature makes a hunter of anyone who was not one, and Monster Sighting can leave a new werewolf in the world, so the map filled up. Since 2.35a only hunters get the faster rate; everyone else is back to the rate from before 2.35. Hunters and werewolves already made stay in your save.

HistoryVersion history#

The mod shows its own changelog once per save, at game start or on the next 1 January after an update. This is what it says, condensed.

2.40: Fixes and balance#

Fixes and balance for the Order, the relics, the hides and the golems, built to carry into a running save, and three kinds of puppet to act through: the golem, the Sire Bond and the Compelled. Borrowing from the Reliquary works: the pick lists show only what you can choose and say why when there is nothing. The Reliquaries get their relics once the temples stand, and keep what is pledged to them. The embrace pickers give the level you choose. Golems can no longer be turned.

Vampires: the Sire Bond forms by itself when someone you turned loves you, and lasts until death, the end of the vampirism or your release; a hybrid needs no perk for it, a vampire needs Vampire's Kiss; Speak for Me, Draw Out Their Secrets, Cover My Tracks, Give Them Your Blood, Seduce Me and Release the Sire Bond, and nothing you ask wears on them. Compel Mortal makes a player's victim his Compelled for one command, costs 25 Vampirism (15 as a True Vampire) only when it takes, and fades after a month; afterwards the victim is Wary for three years. Nobody compels a child, and the AI's compelling vampires are mostly the callous, sadistic and arrogant.

The Order: ranks are numbered, and promotions need three years in a rank with only a few Magistrati to a temple; one A Name for the Rose a year; the masks can run your temple, and act for you when you are at war, in an activity or in prison; Hand On Your Temple; a landless Grand Magus can host a Conclave, and another temple can be asked to call one; Respondeo costs less, can be paid with a Powder or shared by two free, willing members of your temple, makes a county forget an exposed member, and can be sent to answer One of Us Is Known; every seat can Draw on the Council's Treasury, 100, 500 or 1,000 gold, and a member can Ask the Grand Magus to Fund Your Research; the Grand Magus decides whether to Reveal the Gnostic Council, and an AI Grand Magus meets the council every eight to ten years; teaching is paced, can go through Teach Sorcery, and a member can refuse acolytes; three new tutor moments and four new answers to The Tutor's Quarrel; the recruitment events name the town, the place and your tutor; Seek Out the Order keeps its mystery and always answers within about a year; asking the temple for help moves your research at once, once your own research has begun; Change Your Temple and Ask to Join Their Temple move a member to another temple at his rank; the humming relic in the Reliquary has outcomes; the AI no longer appoints a Court Mage, Dark Champion or cult priest at war with it.

Magic: Cast the Fors Factorum: two to five free years, then cancer once, and a cure ends it; a new relic, the Fors Factorum letter; the Elemental Transference cures nine more illnesses, asks members once, pays the families of the townsfolk who carry it, and has a tracker; the Homunculus Beetle can be lent to a Magistratus, and a Temple Magus can take it into custody; Prepare the Powder costs less, and a Powder you carry throws itself when you are marked where you stand; Metamagic research can lead to the Fors Factorum, and Other worlds or Necromancy to the Vade Maecum; "This is black magic".

Hides and packs: the hide duel; a hide leans toward the family of its past Champions and passes on their strength; every hide says what it does; Midnight is a noble fury; The Hide Speaks before a Challenge; The Hide Finds a Page, and every way of naming a page's holder gives a claim; the Ringing pays Hide Bond, can be silenced for a caster, and gives your hide as the reason for its options; Send for a Hide sends a named man with new roads; Offer Pack Terms; a feud only over a hide taken unkindly; Pack Standing grows more slowly past 50 and gives more at 75 and 100; Red Eyes in Your Pack; the Unification for a spouse already in your pack; Other Packs and The Rest of the Pack in the pack panel; Pack Members Clash waits for the alpha, can turn deadly between packs at odds, and backing your wolf sours the two houses; Timber's Vanish is now Cover Your Trail; Let It Take Me lasts until its battle or the war's end.

Golems: the golem puppet, its card orders and Guard Me; the deeds any puppet can do, which do not always reveal who sent it; a golem that holds land acts as a ruler; Last Instructions when the master dies, and Bind the Masterless Golem for a golem nobody holds; no golem carries Soul Forfeit; Make a Golem asks for a shape, then a name, an improvement and a calibration; The Golem Came Back Wrong leaves it Cracked for two years.

The hunt: Supernatural Trails, a window of every open trail, with what left it read for you by a Trailsman; every thin Witch Hunt opens with A List of Names; hunt events show in the hunt's window and count the hunters; Riding Home for guests; a cornered player creature gets a week's grace. Draining a victim completely now relieves stress.

Fixes: the level pickers; borrowing; empty Reliquaries; Respondeo's payers; An Entrance no longer shows the entrant twice; The First Lesson shows your acolyte; inviting an existing member reveals you to each other; a refused hide no longer goes on and off an AI wolf; The Wolves Have Teeth never tells a killer about himself; an alpha is not offered Challenge the Alpha; battle lines that name you read properly; a hybrid can Satiate until both tracks are full; Word at the Fire never names your own hide; the Supernatural buttons close up with no gaps, the Gnostic Council's button is no longer hidden behind the Order's, and the pack, Order and coven buttons open their own story; Supernatural's decisions are in five groups (Decisions); the missing-texture error on every startup is gone; the yearly upkeep skips everyone who is not a creature, hunter or demon.

2.39a — Hotfix#

Released 17 October 2026, the same day as 2.39. Mage, Warlock and True Witch are one path each: finishing one closes the last perk of the other two trees. The warning 2.39 added sat on the capstones themselves, where hovering shows only the trait, so nobody saw it. Now the perk before each capstone carries the warning, and a tree whose last perk is already closed to you says so on its first perk (the magic trees). Safe for running saves.

2.39 — The Hermetic Order of the Blue Rose, Part 2#

Released 17 October 2026. The Order gets its politics: the temples can vote out the Grand Magus with Invocare, the Gnostic Council can grant him Imperium, and some Grand Magi stage an emergency of their own; the council's first great work, the Great Respondeo, makes a realm forget. Outsiders who learn of the Order can break it, or ask the Adeptus Major to let them in, and a Temple Magus can invite someone himself. The Fors Factorum is for the Grand Magus, the Order's master, the Temple Magi and a Magistratus who masters metamagic through his own research. Temples can meet beneath a Collegium, rare by design. The chapter gets romance, which buys standing and never rank, and new moments for neophytes, Temple Magi and packs, most of them at least 60 days apart. A werewolf the Order wiped may remember again. Hover the Order's Latin to read what it means.

New: spells can go wrong for a Vade Maecum witness; the Homunculus Beetle, a relic that starts a plague; the Warded Chains; Break the Chains for Champions and Release Me for jailed vampires; the Draught of a Clear Mind; A Tale on the Road, The Hide Remembers and The Pack Comes Home; the Embrace Witchcraft and Embrace the Mage decisions; and the optional rule Supernatural: Mortals Becoming Demons.

AI and balance: vampires can no longer compel werewolves; Witchcraft's AI needs a reason to duel you, take your magic items, dominate or demand submission, turn a demon against you, consume a child or send inquisitors to war, and a refused duel is remembered for five years; going after a magic item weighs grudges, greed, nature, kinship and friendship, and Mages of the Order mostly leave each other alone; a Mage can hire Zecchia for an item he wants; students turn rival less often; children can no longer be turned or use creature powers.

Fixes: pack invitations; a stuck Gnostic Council meeting, and no one holds two seats; Reset Lifetime Events resets rite edits; a vampire's spouse can ask to be turned; the Mage warns that it closes the other two paths; witch hunters can no longer join the Order; a real hunter no longer vanishes in Time of Need; AI hunters burn only witches they hold; a hybrid no longer dies from an old request to be turned; a neophyte in prison keeps his place; wolves under 18 no longer become lovers; A Hundred Years no longer comes twice; Respondeo no longer spreads a witch's secret; the Conclave lists each candidate once; outsiders no longer see the Gnostic Council's actions; The Tutor's Quarrel shows your acolyte; hide events on a hunt show in the hunt's window; dozens of events check they still make sense when they arrive. Russian translation by Adrian.

2.38 — The Hermetic Order of the Blue Rose#

The Hermetic Order of the Blue Rose comes to Supernatural: temples of Mages that send a note and a blue rose to the people they want, and teach magic to the ones who pass. Members rise from Acolyte to Temple Magus, keep their temple's way of Silence, Subservience or Observance, work toward the Order's Prerogative, and meet in masks at the Conclave, which elects the Grand Magus of the Gnostic Council. Seven lesser relics and the Seal of Solomon join the game; most can be pledged to a temple's or coven's reliquary and lent out, and Seize Their Relics is a war for a rival's hoard. The Vade Maecum Infernal is four scattered pages, one inside Silverback's hide. Zecchia, a demon thief, answers some summonings. Covens have their own panel, can be founded with three witches, and can bring their Mages into the Order. Friendly spells and the council's interactions now sit in the Supernatural menu. Two new game rules turn the Order and the Vade Maecum on or off.

Balance: one capstone each, Mage, Warlock or True Witch; the AI's nature weighs on its magic path and on necromancy; Witchcraft's magical artifacts can reach courtiers and landless adventurers; the werewolf and hybrid traits give less Martial, and the werewolf's ranks are Fledgling Werewolf, Werewolf, Seasoned Werewolf, Dread Werewolf and Elder Werewolf; hide Champions who practise magic regain power every year; packs invite lone wolves they know, and a second wolf at court lowers the bar for founding a pack to Pack Standing 25; True Witches appear again beside the Order's Mages.

Fixes: healing no longer takes away the Horned God's Claws; renouncing necromancy takes away the right to raise Skeleton Warriors; Demand Submission converts its target properly; Shadow Step out of prison runs the base game's escape; a soul-sold character's death pays the cyrograph's owner; creature lifestyle experience shows in tooltips; thousands of script errors are gone.

2.37 — The Six Hides#

Six ancient wolf hides are loose in the world: Silverback, Timber, Tundra, Graybeard, Midnight and Alpha. Each chooses a werewolf or hybrid who fits it, and its Champion is stronger, lives longer, never grows frail and hears magic. Most start as relics in shrines at holy sites; take one by holding the county, by siege or on pilgrimage, or pay someone to fetch it with Send for a Hide. Wolves can found and join packs, rise in Pack Standing, Challenge the alpha, and meet at the new Werewolf Gathering. AI wolves challenge an alpha only for a reason: he is too weak to lead, cruel to his pack, or their enemy. A hide an AI leaves idle for ten years slips away to find a wolf. Two new game rules control the hides and the packs. It works on a running save: the hides turn up at the next new year.

Fixes: the mod's death reasons read as proper sentences; the death of a vampire who refused to feed no longer shows an error; They Stopped Coming has its icon; a broken link in Invoke the Bond is fixed; he, she and his are capitalised at the start of a sentence; a demon can have a child with a werewolf or hybrid.

2.36 — Crusader Kings III 1.20#

Updated for CK3 1.20, which rebuilt religion around rites. On 1.20, 2.35a crashed as a new game loaded in; 2.36 fixes that. The vampirism, lycanthropy, hybrid and hunter doctrines belong to rites now, so every rite of a faith has them and new rites get them too, and crimes, shunning and the witch war follow the rite. Known vampires, werewolves, hybrids and hunters are sins under a rite that outlaws their kind and virtues under one that reveres it, the way 1.20 treats witches. Your own rite judges your feeding, killing and turning others through Spiritual Fulfillment, but only condemns them once you are known; the zealous feel it more and a cynic is never charged. A feed only leaves a trail when somebody talks. The Seekers camp purpose works again, and Scholar is Erudite in 1.20 and keeps the magic bonus.

Fixes: the first warning that a county remembers you no longer mentions a body when there wasn't one; feeding events no longer flood the error log; the Mikaelsons no longer log hundreds of portrait errors at load; French, German, Spanish, Korean, Polish and Chinese players see English rather than raw keys for everything since the hunt. If you play with A Game of Thrones, AGOT has not been updated for 1.20 yet, and the compatibility patch follows AGOT's update.

2.35a — The witch you let live#

Fixes for The Hunt, safe on a running save. Turning down a witch hunt no longer kills the witch, so witches married into other courts stop vanishing. The witch and monster sightings are fast for hunters only and back to their old rate for everyone else, which stops the world filling with new hunters and werewolves. Monster Sighting now has the same conditions as its twin, so it no longer reaches someone imprisoned, incapable, travelling or busy with an activity. An ordinary murder no longer reads as a creature's kill unless the killer was there; a murder in your own hall still can. Characters already in House Hellion stay there.

2.35 — The Hunt#

A new activity: ride out and hunt what is killing people. Kills and prowling leave trails on the counties they happen in, and trails escalate: three incidents and the lord hears about it. He can look into it himself, set his spymaster on it, post a bounty, or send for a named hunter and pay him when it is done. A new court position, the Trailsman, reads the countryside. You can hunt a creature or hunt a witch, each with their own trails and beats. Bring silver, stakes, dogs or men, and the door goes differently. Take an apprentice along and he graduates in the field.

These notes follow the mod's in-game text, which has two slips: there is no dog kit, and the perk is Hunter Academy. Of the creature's answers below, "compel him" is Look them in the eye.

The creature gets the other half of the scene, and real answers: stand and fight, go to ground, compel him, send your progeny, let the wolf out, or talk him onto somebody worse. Get away and you can warn your sire and your get, or sell them. Demons argue instead of running, and may offer a bargain. Hunters and creatures earn names the world starts using. A county that turns three hunters away stops attracting any. You can cover your own kills: pay it off, frame someone, or hide the body. Compel a whole town and the holes start to line up. Hunters ride at bad odds far less often, and can turn back.

Fixes: witch hunts were impossible to reach and now work; two files never loaded, so no trail was ever written; the hunter migration layer never ran; hunters are told about witches and monsters far more often; secret witches leave trails, not only openly known ones; compelling someone for their secrets no longer errors; thousands of script errors a session removed; hunter regiments now need the Hunter Academies perk.

ThanksCredits#

The magic half of this mod is Rycie's Witchcraft, carried forward here with their updates merged in as they ship. Nope's V&L is the same lineage. Neither belongs in a playset with this one. Thanks also to the Princes of Darkness team, BDW and Sparc especially, and to Luke0704 for the Spanish translation, and to the people on YouTube and in the Discord who reported, argued and tested.

The version notes thank twelve people by name for bug reports: CleverPolarBear, Lord Zymeth, Noa3, ТоЬЬыэЯ, Omni, Angel DeStaar, Explode_Toad, manic_Mage, AZTRAGOS, valdijan72, Dr. Baphomet and Plato in the Cave.
